PrismHR Help — Client Administration

Client / Employer Administration

This file is a combined export of PrismHR help topics in Client / Employer Administration. Source help system: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Default.htm Each topic is separated by a heading and includes its original URL.

========================================================================

Cloning a Client

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/2020/Clone_Client.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/2020/Clone_Client.htm

Cloning a Client

You can quickly create a new client using attributes from an existing client. To copy information from an existing client, access the Clone Client form on the Client|Action menu. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

When you open this form while accessing an existing client, the system enables that client’s attributes in the What to Clone panel.

To clone a client, do the following:

  1. Go to the Clone Client form and complete the following fields in the New Client Details panel:

Field — Description

New Client ID — Use the system-generated client ID that displays or enter the ID for the new client. If you enter an existing client ID in this field and press Tab a message displays, “Client ID already exists.” Click OK to clear the message and then re-enter another ID. Note: The system uses “conditional incrementing” on the New Client ID when you use the system-generated ID, which takes affect once you click Clone and the process succeeds.

New Client Name — Enter the name of the client you are creating.

Client Status — Select the current status of the client.

Status Date — Enter or select the date when you want to enable this client.

  1. Deselect or select the attributes for this client in the What to Clone panel.

Note: Note the following:

  • Enable the Federal Entity ID field to clone the client’s original federal entity ID.

  • Enable the Retirement Plan Setup field to include the retirement plan Benefit Plan Setup information and Company Match Rules.

  • If you enable the Company Benefits or Benefit Rules field and disable the Retirement Plan Setup field then during the client clone process, the system will not clone the following client configuration records:

  • The records available when you select Plan Class as Retirement or Employer Match on the Benefit Plan Setup form.

  • All the records available on the Retirement Match Rules form.

  1. Click Clone. The Client Details form displays, where you can continue providing information about this client.

  2. Click Save.

Approval Policy Setup

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approval_Policy_Setup.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approval_Policy_Setup.htm

Approval Policy Setup

There are many places in PrismHR where actions require approvals before they are finalized.

You need to set up approval policies as follows:

  • Ensure each approval policy specifies who must approve an action before it is finalized

  • Override global approval policies at the client level, and maintain policies for various approval types

  • Create custom HR approval roles for an Account Management (Service Providers) role or an HR Role (Worksite Managers/Trusted Advisors)

  • Establish the client’s payroll processing options

========================================================================

Using the Approval Process

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approval_Processing.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approval_Processing.htm

Using the Approval Process

When events take place that could potentially require an approval, such as a new hire or a payroll, PrismHR determines if there is a policy in place for the particular approval type.

  • If there is no policy definition, then the system immediately updates the database with the new hire or other information.

  • If there is an approval policy, then the system generates an approval event. If the first step for the approval is assigned to a particular user, then an e-mail is sent to that user. In addition, when the user logs in, the dashboard alerts the user of any pending approvals.

========================================================================

Reviewing Form I-9 Approvals

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Form-I-9-Section-2.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Form-I-9-Section-2.htm

Reviewing Form I-9 Approvals

Once an employee has submitted Form I-9 Section 1, users who have been set up as an I-9 Approver can complete Section 2. For more information about the employee process for completing Section 1, see See Employee Process for Completing Form I-9 Section 1.

To complete Section 2 of Form I-9:

  1. Click the Client menu.
  2. From Client|Action, click Approvals Pending. The Approvals Pending form displays.
  3. In the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel, click I-9 Section 2 for an employee. The I9 Section 2 form displays. > Note: Note: If multiple users have been set up as an I-9 Approver, pending approvals display in the Approvals Pending (Available to You) panel instead. Click I-9 Section 2 for an employee in this panel to display the Approval form, then click Grab to move the approval into the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel. Once the approval displays in the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel, click I-9 Section 2 for an employee in this panel to display the Review I-9 Section 1 step of the I9 Section 2 form. [Image: screenshot]
  4. Review the employee’s entries on Form I-9 Section 1. If changes are necessary, click Deny to return Section 1 to the employee for corrections. Otherwise, click Next. The Employment Eligibility Verification step displays, where supporting document information can be entered. Alternatively, if the employee uploaded supporting documents while completing Section 1, the documents display and can be reviewed. [Image: screenshot]
  5. Enter document information in the corresponding fields as necessary. When information for all supporting documents has been entered, click Next. The Additional Information step displays. [Image: screenshot]
  6. Enter any additional information about the form as necessary, then click Next. If remote verification is enabled for the user’s worksite location, the Remote Verification & Certification step displays. If remote verification is not enabled, the Certification step displays. The fields that display in these two steps are identical; however, the Remote Verification & Certification step includes additional options for indicating whether the user examined the employee’s supporting documents physically or remotely using an alternative procedure authorized by the Department of Homeland Security (DHS). For more information about remote verification,See Maintaining Worksite LocationsSee Maintaining Worksite Locations . For more information about the alternative procedure, as well as eligibility requirements, refer to the USCIS website. [Image: screenshot]
  7. Indicate whether the employee’s documents were examined remotely or physically, if applicable, and then enter employer or authorized representative information in the remaining fields. Once all entries have been made, click Next. The Review I-9 step displays again, where the complete Form I-9 can be reviewed. [Image: screenshot]
  8. After reviewing the completed Form I-9, click Submit. A message window displays to confirm the form should be submitted. Click Yes to submit the form. The form is then submitted, and options for downloading or printing the form display. [Image: screenshot]
  9. Click Download to download the completed Form I-9, or click Print to print the form.
  10. After downloading or printing the form as necessary, click Done. The I9 Section 2 form closes.

Working with Pending Approvals

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Pending.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Pending.htm

Working with Pending Approvals

The Approvals Pending form lists any pending approvals that are assigned or available to you.

  • An approval that is assigned to a particular user can only be acted upon by that user.

  • Approvals are available to a user if one of the user’s roles matches the role applicable to the current approval step.

Note: Click the Refresh button to remain on the same form or report while refreshing the window to display new entries.

Note: Note the following when working with pending approvals:

  • Existing employees and newly-entered new hires who have a preferred name display that name on the Approvals Pending form and associated approval forms. In this situation, the system uses the employee’s assigned preferred name instead of their legal first name.

  • If the employee does not have a preferred name, the system uses their first name, last name.

  • I-9 Section 2 and Pay Card approvals, or any approval associated with a pre-hire, display the employee’s legal first and last name even if a preferred name is available.

  • For Late Payroll Posting Approval Policies (see Setting Approval Policies and Setting Global Approval Policies for information about this policy):

  • When a Late Payroll Posting pending approval is approved:

  • The associated payroll batch status updates from POSTPEND > INITCOMP.

  • The Approval status is Approved.

  • Starting Payroll Finalization again after receiving the approval completes the finalization process without disruption, assuming there are no changes to the timesheet or recalculations have occurred in the payroll.

  • If you update the timesheet or recalculate the payroll, the approval must be resubmitted.

  • When a Late Payroll Posting pending approval is denied:

  • The associated payroll batch status updates from POSTPEND > INITCOMP.

  • The Approval status is Denied.

  • When you attempt Payroll Finalization again after the approval is denied, the system generates a new Late Payroll Posting Approval for the payroll.

  • For ACH Maximum Approval Policies (see Setting Approval Policies for information about this policy):

  • When the pending approval is approved and the client is set to Disabled in the Worksite Payroll Approval field on the Control tab in the Client Details form, the associated payroll status updates from INITPEND to INITCOMP and the Approval status changes to Approved.

  • When the pending approval is denied, regardless of the client’s setting in the Worksite Payroll Approval field on the Control tab in the Client Details form, the associated payroll status updates from INITPEND to TSOKCOMP and the Approval status changes to Denied.

  • When the pending approval is approved and the client is set to Standard or Multistep in the Worksite Payroll Approval field on the Control tab in the Client Details form, the associated payroll status updates from INITPEND to AP.PEND and the Approval status changes to Approved.

Approvals Pending (Assigned to You)

You can act on approvals listed in the Approvals Pending (Available to You) panel.

  • Standard Approvals: see Reviewing Standard Approvals

  • Payroll Approvals: see Processing Standard Payroll Approval

  • I-9 Approvals: see Reviewing Form I-9 Approvals

Approvals Pending (Available to You)

To act on an approval that is available to you, “grab” the approval. The system assigns the approval to you.

  1. Within the Approvals Pending (Available to You) panel, click the desired Approval Type. The Approval form opens.

You can click Show Audit History to display the history of users involved in processing the action.

  1. Click Grab. The selected approval type now appears in the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel.

There are two methods available to process an approval:

  • Click the Approve icon (blue checkmark) for the Approval Type you want to approve. Click Yes in the confirmation message that displays to continue with the approval. An approval confirmation displays at the top of the form.

  • “Grab” the approval:

  1. Within the Approvals Pending (Available to You) panel, click the desired Approval Type. The Approval form opens.

You can click Show Audit History to display the history of users involved in processing the action.

  1. Click Grab. The selected approval type now appears in the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel.

Note: Note: You can open the pending approval in Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel and click Return to Queue. The approval pending item is then returned to the Available to You queue for you or another user to access later.

Reviewing Processed Approvals

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Processed.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Processed.htm

Reviewing Processed Approvals

Use the Approvals Processed form to review all approvals processed for the client during a specific date range.

[Image: screenshot]

To review processed approvals:

  1. Go to the Approvals Processed form on the Client|Action menu.

  2. Enter the Start Date and End Date that occurs within one year (for example, 01/01/2022 to 12/31/2022).

Note: Starting with the year 2023, you can enter a date range to display processed approvals more than one year in the past. However, if you enter a range of more than one year (for example, 01/01/2023 to 01/01/2024), the “Date range entered must be within one year” message displays.

  1. Click Submit.

The system displays all approvals that occurred during that date range.

  1. To view the details of an approval, click the Approval Type. The Approval form displays, including the history of the people who created the item that required approval action and the person or people who approved it.

[Image: screenshot]

Reviewing Standard Approvals

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Standard.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Approvals_Standard.htm

Reviewing Standard Approvals

For standard approvals, click the Approval Type to review the information. The Approval form opens.

Note: Note the following when working with approvals:

  • Existing employees and newly-entered new hires who have a preferred name display that name on the Approvals Pending form and associated approval forms. In this situation, the system uses the employee’s assigned preferred name instead of their legal first name.

  • If the employee does not have a preferred name, the system uses their first name, last name.

  • I-9 Section 2 and Pay Card approvals, or any approval associated with a pre-hire, display the employee’s legal first and last name even if a preferred name is available.

For example, the following information displays when you select a Rehire approval:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: You can scroll up or down to view all the information.

Note: Note the following about rehire approvals:

  • The information in the Personal Information, Employment Details, and Pay Details panels in the Rehire form do not update in the employee’s record until the rehire approval has been approved.

  • When you select Launch E-Verification for this rehire (without onboarding or benefits enrollment) for an employee in the Rehire form, the E-Verify process does not start until the rehire is approved.

  • Onboarding and Benefits Enrollment processes are not launched until the rehire approval is completed based on selections made on the Rehire form.

To review standard approvals, do the following:

  1. Review the information on the Approval form.

  • You can click Show Audit History to display the history of users involved in processing the action.

  • If relevant, you can click Employee to view the Employee Details.

  1. After reviewing the information, click one of the following:

  • Approve: Click Yes in the confirmation message that displays to continue with the approval. The action is approved. The system checks if there is another approver, as defined in the approval policy. If there is, the system sends the approval to that user. In the figure above, for example, we are on step 1 of 2, and approving the action would send it to users with the appropriate role for step 2. If it were the final step, then the action would go through and be finalized in the system.

  • Deny: After you click Deny, you must enter your reason for denying the action in the Deny Approval form and then press Tab. A checkbox displays; select it, and then click Deny. Please note that this reason will be stored in the system and might be visible to the user who submitted the request for your approval.

  • Return to Queue: The approval returns to the queue of the current step. Return to Queue does not remove prior approval steps.

Note: Note: Return to Queue displays only for approval steps that are “grabbed” from AM or HR roles.

Working with Bill Pending Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Bill_Pending.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Bill_Pending.htm

Working with Bill Pending Data

The Bill Pending Events panel, on the Bill Pending form, lists billing events that will occur during the next scheduled billing date (in other words, they are pending). These billing events can be created on this form or generated automatically by PrismHR.

Background

There are three ways to create invoice line items:

  • As a scheduled bill, according to payroll dates and so on.

  • As an immediate bill, manually generated using the Miscellaneous Invoice Entry form.

  • As a future-dated or “pending” bill, which waits until the next scheduled billing date. Those items appear in the Bill Pending Events form.

[Image: Bill Pending Events example]

  • To view an existing event, click the event’s Bill Date. The Line Item Details form opens, where you can view the read-only information.

  • To manually create a new event, click New Entry. The Line Item Details form opens, where you can enter the billing event.

See Working with Billing Items

System Billing Events

Several events trigger PrismHR to generate a pending bill for clients. Here are two examples:

  • When you set up a new employee, the system creates a line item for the employee setup fee.

  • When you terminate benefits for an employee, the system searches for a billing event using the TB+ naming convention. For example, if a plan ID is HMOBLUE, the system searches for a billing event named TBHMOBLUE. If it does not find that billing event, the system uses TEBA.

Manual Billing Events

Many reasons and situations exist in which you need to manually create a line item. In each case, PrismHR bills the client upon the next invoice, not an immediate invoice.

Importing Bill Pending Data

You can access the Bill Pending import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import bill pending data by clicking Import

[Image: import icon]

.

The Bill Pending import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display when you access this template directly from the Bill Pending form.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by clicking

[Image: hammer and wrench icon]

(Back Office), then select Data Import Tool on the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the Bill Pending import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing Bill Pending data:

  • Importing only creates new entries. It does not edit or update existing bill pending entries.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

  • Users with a user role set to DENY for the Bill Pending form cannot import data using the Bill Pending import template. The Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users with a user role set to INQUIRY for the Bill Pending form, but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool, cannot view the Import icon on the Bill Pending form.

  • Users with a user role set to INQUIRY for the Bill Pending form can view the Bill Pending import template, but cannot import data.

  • Users with a user role set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

Understanding the E-Verify Bill Pending Fee

The system applies an E-Verify fee in the Bill Pending form for each new hire when the Submit for E-Verification field is enabled on the New Hire form. After saving the new hire, the system automatically creates a bill pending fee using the EV-FEES bill code. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • Billing can be set up globally or have an override at the client level. (The override takes precedence over the global setup, if in place.)

  • You must name the Bill Event Code EV-FEES.

  • For the first 30 days of service for new clients, if you enable Skip Employee Fees for New Clients on the Billing Templates form the system does not create the EV-FEES bill code.

  • If you enable Skip Employee Fees for New Clients on the Billing Templates form, then the system does not create the EV-FEES bill code.

  • If the Employer Start Date on the New Hire form is greater than the Hire Date, then the system uses the Employer Start Date as the EV-FEES bill date.

  • The bill pending date is based on the employee’s hire date and populates the location and employee on the bill pending fee.

  • When the client and worksite location is set up for E-Verify, the system generates an E-Verify fee. However, if the worksite location is excluded, then the system does not generate a fee.

  • This fee structure does not apply to rehires or those who have a different code/fee structure for the employee type.

Working with Billing Items

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Bill_Pending_Line_Item_Details.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Bill_Pending_Line_Item_Details.htm

Working with Billing Items

Use the Bill Pending Details form to enter a billing item or view the details of an existing one. For example:

[Image: Line Item Details form, for entering a new bill pending record]

Note: If you are creating a new line item, enter the necessary information. If you are viewing a line item entry, you cannot make changes.

In addition, you can place a bill-pending item on hold (taking it out of “in-progress” status and returning it to “time-entry” status) by editing its Bill Date, Event, and Bill Rate in the Bill Pending Details form.

Important! This process is not available for restartable payrolls.

To enter a billing item:

  1. Go to the Bill Pending form on the Client|Action menu.

  2. Click New Entry. The Bill Pending Details form displays.

  3. Enter the following information for the line item:

Field — Description

Bill Date — Enter or select the Bill Date on which billing will occur.

Event — Enter or select the billing Event code.

Billing Units — Enter the Billing Units.

Bill Rate — Enter the Bill Rate for each billing unit.

Bill Amount — The Bill Amount field is equal to Billing Units x Bill Rate. If you change the Bill Amount, the system recalculates the Bill Rate to make the numbers work.

Employee — Enter or select the ID of the Employee. The employee’s name displays, as well as any Location, Division, Department, Shift, or Project assignments.

Pay Group — Enter or select the Pay Group code. If you specify a pay group, you must enter a Location so the system can calculate any taxes. Note: You can enter an employee or a pay group (or neither), but you cannot have both in the same line item entry.

Payroll Type — Select the payroll batch type. For example, you can select the Regularly Scheduled payroll type if you only want to bill on a regularly scheduled payroll. (Note: The Select option (default) assumes all payroll types.)

Location — Enter or select the Location code.

Division — Enter or select the Division code. Note: If you have multiple locations with separate bank accounts for each of them, you can debit an invoice for a specific location/division’s bank account by setting it to ACH by Location or ACH by Division and selecting the Non-ACH option on the Client|Change|Worksite Locations form, which would eliminate internal bank transfers when submitting a pending client bill.

Department — Enter or select the Department code.

Shift — Enter or select the Shift.

Project — Enter or select the Project code.

Comment — Enter a Comment for this line item, if appropriate. Note: Comments are restricted to a maximum of 40 characters.

Cost Amount — You can enter the Cost Amount as a positive number to charge against this line item, or you can enter a negative amount to lower the total cost.

Repeat Thru Date — Enter or select a Repeat Thru Date that indicates when the recurring event starts and stops. See Using Repeat Thru Date Correctly for more information.

Repeat Cycle — If you entered a Repeat Thru Date, select a Repeat Cycle for the billing frequency. See Using Repeat Cycle Correctly for more information.

Bill Status — If this is an existing line item, the system displays the Bill Status for this line item.

  1. The Audit Information panel lists the user who entered the line item, the date and time they did so, and the invoice number and date for the billing. (Any entries you make in these fields while creating a bill pending line item will not be saved. The system saves the audit information.)

  2. When you are sure of your entries, click Save.

Note: Note: You can delete a bill pending entry that is included in a payroll batch before the batch is finalized; the payroll processor will be required to recalculate the payroll. Please note that you cannot delete a bill pending item that is included in a payroll batch that is awaiting the client’s approval.

To edit a bill-pending item to place it on hold:

  1. Reset the Payroll Calculation: Complete status to Payroll Calculation: Ready on the calculated payroll by finalizing the timesheets again in the Process Payroll form. (This step deletes any initialized vouchers, so the payroll is no longer initialized.)

  2. Select the bill-pending item on the Bill Pending form. The Bill Pending Details form displays.

  3. Update the Bill Date and other pertinent information, which places the bill-pending item on hold.

  4. Click Save. After initializing the payroll again, the edited bill-pending item no longer displays in the initialized invoice.

Note: Note the following:

  • You cannot edit or delete an item that is “complete” (on a posted invoice).

  • You cannot edit or delete an item “in-progress” (on an initialized invoice).

  • You can edit or delete an item that was “in-progress,” if the timesheets are finalized (this forces payroll to reinitialize).

  • You can edit or delete an item that is “pending” (does not appear on any invoice).

Avoiding Pitfalls

Client Bill Pending is an extremely useful tool; at the same time, problems can occur if you are not careful. PrismHR does only what you tell it to do, so ensure your entries are correct. Client Bill Pending is both employee-sensitive and date-sensitive.

Employee Sensitivity

For example, you set up a new employee named Joe, who is an active employee and is paid once a month on the first. You create a line item for Joe on the second day of the month.

PrismHR does not convert the line item into a bill for the client until Joe’s next pay day, which is the first of the following month.

If this feature was not employee-sensitive, you would bill the client on the next invoice, which could fall before the first of the month.

Date Sensitivity

For example, you create a line item for January 1, 2024, but specify a billing date of January 1, 2026 by mistake. The line item remains dormant in PrismHR until the date you specified, then is included in the next invoice after that date.

Using Repeat Thru Date Correctly

If you set up a recurring event, you must define the date limits of the event. The system uses the Repeat Thru Date field to know when the recurring event starts and when it stops.

Be very aware of the dates you want to define and double-check your date entries. The system automatically calculates if any dates have been “missed” upon the first payroll run after you define the recurring event.

For example, you have a weekly billing event for a payroll that you want to occur starting on or after January 1, 2024. You enter 12/31/24 in the Repeat Thru Date field. However, you mistakenly enter the start date (in the Bill Date field) as 1/1/23 instead of 1/1/24. Upon January 2, 2024 (the first business day of the year), PrismHR realizes it has not performed its weekly billing (based on the check date) since the starting date. Therefore, PrismHR creates 52 transactions in order to “catch up” to the present date.

Using Repeat Cycle Correctly

If you set up a recurring event, you must define how often to repeat the event. The system uses the Repeat Cycle field to know how often it must run the recurring event.

For example, you have a monthly billing event for an end-of-the-month payroll. The system reads what you enter literally: in other words, it is very exact. You mistakenly enter the billing date (in the Bill Date field) as 1/31/24 instead of 1/28/24. Upon February 29, 2024 (because of the leap year – otherwise February 28), PrismHR realizes it is not the 31st day of the month. Therefore, PrismHR skips the transaction.

Cost

If you enter an amount in the cost section of Bill Pending, the cost is updated when a payroll is posted.

For example, you need to set up a client bill pending for an overbilling on SUTA. The rate you charge your client is 2.7% and you mistakenly charged them 3.00% on $15,000 taxable wages. SUTA is not a profit center for this client so the billing should match the cost. The liability was calculated at the correct rate of 2.7%. When entering the client’s credit for $45.00, leave the cost column blank. There is no need to adjust cost.

As another example, you bill your client for drug testing the employees. The arrangement you make with the client is they pay $28.00 per drug test. Your provider bills you $20.00 per employee. Enter the client bill pending as $20.00 in the cost field so that the system can calculate the income.

Changing the Client Status

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Client_Status_Change.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Client_Status_Change.htm

Changing the Client Status

Define a client status in the Client Statuses form.

Display a company’s current status or change a company’s status with the Client Status Change form.

To change the client status:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Action, select Client Status Change.

  3. Change status as described in the table, then click Save when all changes are completed.

To do this Perform this action
Set the client status to Active, Inactive, or Pending 1. Select New Status for this client. 2. Enter the Effective Date. 3. Click Save. Note: Setting the status to Inactive displays the Client Inactivation Reason field (required), where you can enter or select a corresponding reason code associated with the client’s inactive status. (If you select Client Inactivation Reason, only reasons matching Inactive display in the Search Inactive Reason list.)
  1. Select New Status for this client.
  2. Enter the Effective Date.
  3. Click Save. | Set the client status to Client Cancelling (pre-termination) | 1. Select Client Cancelling from the New Status drop-down. This indicates that this client will terminate at a future date. 2. Enter the Effective Date for changing to pre-termination status. 3. Enter the Planned Termination Date. 4. Click Save. |
  4. Select Client Cancelling from the New Status drop-down. This indicates that this client will terminate at a future date.
  5. Enter the Effective Date for changing to pre-termination status.
  6. Enter the Planned Termination Date.
  7. Click Save. | Set the client status to Terminated | Before terminating a client: - Ensure the correct client is selected. - Ensure that no payrolls need completing. Important! Terminations cannot be reversed. To terminate a client: 1. Select Terminated from the New Status drop-down. The system displays a warning about what occurs when a client is terminated and asks if you still want to proceed. 2. Click Yes. 3. Enter the Effective Date for the termination. 4. Click Client Termination Reason and select a reason in the Select Term Reason list. Note: Only applicable reasons display in the Select Term Reason list when you select the Client Termination Reason field (required). (The New Status field must be set to Terminated to display this field.) 5. From the Do you want to terminate active employees for this company drop-down, select the appropriate option: - No: The employees’ statuses do not change. A user must manually change those employees’ statuses using the appropriate form, as required. - Yes: When you choose Yes, the system displays the employee termination fields. Set the changes you want to apply to all active employees.  | a.  | Employee Termination Status: Defined on the Employee Statuses form.  |  | —  | —  |  | b.  | Employee Termination Date: Date employee will be terminated.  |  | —  | —  |  | c.  | Employee Termination Reason: Defined on the Reasons form.  |  | —  | —  | d. — Enroll employees with COBRA-electable benefits in COBRA: If enabled, the system will create COBRA participant records for those employees. 6.   • Ensure the correct client is selected.
  • Ensure that no payrolls need completing.
  1. Select Terminated from the New Status drop-down. The system displays a warning about what occurs when a client is terminated and asks if you still want to proceed.
  2. Click Yes.
  3. Enter the Effective Date for the termination.
  4. Click Client Termination Reason and select a reason in the Select Term Reason list.
  5. From the Do you want to terminate active employees for this company drop-down, select the appropriate option:
  • No: The employees’ statuses do not change. A user must manually change those employees’ statuses using the appropriate form, as required.

Copying Client Settings

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Copy_Client_Settings.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Copy_Client_Settings.htm

Copying Client Settings

You can copy client settings using attributes on the Copy Client Settings form from an existing client to another client. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To copy client settings, do the following:

  1. Go to the Copy Client Settings form on the Operations|Utility Processing menu and complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Copy From Client — Enter or select the client ID or client name whose settings you want to copy.

Copy to Client — Enter or select the Client ID where you want the settings copied. The Client Name displays.

  1. Deselect or select the attributes for this client in the What to Copy panel.

Note: Note the following:

  • Enable the Retirement Plan Setup field to include the retirement plan Benefit Plan Setup information and Company Match Rules.

  • If you enable the Company Benefits or Benefit Rules field and disable the Retirement Plan Setup field then during the copy client process, the system will not copy the following client configuration records:

  • The records available when you select Plan Class as Retirement or Employer Match on the Benefit Plan Setup form.

  • All the records available on the Retirement Match Rules form.

  1. Click Copy. The Existing Data panel displays existing client settings.

  2. Click Update Existing. A warning message displays, “This process will override any existing records in the client. Are you sure you want to continue?” Click Yes to continue.

Note: Note the following:

  • Any custom work regarding copying client settings that PrismHR already performed does not apply to this new feature.

  • Client Access Group security is enabled on this form.

  • Users whose role is set to INQUIRY for the Copy Client Settings form cannot enter or select the Copy From Client or the Copy to Client.

Deleting a Pending Client

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Action/Delete-Pending-Client.htm Path: PD/Company/Action/Delete-Pending-Client.htm

Deleting a Pending Client

Use this form to remove a client entered into the system that never actually ran payroll and is no longer expected to be a client.

Prerequisites

The following prerequisites must be met before you can perform this action:

  • A ‘Pending’ Client Status must exist at the system level, with a Status Class of ‘Pending’ (System|Change|Client Statuses)

  • The client must first be terminated (Client|Action|Client Status Change process).

  • The client must then be changed to a ‘Pending’ status (Client|Action|Client Status Change process).

  • The client cannot have any employees or payroll history.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To delete a pending client:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Action, click Delete Pending Client.

  3. Click the Client link to select a client. The status must be Pending.

  4. Click Delete Client to complete this process.

Miscellaneous Charges

Use the Miscellaneous Charges form to enter information into the Adjustments column of the Profitability Report.

WARNING: Once you save a Miscellaneous Charge, you cannot edit or delete it. Be sure the information is correct before saving.

[Image: Miscellaneous charges form]

To submit this form, do the following:

  1. Enter the invoice Bill Date.

  2. Enter the Bill Code. The bill code Description displays.

  3. Enter a Comment for this charge.

  4. Enter a Cost amount, if appropriate.

  5. Review the information for accuracy.

  6. Click Save.

Working with Data Retriever

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Analytics-Data%20Retriever/Data_Retriever_Q3.htm Path: PD/Company/Analytics-Data Retriever/Data_Retriever_Q3.htm

Working with Data Retriever

The Data Retriever form is a quick and flexible way to retrieve, export, and manipulate data from your system. You can quickly pull raw data from multiple clients and the entities within these clients. Data Retriever can give you access to aggregate system data for purposes of analysis or analytics.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: By default, the company tax and workers’ compensation data is available only to the service providers. Unless you enable client access to this data these items are restricted from view for worksite manager and worksite trusted advisor users.

Use the + to expand the categories in any column.

To create a Data Retriever report:

  1. In the Step 1 column, select the clients you want to query. You can select as many clients as you want.

  2. In the Step 2 column, select the fields you want to query. If you query Employment Status, you can click [Image: screenshot] to select statuses on which to filter.

  3. In the Step 3 column, select the totals you want, then select a date range from the calendar.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. To add another date range or to select additional data, click the date range link.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Optionally, you can expand certain items and select a sort order for them.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. Click Run. When your report is ready, the results display.

Note: Note: Data retriever is configured to calculate up to four reports at one time. If a user attempts to generate a report while the four reports are calculating, the report will be placed in a queue. If you would like to view the status of any report that has been run or is currently in the queue, you can navigate to Data Retriever>Actions menu>View Run Report (Last 24 hours).

  1. To export the report to a spreadsheet format, click Export. The system exports your data to a .csv file.

From the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

, you can select:

  • Suppress/Do not suppress lines with zero totals

  • Save Report Template -To save a report as a template or edit and replace an existing template. For more information on saving report templates by user type, see See Save Report Template by User Type

Note: Note: You can save report templates for yourself only or share them and provide access to specific user types.

  • Load Report Template

  • Export Report

  • New Report

  • View Run Reports (Last 24 Hours)

  • Show Descriptions/Do Not Show Descriptions

  • Show Descriptions/Show Code and Descriptions

  • Add to/Remove from Favorites

For more information on the fields in Data Retriever, See Available Data

Save Report Template by User Type

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Analytics-Data%20Retriever/Data_Retriever_Save_Report_Template_by_Use_Type.htm Path: PD/Company/Analytics-Data Retriever/Data_Retriever_Save_Report_Template_by_Use_Type.htm

Save Report Template by User Type

When you save a report template in Data Retriever, you can specify which type of user can have access to it.

  1. From the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] in Data Retriever, select Save Report Template.

  2. Enable the option to save the report for yourself only or select a user type to associate with the report:

  • Service Providers - only Service Providers can see the report.

  • Worksite Managers - Service Providers and Worksite Managers can see the report.

  • Trusted Advisers - Service Providers, Worksite Managers, and Trusted Advisers can see the report.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

To edit an existing report template, load the template, make changes, and save it. Be sure to select Replace the existing report?.

To create a new report template from an existing one, load the template, make changes, and save it. Be sure to change the Description of Report and clear the option to Replace the existing report?.

Data Retriever Security

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Analytics-Data%20Retriever/Data_Retriever_Security.htm Path: PD/Company/Analytics-Data Retriever/Data_Retriever_Security.htm

Data Retriever Security

By default, access to potentially sensitive data is restricted in Data Retriever. For example, company tax and workers’ compensation data is available only to service providers. Unless you enable client access to this data for worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor users, it does not display.

Your organization should have a well-planned strategy for applying role-based security. Based on the role associated with a user account, you can define the forms and fields in PrismHR that users can view or edit. You can also define the fields that users can query.

Step 2 Data

You can set the security for Step 2 of the Data Retriever query process on the User Roles form. Step 2 data is pulled from the following forms:

  • EMPLOYEE.PER*MAINT

  • EMPLOYEE.PER*MAINT.PERSONAL

  • EMPLOYEE.PER*MAINT.PAY

Toggle the Access option to control access to the data.

  • Full and Inquiry access allows the user role to work with the data.

  • Deny access prevents the user role from viewing or querying the data.

[Image: screenshot]

For field-level security, click the Field link. The Field Security Maintenance form displays. Toggle the Enable/Disable/Hidden options for each field.

  • Enable access allows the user role to work with the data.

  • Disable access allows the user role to see that there is data, but prevents working with it.

  • Hidden access prevents the user role from even seeing that the data is in the system.

[Image: screenshot]

Step 3 Security

For the calculated data that displays in Step 3, you also set security on the User Roles form. Full, Inquiry, or Deny access is set on AGG.DATA*GETIT.

To set field-level security for Step 3 data, click the Field link.

[Image: screenshot]

The Field Security form displays. Use the toggle switch to enable or restrict access to each field.

[Image: screenshot]

You can also enable or restrict access to the expanded data items that display after you set the date rage.

[Image: screenshot]

For example, Invoiced items include Billing Format, Invoiced As and Detail Code. You must set security for each of these items.

The Invoiced items in Step 3 include only amounts from billing vouchers that are associated with an employee ID. Miscellaneous charges (for example, delivery fees) are not included on the reports. If miscellaneous or bundled charges are included, client reports will not balance back to the invoice.

Note: Note: Note that if a client changes billing format mid-year, Data Retriever shows the billing breakout formats. Once the data cube is rebuilt, the data will only break out based upon the current billing format.

Update for Imported Checks

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Analytics-Data%20Retriever/Update_for_Imported_Checks.htm Path: PD/Company/Analytics-Data Retriever/Update_for_Imported_Checks.htm

Update for Imported Checks

If you used the backend system to import employee payroll vouchers, you must use the Data Retriever Update for Imported Checks utility to ensure that your data is accessible in Data Retriever. This feature allows your data to flow to the appropriate employee payroll vouchers and registers, exactly as if payroll was processed in PrismHR.

After you import employee payroll vouchers:

  1. From the Back Office menu, under System|Action, select Data Retriever for Imported Checks.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Client ID and Year.

  2. Click Run.

  3. The system displays a confirmation message. Click OK.

Vouchers that have been imported through the backend system are now included in Step 3 data. Although the data remains in system, the temporary data files are purged when the import batch is posted.

Linking PTO Accrual Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Accruing_PTO_Hours_for_Future_Use.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Accruing_PTO_Hours_for_Future_Use.htm

Linking PTO Accrual Plans

.

You can use the Linked Accrual Plans feature to accrue employee PTO hours for transfer once the plan year ends. This feature consists of two PTO plans that you must set up so employees can accrue time for a plan year in one register that they cannot use until the plan year ends. After the plan year ends, the accrued hours move to a new register where employees can use their PTO hours. (Employees can have several different linked accrual plans, one for sick time, one for vacation time, and so on.)

Note: Note the following:

  • Employees must be enrolled in two PTO registers (one for accrual and one for balance) to use this feature. If there is no Balance PTO register associated with the linked class for the employee, then the system does not move anything will from the Accrual register to the Balance register. (The hours remain in the Accrual register.)

  • If the Balance PTO Register has a yearly maximum set, the system moves prior year balances from the Accrual register to the Balance register up to the maximum amount. (The remaining amount in the accrual register is lost.)

  • When the system transfers accrued hours from the Accrual register to the Balance register, it places the hours in the Balance register in the YTD Calculated column.

  • Accrual plans accrue for the pay period and then transfer to the Balance plan.

Using Linked Accrual Plans Feature

To use the Linked Accrual Plans feature, you must set up two PTO plans in the PTO Benefit Plans form:

  • Accrual plan: Accrues employee PTO time during the year. At the end of the plan year, the system transfers the hours in the accrual plan to the linked balance plan. (The Accrual plan resets to zero.) Employees can now use the accrued hours in the Balance plan.

  • Balance plan: Contains the transferred PTO accrual time.

The following diagram demonstrates the setup for Linked Accrual Plans:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • The employee’s accrual balance for the current year is the year-to-date PTO hours accrued from the previous year.

  • Employees carry over some hours in some cases, but most plans expire the hours at the end of the plan year.

  • Most of these plans are set up as anniversary plans, not by the plan year.

Setting Up the Linked Accrual Plans Feature

Set up the following:

  1. PTO Class: Use the PTO Classes form to define which PTO classes will be used in conjunction with each other, consisting of a PTO class for the accrual and a PTO class for the balance. (See the Client Administration Guide for details on defining PTO classes.)

For example:

a. Create a PTO Class Code for the Balance plan. For example, VACBAL.

Note: Do not enable Linked Accrual.

b. Create a PTO Class Code for the Accrual plan. For example, VACACCR.
c. Enable Linked Accrual.
— —
d. In the Transfer To field, select VACBAL.
— —

Note: These PTO classes are now linked and cannot be linked to other classes.

  1. PTO Register: Enable the Prior Year Accrual trigger method on the PTO Register Types form to trigger the balance register to move the accrued PTO hours from the accrual plan to the balance plan. (See the Client Administration Guide for details on working with PTO registers.)

For example:

a. Create a PTO Type Code register for the Balance plan. For example, VACBAL.

  • PTO Class: VACBAL.

  • Year End Basis: Set Anniversary or Fiscal, but should match the Accrual register.

  • Trigger Method: Prior Year Accrual.

  • Trigger Date: Do not use.

  • Print on Check, Pay Stub Name, Paid Absence, Absence Code: Set these fields for the Balance register.

b. Create a PTO Type Code register for the Accrual plan. For example, VACACCR.

  • PTO Class: VACACCR.

  • Year End Basis: Set Anniversary or Fiscal, but should match the Balance register.

  • Trigger Method: Select any trigger method.

  • Trigger Date: Select any trigger date.

  • Absence Code: Set this field for the accrual register. (It is not currently used, but it should be populated.)

  • Print on Check, Pay Stub Name, Inactive Register, and Paid Absence: Do not use.

  1. PTO Benefit Plan: Enroll employees in an Accrual plan and a Balance plan on the PTO Benefit Plans form. The Accrual plan determines the PTO time accrued. The system does not use the Balance plan to determine the accruals, but employees must be enrolled in both plans for the transfer to work correctly.

  1. — Create a PTO Benefit Plan for the Balance plan. For example, VACBAL. (You must select a calculation basis, but the system does not use it to calculate accruals.)

  2. — Create a PTO Benefit Plan for the Accrual plan. For example, VACACCR. (This will hold the settings for the accruals.)

Understanding the Transfer Process

When the system transfers accrued PTO hours, the employee register displays the accrued amount in the calculated column in the Balance register and displays an annotation, “Accrual Transfer MM/DD/YY,” in the details. The system then zeroes out the year-to-date accrual amounts in the employee Accrual register and displays an annotation, “XX number of hours moved to linked plan.”

========================================================================

Adjusting Summary Time Sheet Columns

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Adjusting_Summary_Time_Sheet_Columns.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Adjusting_Summary_Time_Sheet_Columns.htm

Adjusting Summary Time Sheet Columns

Users specify the Summary Time Sheet Pay Codes in the Client Details Time Sheet tab. This determines the pay columns that display in Summary Time Sheet Entry.

Note: Note: Client Details is found in the Client menu under Client|Change.

You can change or reorder the columns as needed and the system displays messages to alert you to the impact that your edits will have. Use one of the following procedures to change or reorder the columns and get the results you want:

  • Changing Columns

  • Reordering Columns

  • Changing to a Different Type of Pay

Changing Columns

You can change the column from one pay code to another with the same earning type (such as cash).

  1. In the Time Sheet tab of Client Details, edit one of the Pay Codes to change it to one for the same type of pay (for example, REG1 to REG2).

  2. In the message “This action will replace pay code [REG1] with pay code [REG2] for all current summary time sheet records. These changes will occur when this form is saved. Do you wish to continue?” click the Yes button to reassign the values to the new pay code (for example, REG2).

Note: Note: If you click No, the system does not change the pay code.

  1. Click Save.

Reordering Columns

If you want to rearrange the columns and, ultimately, keep the current hours assigned to the same pay codes they were before you changed the columns, follow these steps.

  1. In the Time Sheet tab of Client Details, edit one of the Pay Codes to change it to one for the same type of pay (for example, REG3 to REG4).

  2. In the message “This action will replace pay code [REG3] with pay code [REG4] for all current summary time sheet records. These changes will occur when this form is saved. Do you wish to continue?” click the Yes button to (temporarily) reassign the values to the new pay code (for example, REG4).

Note: Note: If you click No, the system does not change the pay code.

  1. Enter the original pay code (for example, REG3) in a new column.

  2. In the message, “You previously stated that you would like to replace [REG3] pay code on active time sheets with [REG4]. Do you still wish to do this [Yes], or move the values associated with the [REG3] to this new position [No]?”:

  • Click Yes to leave any values assigned to the new pay code (REG4).

  • Click No to assign any values back to the original pay code (REG3).

  1. Click Save.

Changing to a Different Type of Pay

If you need to change a pay code from one type of pay to another, you can only do so if there are no values entered in an active payroll. Examples are when you change to a pay code with a different calculation method or pay reason.

  • If there are no values, you can simply change the pay code.

  • When there are values in an active payroll, the system displays a message informing you that you need to use Payroll Control to delete and recreate the payroll. If that is what you need to do:

  1. Open Payroll Control (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Change).

  2. Enter the Payroll Number of the active payroll.

  3. Click Delete.

  4. In the confirmation message, click Yes.

  5. Click Yes in the message to create a replacement payroll for the one you just deleted.

  6. Return to the Client Details Time Sheet tab and change the pay code.

  7. Users can proceed to enter data in the time sheet for the new pay code.

Note: Note: If there are only a few values in the time sheet, you can simply delete them from the time sheet rather than deleting the entire payroll and creating a new one.

Hiding or Disabling the All Cash Accounts Panel

Service providers can hide or disable the All Cash Accounts panel from their worksite managers and worksite trusted advisors, so those users cannot view and select system bank accounts.

Note: Note the following before hiding or disabling the All Cash Accounts panel:

  • You must first add the ASO Global G/L Account Setup form to the worksite manager’s or worksite trusted advisor’s user role to disable or hide this panel.

  • The Delete button is unavailable when you hide or disable the All Cash Accounts panel.

To hide or disable this panel, do the following:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From System|Change, select User Roles.

  3. Enter or select the Role ID.

  4. Filter the Search for Description field to ASO Global G/L Account Setup. The form’s information displays.

  5. Select Field in the Security column. The Field Security Maintenance form displays.

  6. Click Add. The Field Security Selection form displays “All Cash Accounts” (the panel you want to disable or hide) in the Description column.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the checkbox in the Select Field column, then click Accept. The Field Security Maintenance form displays.

  2. Select Enable in the Permissions column to set access to either Disable or Hidden (these options achieve the same result).

  3. Click Save.

Building Structure for Chart of Accounts Segments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/ASO_GL_Accounts.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/ASO_GL_Accounts.htm

Building Structure for Chart of Accounts Segments

Use the ASO G/L Account Setup form to build the structure for chart of accounts segments, if they are different than what is set up in the global parameters. Indicate the overrides, if any, that are necessary to meet the client’s account structure requirements.

To set up the structure for chart of accounts segments:

  1. Enter the core Account code to build.

  2. If required, enter the variable or string Prefix Values for the G/L account number. For example, enter 01 to prefix the G/L account numbers with 01.

  3. If required, enter the variable or string Suffix Values for the G/L account number.

  4. Select the Segment Delimiter to use between the account number and the prefix or suffix values.

Prefix/Suffix Overrides

If the G/L account requires a prefix or suffix override, enter that information in the Prefix/Suffix Overrides panel.

  1. For each override:

  • Enter the When Prefix/Suffix term to override, for example, DEPARTMENT.

  • Enter the is equal to value, for example, ACCT.

  • Enter the replace with value to use instead, for example, 700.

In our example, PrismHR replaces the department code ACCT with 700. The account number displays as 700-6000 in the report or download for the client.

Account Overrides

If the G/L account requires an account override, enter that information in the Account Overrides panel.

  1. For each override:

  • Select the When Field Equals term to override, for example, LOCATION.

  • Enter the is equal to value, for example, MAIN.

  • Enter the replace account with value to use instead, for example, 6000.10.

In our example, PrismHR overrides the account number for the MAIN location with 6000.10.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up the ASO Global G/L Account

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/ASO_Global_GL_Account.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/ASO_Global_GL_Account.htm

Setting Up the ASO Global G/L Account

Use the panels in the ASO Global G/L Account Setup form to establish all general ledger accounts for an Administration Services Only (ASO) client. This sets up the global parameters and builds the structure that determines how to assign the account number to codes in PrismHR.

  • Maintaining Client and Employee Allocation Parameters: Determines how payroll account numbers are set up

  • Prefix/Suffix Overrides: Establishes override rules

  • Payroll Accounting: Establishes G/L Codes

  • All Deduction Codes: Establishes accounts for deduction codes

  • All Benefit Plans: Establishes accounts for benefit plans

  • All Workers’ Compensation States: Establishes accounts for Workers’ Compensation

  • All Tax Codes: Establishes accounts for taxes

  • Maintaining ASO Client Accounting: Establishes accounts for cash management

  • Bill Code ASO Accounts: Establishes accounts for bill codes

Maintaining Client and Employee Allocation Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/ASO_Payroll_Accounting_Setup.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/ASO_Payroll_Accounting_Setup.htm

Maintaining Client and Employee Allocation Parameters

Use the ASO Global G/L Account Setup form to maintain client and employee allocation parameters, account prefix and suffix values for general ledger accounts, and the accounting system download file type for ASO companies. You can also copy all ASO accounting setup data from one client to another using the Copy G/L To Another Client section.

[Image: screenshot]

To maintain client and employee allocation parameters:

  1. Select Allocate Company Contributions to allocate client contributions to cost accounting in the same proportion as the time sheet earnings.

  2. Select Allocate Employee Deductions to allocate employee deductions in the same proportion as the time sheet earnings.

Note: Note: You can enter prefixes or suffixes only if allowed by the G/L accounts.

  1. Select each Account Prefix used by the client, if any.

  2. Select each Account Suffix used by the client, if any.

  3. Select the type of Segment Delimiter that separates the client’s G/L account segments, if any.

  4. Enter the Core Account Segment No of the account number that contains the G/L account setup.

  5. Select the third-party Accounting System Type used by the client to create download files in the corresponding format.

Accounting packages that use comma-delimited text files can use the Peachtree format; it is also the generic choice for accounting system type.

  1. Enter the Name of Output File to specify the name of the file that the system creates for the client accounting download, if any.

  2. Click Save.

Copying ASO Accounting Setup Data

To help reduce the manual labor and margin for error associated with a manual entry, you can copy all ASO accounting setup data from one client to another using the Copy G/L To Another Client section.

To copy ASO accounting setup data from one client to another:

  1. In the Copy G/L To Another Client section, enter or select the Client ID. If you select the Client ID, the Select Client list displays. Search for the client by entering the Client ID or Client Name, then filter the selection further by selecting the Service Type and Status. The clients that match your entry display.

  2. After you select a client, that entry displays back in the Copy G/L To Another Client section.

  3. Select Copy G/L to Client. Note the following:

  • If the client you select contains any existing data in the ASO Accounting Codes, ASO G/L Account Setup, or ASO Global G/L Account Setup forms, when you select Copy G/L to Client the following message displays, “Client <selected Client ID> already has existing data for ASO Accounting Codes, ASO G/L Account Setup or ASO Global G/L Account Setup. Data cannot be copied over existing records.” Therefore, you cannot continue with this process.

  • If the client you select does not contain any existing data in the ASO Accounting Codes, ASO G/L Account Setup, and ASO Global G/L Account Setup forms, when you select Copy G/L to Client the following confirmation message displays, “You are about to copy the G/L setup from client <active client> to client <selected Client ID.> This will copy over any existing values from ASO Accounting Codes, ASO G/L Account Setup form, and ASO Global G/L Account Setup form. Do you wish to continue?” Click Yes to copy the ASO accounting setup data.

  • If you select Copy G/L to Client without selecting a Client ID the following message displays, “Select a client to continue.”

  • If you select the active client ID as the copy to client the following message displays, “Copy from and copy to client cannot be the same.”

  1. After clicking Yes in the confirmation message, the copy process starts. Note the following:

  • The process copies all ASO Accounting Codes records, including all records set up on the ASO G/L Account setup form and all records set up on the ASO Global G/L Account setup forms for the active client to the selected client.

  • The process is only successful when there is no existing data for the three forms, ASO Accounting Codes, ASO G/L Account Setup, and ASO Global G/L Account Setup forms, in the copy to the client.

  1. After the copy process finishes, the active record displays on the active form and the following confirmation message displays, “ASO Accounting records have successfully been copied to client <selected Client ID>.”

Working with Auto Holiday Pay Days

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Auto_Holiday_Pay_Days.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Auto_Holiday_Pay_Days.htm

Working with Auto Holiday Pay Days

You can use the Auto Holiday Pay Days form to define which days apply to employee holiday time. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Access this form by selecting the Days link on the Auto Holiday Pay form. (See Adding Auto Holiday Pay.)

To set up employee holiday days:

  1. Enter the Year where you want to apply the holiday.

  2. Click the Holiday Dates grid to select a date from the calendar.

  3. Click Save.

Note: Note the following:

  • You can enter or select holidays up to ten years in the past or ten years in the future from the current year (defaults to the current year). You can enter or select holidays up to ten years in the past or one year in the future (defaults to the current year). (Note: If you enter a date that does not meet this criteria an error message displays, “The following date(s) are not in the year selected and will be removed <date>.” Click OK to continue.)

  • If you add or delete a date that occurs in an in-process payroll, you need to recalculate the payroll to apply or remove that date.

  • If you try to delete an override date by clicking the X next to the date in the Holiday Dates panel a warning displays, “All overrides for this date will be deleted.” Click Yes to continue.

  • The system tracks date changes made in this form in the audit log.

Maintaining Auto Holiday Pay Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Auto_Holiday_Pay_Rule_Maintenance.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Auto_Holiday_Pay_Rule_Maintenance.htm

Maintaining Auto Holiday Pay Rules

You can use the Auto Holiday Pay Rule Maintenance form to define the rules which determine the amount of automatic holiday pay for employees and how many hours they receive. In addition, you can edit an existing rule to make updates.

Note: Access this form by selecting the New Rule link on the Auto Holiday Pay form. (See Adding Auto Holiday Pay.)

To set up paying employee holiday time:

  1. Select the client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Auto Holiday Pay. The Auto Holiday Pay form displays.

  3. Click New Rule. The Auto Holiday Pay Rule Maintenance form displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter or select the parameters to define which employees receive the holiday, then complete the rest of the fields as necessary.

Note: Note the following:

  • If you enter an Employee Type (optional) that does not exist the system displays an error message, “Record <employee type> is not on file EMPLOYEE.TYPE.CODES.” Click OK to continue.

  • The Variable Salary option only displays in the Pay Method field if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form. You can use the Variable Salary option to accurately track the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work. For example, non-profit companies could use this option to track the work hours of their employees who are paid a standard salaried amount, so they can use these hours and their allocations for grant purposes.

  • If you enter a Pay Code (required) that does not exist the system displays an error message, “Record <pay code> is not on file EARNING.CODES.” Click OK to continue.

  • If you enter an alpha character as the Number of Hours (required) an error message displays, “A valid number is required.” Click OK to continue.

  • If you save a rule without entering the required information, an error message displays.

  • You can enter or select the Override Dates based on information that exists on the Auto Holiday Pay Days form. (You cannot duplicate override dates.)

Note: The Auto Holiday Override Hours display on the employee’s timesheet when the criteria for the Auto Holiday Pay Rule and the Override Date exists in the current regular payroll after it is created.

  1. Click Save. The rule displays on the Auto Holiday Pay form.

Note: After you create the rule, the system adds the holiday hours from the rule on that day into the timesheet when you create the payroll. In addition, you can edit or delete the rule by clicking the Line #.
