Defining Billing Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules.htm

Defining Billing Rules

Client billing can be bundled or unbundled:

  • Bundled billing charges a lump sum percentage of gross payroll per pay period. It includes FUTA, SUTA, FICA, and workers’ compensation.

  • Unbundled billing breaks out each line item charge for payroll taxes, workers’ comp premiums, and administrative fees.

Note: Note: The billing method (bundled or unbundled) does not affect the invoice format. Both the bill format and the billing template are set on the Billing tab in the Client Details form.

After defining billing rules, you can begin to enter other billing settings on the Billing tab on the Client Details form.

========================================================================

Using Bundled Billing Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Bundled.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Bundled.htm

Using Bundled Billing Rules

Use the Bundled Billing Rules form to establish information for charging one fee per workers’ compensation class for FUTA, SUTA, FICA, and workers’ compensation. You can “bundle” benefit billing based on the workers’ compensation code.

You can use the Bundled Billings Comp Class workers’ compensation code to reflect actual costs that are based on workers’ compensation classifications. You can also set up bundled billing so that each Comp Class has the same billing rate, such as when a client has a flat rate for all classes of employees.

To set up bundled billing:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Billing Rules (Bundled).

  3. Fill in these fields as required.

Field Description
Billing Rule Enter an existing Billing Rule ID, or click New Rule to create a new bundled billing rule. The system automatically assigns an ID.
Workers’ Compensation Code Enter the Workers’ Compensation Code for this bundled billing rule.
Price Code If appropriate, select the Price Code to use instead of the standard billing rate. The Price Code overrides the Bundled Rate set on the Bundled Billing Rule panel for any employee assigned to this job code and a matching Price Code. Use this field if an employee is granted special pricing (rather than using the standard bundled rate and with an employee-specific add-on unbundled rule, which is more common).
Bundled Rate Enter the Bundled Rate to use when billing the client for this workers’ compensation code. This percentage is multiplied by the Unadjusted Gross Taxable pay.
Special Bundled Rate If required, enter the Special Bundled Rate to use for a specific date range.
Special Rate From Date Enter the date when the Special Bundled Rate begins. Payrolls before this date do not use the rate.
Special Rate To Date Enter the date when the Special Bundled Rate ends. Payrolls after this date do not use the rate.
Rule Start Date/Rule End Date If the rule only applies during a specific date range: - Enter the Rule Start Date when this rule begins. - Enter the Rule End Date when this rule ends.
  • Enter the Rule Start Date when this rule begins.
  • Enter the Rule End Date when this rule ends.
  1. If Bundled Tiers (Optional) are required:

  • Enter the Maximum Pay Level covered by this billing rate. Use this field only if bundled rates vary by pay level.

  • Select the Department Code for this billing tier.

  • Enter each of the Pay Codes affected by this billing rate.

  1. If bundled rules exist for the client, they display in the Existing Bundled Rules panel.

  2. Click Save.

========================================================================

Using Unbundled Billing Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled.htm

Using Unbundled Billing Rules

Use the Billing Rules (Unbundled) form to create the individual fees you charge to your clients.

To set unbundled billing rules:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Billing Rules (Unbundled). The Billing Rules (Unbundled) form displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Billing Rule Enter or select an existing Billing Rule ID, or click New Rule to create a new unbundled billing rule. The system automatically assigns an ID.
Rule Description Enter a Rule Description.
Rate Enter the billing Rate. The system uses this in conjunction with the Based On value.
Based On Select a Based On value, which represents the logic to use when calculating the rate. For more information about “based on” options, see Using Unbundled Bill Rules Based On Options.
Use Timesheet Location Or State Only displays for rules based on Percent of Wages or Percent of WorkerComp Billed. If enabled, the billing rule targets the location or state billed on the time sheet.
Rule Start Date/Rule End Date If the rule only applies during a specific date range: - Enter the Rule Start Date when this rule begins. - Enter the Rule End Date when this rule ends.
  • Enter the Rule Start Date when this rule begins.
  • Enter the Rule End Date when this rule ends. | Voucher Types | Select the Voucher Types to which a billing rule applies. | | Employee | If the billing rule applies to a single employee, enter the Employee ID. If the billing rule applies to all employees who do not have another billing rule assigned to them, leave this field blank. | | Batch Type | Select the Batch Type to which this billing rule applies. | | Employee Type | Select the Employee Type to which this billing rule applies, if any. For example, FT is a full time employee. | | Pay Group | Select the Pay Group to which this billing rule applies, if any. For example, W is a weekly pay group. | | Deduction Period | Enter the Deduction Period number (1 through 5) to which this billing rule applies, if any. If employees are paid during this period, the system assesses a fee. If you enter a Deduction Period, you must also enter a Pay Group unless Based On is set to Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class. | | Position | Enter the Position to restrict the billing rule to one position. For example, SALES is a position type. | | Obsolete | Enable this field to set the selected Billing Rule as obsolete. (The default is disabled.) | | Obsolete Date | Enter or select the date when the Billing Rule becomes obsolete. (If you enable the Obsolete field and do not enter or select the Obsolete Date, the system automatically defaults to the current date.) | | Pay Period | Select the Pay Period to which this rule applies. If it applies to all periods, do not make a selection. | | Home Location Code | Enter a Home Location Code to limit the application of this unbundled billing rule to that location. If the rule applies to all locations by default, enter DEFAULT-ALL. Using DEFAULT-ALL for a billing rule allows locations to have “by exception” logic. For example, you could have a rule where all locations are charged $100 except for the CORP location, which is charged $40. In that case, you would create two billing rules: one with the DEFAULT-ALL location code and one with the CORP location code. | | Special Rate | Enter the Special Rate in effect for a specified period. The system uses this value instead of the standard rate. Note: You cannot enter a special rate for Per Active Employee Per Month (PAEPM) rules. | | Special Rate Start/Special Rate End | If the special rate only applies during a specific period: - Enter the Special Rate Start date when the special rate goes into effect. - Enter the Special Rate End date when the special rate ends. Note: You cannot enter a special rate start/end date for Per Active Employee Per Month (PAEPM) rules. |
  • Enter the Special Rate Start date when the special rate goes into effect.
  • Enter the Special Rate End date when the special rate ends. | Billing Code | Enter the Billing Code the system uses. Typically, this is 006, but you can assign another code to use instead of this standard administration fee. | | Benefit Plan | Enter the Benefit Plan to use when calculating an administrative fee. This field only impacts calculations when the rule’s Based On field is set to Percent of Listed Benefit or Percent of Listed Benefit Net Billed to Client. | | Optional Fields | | | Benefit Plan Class | This field is only active when Based On is set to Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class. Select one of the classifications used for group benefit plans or select Retirement. The system charges the amount once if an employee has any plans in that class (unless the group benefit plan is set to suppress flat billing by plan type). If there is a benefit adjustment in addition to the regular plan, the billing rule is applied only once. If the pay voucher has only benefit adjustments, then the billing rule applies once. | | Benefit Plan Types | You can only specify benefit plan types on which to charge fees when Based On is set to Percent of Listed Benefit, Percent of Listed Benefit Net Billed to Client, or Flat Amount based on Benefit Rule. Select each Benefit Plan Type Equal To for which you bill an administrative fee surcharge, or select each Benefit Plan Type Not Equal To for which you do not bill an administrative fee surcharge. | | Pay Class Equal To/ Pay Class Not Equal To | If the billing rule applies to only a few pay classes, list them in the Pay Class Equal To table. If there are only a few exceptions, use the Pay Class Not Equal To table instead. If the billing rule covers all pay classes, leave both tables empty. For example, set Pay Class Not Equal ToExpense Reimbursement when you do not want to calculate Admin Fee as a percentage of wages on non-taxable wages. Important! When listing the Pay Class, note that they are only valid when using the following Based On options: - Hours Worked - Percent of Wages - Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire) - Flat Amount - Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE) - Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class - Monthly Rate/52 weeks = Only One of these - Monthly Rate/48 weeks =/ Per Voucher will Fire - Percent of Retirement Match Amount - Percent to Not Shelter Benefit plan from FICA - Percent of WorkerComp Billed - Percent of FUTA Billed - Percent of FUTA Wages Billed - Percent of SUTA Billed - Percent of SUTA Wages Billed - Tax-Code Based Rule (Rate(s) on sub screen) - Tiered Cumulative Per Employee Per Pay Period - Tiered Per Employee Per Pay Period |
  • Hours Worked
  • Percent of Wages
  • Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire)
  • Flat Amount
  • Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE)
  • Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class
  • Monthly Rate/52 weeks = Only One of these
  • Monthly Rate/48 weeks =/ Per Voucher will Fire
  • Percent of Retirement Match Amount
  • Percent to Not Shelter Benefit plan from FICA
  • Percent of WorkerComp Billed
  • Percent of FUTA Billed
  • Percent of FUTA Wages Billed
  • Percent of SUTA Billed
  • Percent of SUTA Wages Billed
  • Tax-Code Based Rule (Rate(s) on sub screen)
  • Tiered Cumulative Per Employee Per Pay Period
  • Tiered Per Employee Per Pay Period | Pay Code Equal To/ Pay Code Not Equal To | If the billing rule applies to only a few pay codes, list them in the Pay Code Equal To table. If there are a few pay code exceptions, use the Pay Code Not Equal To table to list the pay codes not covered by this billing rule. If the billing rule covers all pay codes, leave both tables empty. Important! When listing the Pay Code, note that they are only valid when using the following Based On options: - Hours Worked - Percent of Wages - Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire) - Flat Amount - Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE) - Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class - Monthly Rate/52 weeks = Only One of these - Monthly Rate/48 weeks =/ Per Voucher will Fire - Percent of Retirement Match Amount - Percent to Not Shelter Benefit plan from FICA - Percent of WorkerComp Billed - Percent of FUTA Billed - Percent of FUTA Wages Billed - Percent of SUTA Billed - Percent of SUTA Wages Billed - Tax-Code Based Rule (Rate(s) on sub screen) - Tiered Cumulative Per Employee Per Pay Period - Tiered Per Employee Per Pay Period |

  1. Click Save.

Tiered Billing Grid

Certain billing methods involve setting up different bill rates based on the number of employees in a given group. The tiered billing grid displays when this is the case.

[Image: screenshot]

The table provides three columns:

  • Tiered From and Tiered To — the range of employees (for example, 1 to 20) in a particular tier

  • Tiered Rate — amount to bill per employee in this tier

For example, if the grid represents administrative fees, you can charge a $40 fee per employee for the first 20 employees, a $20 fee for the next 20 employees, and so on.

Setting Up Other Unbundled Billing Rule Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Addtl_Conds.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Addtl_Conds.htm

Setting Up Other Unbundled Billing Rule Parameters

Set up additional unbundled billing rule parameters with the Additional Conditions form.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Billing Rules (Unbundled).

  3. From the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , select Additional Conditions.

  4. Fill in the fields as required.

Field Description
Select the option as appropriate for ACH For ACH (applicable only if the unbundled billing rule is based on a flat amount): - Use If Any ACH: Apply this billing rule on a voucher with ACH. - Use Per ACH Line: Charge a fee for each ACH line. - Use If Electronic Stub: Applies only to electronic pay stubs.
  • Use If Any ACH: Apply this billing rule on a voucher with ACH.
  • Use Per ACH Line: Charge a fee for each ACH line.
  • Use If Electronic Stub: Applies only to electronic pay stubs. | Select the option as appropriate for garnishments | For garnishments: - Use If Any Garnish: Apply this rule if there are any garnishments. - Use Per Garn Item: Charge a fee for each garnishment. |
  • Use If Any Garnish: Apply this rule if there are any garnishments.
  • Use Per Garn Item: Charge a fee for each garnishment. | Use If Any Check | Select Use If Any Check to apply the rule for any pay voucher. | | Exclude From Manual Check | Select Exclude From Manual Check to suppress the billing (delivery fee) for a manual check that occurs when a billing rule is processing live checks. Note: This field only displays if you select Use If Any Check and one of the following rules in the Based On field in the Billing Rules (Unbundled) form: - Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire) - Flat Amount - Flat Amount / Invoice - Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE) |
  • Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire)
  • Flat Amount
  • Flat Amount / Invoice
  • Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE) | Rule Minimum | Enter the Rule Minimum, which is the least amount this rule should bill. | | Rule Maximum | Enter the Rule Maximum, which is the dollar limit for this rule. | | Exclude from FPEMIN | Select Exclude From FPEMIN to exclude this rule from the Flat Amount/Per Employee Invoice Minimum billing rule. | | FSA Plan Type Equal To | Enter each FSA Plan Type Equal To that causes the system to apply this rule. The system applies the rule once for any of the plans. To associate a fee with each plan, create a separate rule. | | FSA Plan Type Not Equal To | Enter each FSA Plan Type Not Equal To that should not be present to cause the system to apply this rule. | | Retirement Plans Equal To | Enter each Retirement Plans Equal To that if found on the voucher applies this rule. | | Retirement Plans Not Equal To | Enter the Retirement Plans Not Equal To that if not used on the voucher applies this rule. | | State(s) Equal To | If you select the Use If Any Check option, you can enter a list of State(s) Equal to where this unbundled billing rule applies. |
  1. Click Close.

Using Unbundled Bill Rules Based On Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Based_On_Options.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Based_On_Options.htm

Using Unbundled Bill Rules Based On Options

You can use these options as the basis for calculating unbundled billing rules.

Note: Note the following:

  • If you define rules based on the Employee Minimums and Employee Maximum, the system uses only one rule per voucher.

  • If you define one rule with Invoice Minimum, and another with Invoice Maximum, the system uses only one rule per payroll.

  • If a Per Active Employee Per Month (PAEPM) rule is already active for a client, you cannot create another PAEPM rule for that client using the same rule dates. (A warning displays if you try to create another PAEPM rule using the same rule dates, which would make them active at the same time.)

Use the Based On options as follows:

Based On Calculation
Units Enter the flat amount to calculate per unit paid. For example, one unit of bonus pay with a Rate amount of 10 ($10.00) generates a $10.00 admin fee.
Hours Worked Enter the flat amount to calculate per hour worked. For example, 40 hours worked with a Rate amount of 10 ($10.00) generates a $400.00 admin fee.
Percent of Wages Enter the percent of wages to charge. For example, $1270.00 of gross wages with a Rate amount of 10 (10%) generates a $127.00 admin fee.
Amt per Check (One Per Voucher will Fire) Enter the flat amount to bill per check. For example, 100 vouchers with a Rate amount of 1 ($1.00) generates a $100.00 admin fee.
Flat Amount Bills the client this amount for each employee and each check. For example, two employees and three total checks (one employee receives two checks) with a Rate amount of 10 ($10.00) generates a $30.00 Admin fee.
Flat Amount / Invoice Bills the client this amount per invoice generated. For example, two invoices and a Rate amount of 50 ($50.00) generates a $100.00 Admin fee.
Flat Amount / Paid EE (only once per EE) Triggers the flat amount only once per employee in the payroll batch. For example, if an employee receives a regular, supplemental, and vacation voucher on the same payroll, the billing rule triggers only once. If there were two employees and four total vouchers with a Rate amount of 10 ($10.00), the system generates a $20.00 Admin fee.
Flat Amount / Paid EE sets IMIN basis. (Must also have 0 IMIN) Determines an invoice minimum based on an amount per employee. For example, if the Rate amount is 7.5, then two employees, one with $7.00 admin fee and another with $10.00 admin fee would not trip the value of $15.00. (If you selected Based On Employee Minimum, the first employee would generate a $0.50 fee, and there would be no fee for the second employee.) > Note: Note the following: - If you define one rule with Monthly Rate/52 weeks, and another with Monthly Rate/48 weeks, the system uses only one per voucher. - The Flat Amount / Paid EE sets IMIN basis. (Must also have 0 IMIN) and Invoice Minimum = Only One of these options must be the last billing rules that the system calculates. If either exists as a Billing Rule, the system will resort the rules so they calculate last.
  • If you define one rule with Monthly Rate/52 weeks, and another with Monthly Rate/48 weeks, the system uses only one per voucher.
  • The Flat Amount / Paid EE sets IMIN basis. (Must also have 0 IMIN) and Invoice Minimum = Only One of these options must be the last billing rules that the system calculates. If either exists as a Billing Rule, the system will resort the rules so they calculate last. | Flat Amount / Benefit Plan Class | Triggers the flat amount based on the classification selected from the Benefit Plan Class drop-down. | | Monthly Rate / 52 Weeks | Enter the monthly rate to charge; the system divides it by pay period based on 52 weeks a year per employee. For example, if the Rate amount is 65, the system bills the client $15.00 per employee for a weekly payroll. This does not limit the billing to $65.00 per month. If an employee receives multiple vouchers and the voucher type is not limited, more than $65.00 can be charged in a month. | | Monthly Rate / 48 Weeks | Enter the monthly rate to charge; the system divides it by pay period based on 48 weeks a year per employee. For example, if the Rate amount is 65, the system bills the client $16.25 per employee for a weekly payroll. | | Employee Minimum | Bills the minimum amount on each voucher for each employee. For example, if the rule has a Rate amount of 25 and an employee receives three vouchers (regular, supplemental, and vacation), the system bills the client $75 for that employee. | | Employee Minimum, NO Credit On Overbills | For example, if the employee minimum is $10.00, but a client pays only $7.50 because they had $2.50 in overlimit credits, the system assesses a $2.50 charge. (Employee Minimum would not assess a fee.) | | Employee Maximum | The maximum per voucher per employee that will be billed. For example; a user could set up one rule as a percent of wages and a second rule with an employee maximum, and the system would limit the calculation to the employee maximum. If rule #1 is set up as 20% of wages, and the wages are $2000, then rule #1 calculates at $400. If rule #2 is set up with an employee maximum of $25, then the system limits the admin fee to $25 even though rule #1 was calculated at $400. | | Invoice Minimum | Any admin fee below this amount increases to this amount. | | Invoice Maximum | Any admin fee above this amount reduces to this amount. | | Invoice Minimum (incl Zero Invoice) | Can be used in conjunction with Invoice Minimum. | | Per Employee Per Month | Bills automatically the first payroll after the billing rule is set up and automatically bills on the first payroll of each month following. It only bills for employees that are paid within that pay period. - If there is a new hire on the next payroll, the system will bill for just that employee. - If an employee was not paid on the first payroll of the month but gets paid on the next payroll, the system will bill for just that employee. |
  • If there is a new hire on the next payroll, the system will bill for just that employee.
  • If an employee was not paid on the first payroll of the month but gets paid on the next payroll, the system will bill for just that employee. | Percent of Listed Benefit | Takes the benefit entered in Benefit Plan and charges this percent against the total amount. For example, 10% of a $1000 benefit (with employee paying $500) would be $100. | | Percent of Listed Benefit Net Billed to Client | Takes the benefit listed and charges this percent against the net amount. For example, 10% of a $1000 benefit (with employee paying $500) would be $50. | | Percent of Retirement Match Amount | Charges a percentage of the Match Fees. | | Percent to Not Shelter Benefit Plan from FICA | Percentage that will not be sheltered as a benefit from FICA. A sheltered amount shelters the whole amount from FICA for billing purposes, so use this option when you want to pass some of the savings through, but not all of them. | | Flat Amount Based on Benefit Rule | Charges a flat amount once per Benefit Plan. | | Percent of WorkerComp Billed | Percentage of billed workers’ compensation amount. | | Percent of FUTA Billed | Percentage of billed amount for FUTA. | | Percent of FUTA Wages Billed | Percentage of billed FUTA wages. | | Percent of SUTA Billed | Percentage of billed SUTA amount. | | Percent of SUTA Wages Billed | Percentage of billed SUTA wages. | | Percent of Tip Tax Credit Over Min | Uses special calculation | | Tax-Code Based Rule | This option requires you to use the Tax Criteria panel to complete the calculations. The system ignores the Rate defined in the Unbundled Billing Rules panel. | | Tiered Cumulative Per Employee Per Pay Period | This option calculates the admin fee based on the client’s employee count, when multiple pay groups exist in the batch. The fee is based on thetotal number of active employees in the client associated with those pay groups, even if those employees are not in the pay groups themselves. This feature uses a special tier table with three columns: - Tiered From (smallest number of employees in this tier) - Tiered To (largest number of employees in this tier) - Tiered Rate (admin fee to charge per employee in this tier) Add a row to the table for each calculation tier. |
  • Tiered From (smallest number of employees in this tier)
  • Tiered To (largest number of employees in this tier)
  • Tiered Rate (admin fee to charge per employee in this tier) | Tiered Cumulative Per Employee | This is custom programming that calculates the admin fee based on the employee count from two different pay groups. The fee is based on the number of active employees in each group. This feature uses the same tier table described in the row above. | | Tiered Per Employee Per Pay Period | Use this method to bill the same administrative fees for all employees based on the total number of employees. Note: Pay Group is the only filter criteria available for the Tiered Per Employee Per Pay Period option. (The system does not recognize any other filters when using this option.) This feature uses a special tier table with three columns: - Tiered From: Smallest number of employees in this tier - Tiered To: Largest number of employees in this tier - Tiered Rate: Admin fee to charge per employee in this tier Add a row to the table for each calculation tier. |
  • Tiered From: Smallest number of employees in this tier
  • Tiered To: Largest number of employees in this tier
  • Tiered Rate: Admin fee to charge per employee in this tier | Flat Amt based on Count | Use this method to charge a flat amount per voucher. Specify the rule limiters in the Additional Conditions form’s Rule Minimum and Rule Maximum fields. For example, you need to bill the client at two different rates, based on the number of employees. You would establish two rules using Flat Amt based on Count. Set up the first rule with a Rate of $35, the Rule Minimum set to 0, and the Rule Maximum set to 5. Set up the second rule with a Rate of $100, a Rule Minimum of 6, and a Rule Maximum of 99. When there are five employees or fewer in the batch control record, PrismHR uses the first rule: four employee result in a bill for $140. For a batch with 6-99 employees, the system uses the second rule: six employees result in a bill for $600. | | Flat Amt based on Count Per Tier | Use this method to charge the client a certain amount for the first X number of vouchers and then a different amount for the next X number of vouchers. Specify the tiers for the rule in the Additional Conditions form’s Rule Minimum and Rule Maximum fields. For example, you need to bill one rate for the first five employees, and then at another rate for every employee after that. You would establish two rules using Flat Amt based on Count Per Tier. Set up the first rule with the Rate of $35, the Rule Minimum set to 0, and the Rule Maximum set to 5. Set up the second rule with a Rate of $100, with a Rule Minimum of 6, and a Rule Maximum of 99. The system calculates the first five employees using the first rule, and the sixth employee using the second rule, resulting in a bill for $275 ((5$35)+(1$100)). | | Annual W-2 Production Fee | Use this method to generate a one-time yearly charge for Forms W-2 (W-2s) as a production fee. This is a one-time flat fee that the system will bill for the current year the first time the rule applies. If your organization would prefer that it run in January, set the Rule Start Date to a date in January of the next year. This rule generates a miscellaneous billing charge. | | Annual Fee per W-2 | Use this method to generate a one-time yearly charge for W-2s as a per-employee fee. This billing rule is based on a count of generated W‑2s. W‑2s are generated in the first quarter of the following year (for example, your organization will generate 2024 W‑2s in Q1 2025). If W‑2s have not been generated yet (such as in early January), then the system processes the rule during each payroll until your organization generates the W‑2s. This rule generates a miscellaneous billing charge. | | Minimum Bill for Code 005 | Any amount below this value increases to this value. | | Per Active Employee Per Month | This option calculates the admin fee based on the client’s employee count on the first regular payroll of the month, using a tiered charge format. (The second payroll captures any new employees.) The fee is based on thetotal number of active employees in the client associated with the first payroll of the month. This feature uses a special tier table with three columns: - Tiered From (smallest number of employees in this tier) - Tiered To (largest number of employees in this tier) - Tiered Rate (admin fee to charge per employee in this tier) Add a row to the table for each calculation tier. Enabling the Per Active Employee Per Month option bills active employees for the month. For example, if the first payroll of the month has 100 active employees, they will be billed for 100 employees. On the second payroll if there is a new hire and a termination, they will still be billed for one employee on that payroll, since the new hire needs to be billed and the termination was active for part of the month. Note: An edge case could occur that is currently not supported. When you calculate a payroll, the system determines the number of employees that are active at that time and once the payroll is posted, it commits counts to the employee records. If you calculate the payroll and add several employees or terminate employees and then post without recalculating, the system misses the new employees added/removed in the billing cycle. > Note: Note the following: - The billing rate calculates the tier rate for the employee count. - The admin fee bills the entire month at the same rate. |
  • Tiered From (smallest number of employees in this tier)
  • Tiered To (largest number of employees in this tier)
  • Tiered Rate (admin fee to charge per employee in this tier)
  • The billing rate calculates the tier rate for the employee count.
  • The admin fee bills the entire month at the same rate. Related Topics

Entering Tax Criteria

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Tax_Criteria.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Billing_Rules_Unbundled_Tax_Criteria.htm

Entering Tax Criteria

Enter tax criteria by setting Based On in unbundled billing to Tax-Code Based Rule. The Tax Criteria panel displays.

[Image: Tax Criteria panel, featuring the EE and ER Tax Codes grids]

  1. Go to the Tax Criteria panel and complete the following fields for the employee tax criteria:

Field — Description

EE Tax Codes — Enter each of the EE Tax Codes (employee tax deduction codes) to associate with this billing rule.

Per Employee — Indicate whether to charge the code Per Employee. If not, then the system assesses the charge once per payroll batch.

Bill Rate — Enter the Bill Rate to charge on the code.

F/P — Indicate whether the amount is a Flat Rate or a Percent.

  1. Complete the following fields for the employer tax criteria:

Field — Description

ER Tax Codes — Enter each ER Tax Codes (employer tax deduction codes) to associate with this billing rule.

Per Employee — Indicate whether to charge the code Per Employee. If not, then the system assesses the charge once per payroll batch.

Bill Rate — Enter the Bill Rate to charge on the code.

F/P — Indicate whether the amount is a Flat Rate or a Percent.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Certified Payroll Report Format Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Certified_Payroll_Report_Parameters.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Certified_Payroll_Report_Parameters.htm

Defining Certified Payroll Report Format Parameters

You can set up the Certified Payroll Report to automatically run with payroll. Use the Certified Payroll Report Format Parameters form to define the format parameters for this report.

Note: Note: This report executes only for regularly scheduled, weekly payrolls (AUTORUN version), and displays information about wages and payroll for all employees. It does not execute for the ON-DEMAND version. (To run that version of the report, see Certified Payroll Report.)

To define the Certified Payroll Report format parameters:

  1. Go to the Certified Payroll Report Format Parameters form and complete the following fields.

Field Name — Description

Format — Select the Format from the drop-down. Note: Currently, this report only supports the Federal Government version.

Include Gender on the Report — Select Include Gender on the Report, if needed.

Include Ethnicity — Select Include Ethnicity, if needed.

Include Employee Address — Select Include Employee Address, if needed.

Employee Identifier — Select an Employee Identifier you want to use in the report: (Full Social Security Number, Last Four Digits of Social, Employee ID, or None).

Show zero work weeks — Select this field to include projects that did not have any work to display in the Certified Payroll Report, essentially providing you with a zero report (all amounts listed in the report display as zero). If you do not select this field and there was no project work during the requested date, an error message displays.

Show extended rate of pay up to 4 decimal places — Select this field to display the pay rate in the same format that displays on vouchers, which display up to four decimal places.

Allocate Deductions/Taxes by Project — Select Allocate Deductions/Taxes by Project to calculate deductions and taxes. Selecting this field sets the calculation to allocate by project total. When unchecked, the allocation is based on gross pay.

  1. Click Save.

Change Reason History- Client Details

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Change-Reason-History-Client_Details.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Change-Reason-History-Client_Details.htm

Change Reason History- Client Details

Use this form to get the change reason history for the Client Details form.

Note: Note: This form will be able to capture the change reason history only if the field named Require change reason on critical forms on the System Parameters form is either selected as Optional (and the user has entered a reason for record update) or Required. When you make any change to the Client Details form, you will be presented with the following dialog box. Once you record the reason for the change herein, the same will be presented in the Change Reason History form.

[Image: screenshot]

To access the form, under the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

on the Client Details form, click Change Reason History.

On this form the following two fields are available:

  • Change Date on or after – This is the start date of the date range. By default, this will be 60 days prior to today’s date. You can change this date according to your required date range.

  • Change Date on or before – This is the end data of the date range. By default, this will be today’s date. You can change this date according to your required date range.

Click Close to close this form and return to the base form.

Changing SUTA Flags on Payroll Vouchers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Change-SUTA-Flag-Vouchers.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Change-SUTA-Flag-Vouchers.htm

Changing SUTA Flags on Payroll Vouchers

Run this process to update previously existing pay vouchers and change SUTA Reporting Entity from Client to PEO (or vice versa). You must run a tax register rebuild to ensure that subsequent payrolls are correct. Once this process is complete, an acknowledgment message is sent out stating the number of updated vouchers. Acknowledgment of this message generates a report showing the vouchers that were edited.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To change SUTA flags on vouchers:

  1. Click Back Office [Image: screenshot] .

  2. From Employer|Action, select Change SUTA Flag on Vouchers (PEO/Client).

  3. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Tax Codes/Tax Description Select the tax deduction code you want to update. This code must be a SUTA code. Once a code is selected, it fills in the description field.
Reporting Select PEO or Client to determine a report type. Vouchers are changed to this reporting type for the value selected here.
Special Option Determines how individual tax is accumulated and reported. Options are: - A - Auto-assigns state unemployment insurance (SUI) based on the current configuration. - C - Forces Client SUI Reporting Only Basis.
Note: Values entered in Reporting and Special Option determine the method used by the system to update the Entity and Client Reporting Only fields on the Employer Payroll Tax Detail record. \ Update SUTA Reporting On Payroll Vouchers Record Fields \ Update Methodology \ \ \ — \ — \ — \ \ PEO/Client Reporting \ Special Option \ \ \ P \ Blank \ Entity = P, Client Reporting Only = N \ \ C \ Blank \ Entity = C, Client Reporting Only = null \ \ P \ C \ Entity = P, Client Reporting Only = Y \ \ P or C \ A \ The update utility uses the same method (UTL.GET.SUTA.BASIS) that runs during the payroll initialization process. This method looks at: 1. The Service Type field on the Client Details record. 2. The Tax Basis parameters on the State Processing rule record for the applicable state. 3. The Client SUI reporting fields on the Client Details form that override the settings on the State record to determine what the values of the ‘Entity’ and ‘Client Reporting Only’ field will be set to. Since the Service Type field on the Client Details record affects the outcome when this special option is used, the option to override the service type for the Client ID entered is provided. This option is only available when a Client ID is entered. It is meant to be used when a client’s service type has changed and you want the update program to use a service other than the one currently found on the Client Details record for the client entered. \
Update SUTA Reporting On Payroll Vouchers Record Fields Update Methodology
PEO/Client Reporting Special Option
P Blank Entity = P, Client Reporting Only = N
C Blank Entity = C, Client Reporting Only = null
P C Entity = P, Client Reporting Only = Y
P or C A The update utility uses the same method (UTL.GET.SUTA.BASIS) that runs during the payroll initialization process. This method looks at: 1. The Service Type field on the Client Details record. 2. The Tax Basis parameters on the State Processing rule record for the applicable state. 3. The Client SUI reporting fields on the Client Details form that override the settings on the State record to determine what the values of the ‘Entity’ and ‘Client Reporting Only’ field will be set to. Since the Service Type field on the Client Details record affects the outcome when this special option is used, the option to override the service type for the Client ID entered is provided. This option is only available when a Client ID is entered. It is meant to be used when a client’s service type has changed and you want the update program to use a service other than the one currently found on the Client Details record for the client entered.
Start Pay Date/End Pay Date Start: The date to start updating payroll vouchers. End: The date to stop updating payroll vouchers.
Employer ID Optional: Changes SUTA for a specific employer.
Client ID Optional: Changes SUTA for a specific client.
Employee ID Optional: Changes SUTA for a specific employee.
Override service type Forces the client service type used when the service type for the client has changed (Special Option set to A). Options are: - ASO - Administrative Services Only - PEO - Professional Employer Services - PAY - Processing Payroll Only - ASA - Administrative Services/Alt. Billing - COR - Corporate Payroll - HRN - HR Services - No Payroll
File Type Determines the output type for reports: Text only, or CSV.
Rebuild Vouchers Flag Whether to Update Existing Vouchers with the new SUTA flag information, or Create New Adjustment Vouchers to reflect the change.
Send to Mastertax This field is only available if Rebuild Vouchers Flag is set to “Create New Adjustment Vouchers”. If Yes, the MasterTax PTS file will display the wages and taxes associated with the original SUTA flag as negative values. The PTS file also displays the updated amounts as positive values associated with the new SUTA flag.
  1. Click Run. Here is a report example in .txt format. [Image: screenshot]

Setting Check Journal Report Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Check_Journal_Report_Parameters.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Check_Journal_Report_Parameters.htm

Setting Check Journal Report Parameters

Use the Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters form to determine the format for the report during payroll processing.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

[Image: Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters]

To set the Check Journal Report parameters:

  1. In the Sort Parameters panel, set the Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary Sort By categories.

  2. In the Other Parameters panel, select the appropriate settings to include in or exclude from the report:

Select this field: — To do the following:

Display Pay Codes Using Timesheet Detail Format — Uses the same detail as the time sheet and voucher

Include Expense Reimbursements — Includes expense reimbursement pay codes

Include Non-Cash Pay Codes — Includes non-cash as well as cash pay codes

Include ‘Employee Total Expense’ on Report — Includes the Employee Total Expense, which is the sum of all pay and contributions

Sort on Codes instead of Descriptions — Sorts by code rather than description

Suppress Employee Names on the Report — Excludes employee sort names from the report

Consolidate Report Totals by Pay Code Only — Displays the totals by pay code, rather than detail lines

Exclude Check Detail Information — Excludes detailed check information (check number, check date, and period start and end dates), allowing users to see more employees per page

Display Zero Hours/Units For Fringe Pay Codes — Displays zero hours or units for fringe pay codes

Split Hours and Units Columns — Enters a page break on the primary sort field

Generate Page Break — Enters a page break on the primary sort field

  1. Select the Report Type.

  2. Select the Report Format.

  3. Click Save.

Configuring Client-specific Global PTO Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client%20Global%20PTO%20Maintenance.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client Global PTO Maintenance.htm

Configuring Client-specific Global PTO Parameters

Use the Client Global PTO Maintenance form to view and configure client-specific Global PTO parameters.

To access this form, click Client. Under Client|Change, click Client Global PTO Maintenance.

To configure client-specific Global PTO parameters:

    1. Complete the following fields:

Field Name — Description

Excluded Plans by State — This field is optional. This form provides the ability to exclude full states from the Global PTO enrollment and accrual process. This option is intended for clients who may be required to provide State Sick Leave in certain states but not all. If you want to, you can now exclude certain Global PTO Benefit Plans from the enrollment and accrual once the client is enabled for Global PTO. - State - You can enter the two digit state codes manually or you can click the State hyperlink and select them from the Select States (permitted list of States Code records configured on the State Rules form) dialog box. You cannot select a state here that is already listed in the Global PTO Plan Overrides section below. - Description - This field automatically populates the state name once the State field is populated.

Excluded Plans by ID — This field is optional. If multiple Global PTO Benefit plans exist on the State Rules form for a state, and you need to prevent enrollment and accrual in one or more plans for that state for the client, you can enter the specific Global PTO Benefit Plan IDs here. If a client has Global PTO Benefit Plan IDs configured under this field, then the system ignores creation and/or accrual of any Global PTO plan(s) identified in this field. - Exclude Plans by ID - You can enter the Global PTO Benefit Plan IDs manually or you can click the Exclude Plans by ID hyperlink and select them from the Select Global PTO Benefit Plans dialog box. You cannot select a state here that has already listed having Global PTO Plan Overrides. - Description - This field automatically populates the Global PTO Benefit Plan description once the Exclude Plans by ID field is populated.

Global PTO Plan Overrides — When Global PTO Plan Override rules are configured, this section will display all existing Global PTO override rules for the client. You can click on the number hyperlink under the “Line #” column to open the Global PTO Client Override form to view the override rules for that state. You can make changes to the override rules on that form and save the changes which will reflect here upon editing. The following columns display for this section: - Line # - State - Plan ID - I/E - Employee Type - I/E - Pay Method - Override Plan ID

Sort By — This field is drop-down. You can select the sort order for the list above by any one of the following options: - Order Applied (Default) - State - Plan ID - Employee Type - Pay Method - Override Plan ID

  1. Click Save to save the changes.

  2. Click New Rule to open the Global PTO Client Override form. (See Configuring Client-specific Global PTO Override Rules.)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Account.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Account.htm

Maintaining Client Contact and Account Assignment Information

You can set contact and account assignment information with the Account tab on the Client Details form.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.
  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.
  3. Click the Account tab.

Adding and Editing Contact Information

Specify new contacts and edit existing contacts in the Contact Information panel:

  • Add contact information: Select an existing contact name, then click New in the Contacts form. Complete the required fields and click Save.

  • Edit contact information: Change existing information by selecting a contact name and updating the required fields on the Contacts form, then click Save.

Designating Employee Representatives

Specify employee representatives for each account type (for example, Payroll) with the Account Assignments panel.

Note: Note: These employee representatives do not influence the approval process. Users should contact them for informational purposes only.

  1. Enter a user ID or click the field name and select an ID from the Search users list to assign the account representatives for each role. The users must have the appropriate account management role assigned to their User account.

Note: You can only enter service providers in the Payroll, Human Resources, Benefits, Risk, Sales, Coordinator, Broker, Print/Mail User, and Backup Assignments employee representative account type fields. (The Print/Mail User field only displays when the client’s global configuration is set to Separate Print/Ship Functions.)

  1. Click Save.

Specifying a Mailing Address

Set a company’s mailing address with the Mailing Address panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Address, City, State — Enter the company’s mailing Address. Enter a company’s mailing address and business location (city and state).

ZIP Code — Enter a company’s zip code. Use the ZIP+4 format if required.

Bill using this Client’s Address — Send a company’s billing to the address of another company by entering the recipient company ID in this field.

  1. Click Save.

Customizing My Support Team Dashboard Contacts

Assign contacts that display on the My Support Team Dashboard with the Customize My Support Team Dashboard panel.

Enter the contact name or click the field name and select a name from the Search users list that displays.

Note: To display the Customize My Support Team Dashboard panel, you must first enable the DASH_SUPPORT_TEAM_ADD custom feature code. After enabling this custom feature code, the Customizable My Support Team Fields panel displays on the System Parameters form where you can add a description for each custom field, which determines how these fields display on the Account tab. (See the PrismHRSystem Administration Guide.)

Establishing Bank Accounts

Specify bank account information for a client with the Bank Accounts panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Payroll Check Account — Enter or select information for the account payroll checks are drawn from.

Direct Deposit Account — Enter or select information for the account direct deposit (ACH) amounts are drawn from. If left blank, the Payroll Check Account is used.

Garnishment Check Account — Enter or select information for the account wage garnishments for the employees of a company are drawn from.

Manual Checks Account — Enter or select the account manual checks are drawn from. This field is required if manual checks are entered with the Pay Check Calculator form.

On Demand Checks Account — Enter or select the account that manual checks are drawn from for On Demand Check processing. (See Processing On Demand Checks.)

Reverse Wire Account — Enter the checking account number used with the reverse wire transfer. Important! The reverse wire process does not work for internal processing. (You cannot make an entry in the Reverse Wire Account field if the active client is set up for internal ACH processing on the System Parameters form.) Note: The system validates this checking account, so you cannot enter an account that is linked to an ACH originator set up for a reverse wire transfer in any other bank account fields, except for the reverse wire transfer account. After entering the checking account number a warning displays, “Warning, you must make arrangements with the bank and with the client before setting up a reverse-wire-transfer.” Click OK to continue, then perform the tasks listed in the warning.

Child Support EFT Account — Enter or select information for the account child support garnishments are electronically drawn from.

Receipts Account — Enter or select information for the account used for ACH or Credit Card receipts.

Third Party Check Account — Enter or select information for the account third party checks are drawn from.

Pay Card Account — Enter or select information for the account used to pay cards/debit cards.

  1. Click Save.

Adding Auto Holiday Pay

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Auto_Holiday_Pay.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Auto_Holiday_Pay.htm

Adding Auto Holiday Pay

You can use the Auto Holiday Pay form to streamline paying employee holiday time by setting up holiday rules, so your employees are paid this time at the client level. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • This form is only available for service providers.

  • The Override Date column indicates when the Auto Holiday Pay Rules for that Line # has at least one entry in the Holiday Dates panel in the Auto Holiday Pay Days form.

Important! An employee’s eligibility for auto holiday hours is based on the first date of the payroll’s pay period when the timesheet/payroll control record is created without regard to effective-dated changes that might fall within the pay period.

Using the Auto Holiday Pay form, you can set up the following:

  • New Rule: Define the rule which determines the amount of automatic holiday pay for employees and how many hours they receive using the Auto Holiday Rule Maintenance form. In addition, you can edit an existing rule to make updates. (See Maintaining Auto Holiday Pay Rules.)

Important! When you add a new rule and apply it to a current, regular payroll, if you delete the payroll and recreate it to populate those holiday hours, the system does not populate the holiday hours if you select Yes when this message displays, “Do you want to keep the keyed entries and apply them to the new payroll? Note: This will include all previously accepted pay rate changes and leave requests.”

Note: Note the following when creating a rule:

  • The FLSA Type is required for every rule.

  • Holiday rules only apply to regular payrolls and when the timesheet is created for a regular payroll. (The system does not apply holiday rules to payrolls marked as “replaces a scheduled batch.”)

  • Days: Define which days apply to employee holiday time. (See Working with Auto Holiday Pay Days.)

  • Import Rules: Import existing import rules using the Auto Holiday Pay Rules import template in the Data Import Tool. (See Importing Auto Holiday Pay Rules.)

Setting Up Auto Holiday Pay

To set up paying employee holiday time:

  1. Select the client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Auto Holiday Pay. The Auto Holiday Pay form displays.

  3. Click New Rule. The Auto Holiday Pay Rule Maintenance form displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter or select the parameters to define which employees receive the holiday, then complete the rest of the fields as necessary.

Note: Note the following:

  • If you enter an Employee Type (optional) that does not exist the system displays an error message, “Record <employee type> is not on file EMPLOYEE.TYPE.CODES.” Click OK to continue.

  • The Variable Salary option only displays in the Pay Method field if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form. You can use the Variable Salary option to accurately track the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work. For example, non-profit companies could use this option to track the work hours of their employees who are paid a standard salaried amount, so they can use these hours and their allocations for grant purposes.

  • If you enter a Pay Code (required) that does not exist the system displays an error message, “Record <pay code> is not on file EARNING.CODES.” Click OK to continue.

  • If you enter an alpha character as the Number of Hours (required) an error message displays, “A valid number is required.” Click OK to continue.

  • If you save a rule without entering the required information, an error message displays.

  • You can enter or select the Override Dates based on information that exists on the Auto Holiday Pay Days form. (You cannot duplicate override dates.)

Note: The Auto Holiday Override Hours display on the employee’s timesheet when the criteria for the Auto Holiday Pay Rule and the Override Date exists in the current regular payroll after it is created.

  1. Click Save. The rule displays on the Auto Holiday Pay form.

Note: After you create the rule, the system adds the holiday hours from the rule on that day into the timesheet when you create the payroll. In addition, you can edit or delete the rule by clicking the Line #.

  1. Click Days to define the days that apply to the rule. The Auto Holiday Pay Days form displays.

Note: You can also import auto holiday pay days to reduce the amount of manual work required. (See Importing Auto Holiday Pay Days.)

  1. Click Import Rules to import existing import rules for different clients using the Auto Holiday Pay Rules import template in the Data Import Tool. (See Importing Auto Holiday Pay Rules.)

Note: To sort the resulting information, select an option in the Sort By field, then click Save to save the sort updates. However, the option you select in the Sort By field always reverts back to Order Applied the next time you access this form. (The other Sort By options are for viewing purposes only.)

Removing Auto Holiday Pay Time

If an employee no longer receives auto holiday pay due to a change in their Employee Type, Pay Method, or FLSA Exempt status, there are two options available to remove any existing auto holiday pay from the current timesheet:

  • Option 1: Delete the Payroll Control record. Select Yes to “Do you want to create a replacement payroll for this pay group?” and select No to “Do you want to keep keyed entries and apply them to the new payroll?” The system reapplies the auto holiday pay rules.

  • Option 2: Manually delete the holiday hours for the employee in the timesheet.

Setting Commission Levels

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Commissions.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Commissions.htm

Setting Commission Levels

You can set commission levels for service provider salespeople that earn commissions based on client activity using the Commission Maintenance form.

Note: You can access this form by selecting Commissions on the Actions menu in the Client Details form.

You can enter more than one commission schedule for a salesperson, and those can be in effect during different time periods or simultaneously. For example, a salesperson can simultaneously receive commissions based on gross wages as well as active lives (active employees).

The system calculates commissions during the specified date range. If the period covered by the From Date and To Date for one or more salespeople overlap, the system computes all those schedules during that time period.

Note: Note the following:

  • When setting up commissions on the client level, you cannot set up both client and system-level plans on the same line. If you attempt this setup and click Accept, an error message displays.

  • If both a client-level and a system-level plan are applied, then the system calculates the commissions based on the system-level plan.

To set commission levels, do the following:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select Commissions from the Actions menu.

  4. Complete these fields as required in the Commission Maintenance panel.

Field Name Description
Salesperson In the Salesperson field, enter the user ID of an employee at your organization who is a sales representative associated with the client.
From Date Enter the From Date on which the commission level goes into effect.
To Date Enter the To Date on which the commission level expires.
There are two ways to calculate commissions, simple or complex. To calculate simple commissions:
Amount Enter the commission amount as a flat amount or a percentage.
Flat or Percent Indicate if the commission amount is Flat or Percent.
Based On Indicate what the commission is based on. The following Based On options work with the Percent option (see above): - Gross Wages: Total gross wages of the client payroll invoice. - Admin Fees: Total amount of administrative fees on the client payroll invoice. - Benefit Commission Type: Benefits-based commission for a specific benefit type. (You must enter the benefit type code in the Based on Item field.) - Benefit Commission Percentage: Percentage based on the benefit list. (You must enter the specific group benefit plan ID in the Based on Item field.) - Workers Comp: Total amount of workers’ compensation billed to the client for the payroll period. - Workers Compensation Profit: Checks bill code 005 and computes the profit, and also checks for information entered on losses. - Average Number Lives: Average number of active employees as of the period end date of the payroll (regardless of whether the employees were paid in the payroll batch). - Gross Profit: Sales items minus the direct cost items. The value is shown on the Profitability Report in the backend system. - Tax Profit: Sales of tax-based items (such as FUTA and SUTA) minus the cost for those tax items. Tax profit is rarely used for commissions because when not passing shelter credits it can often show a loss for the month. - Percent of Bill Code: Fixed percentage for the amount charged on a specific bill code. (You must enter the percentage in the Based on Item field.) - Profits to Admin (Enter Bill Code in Based On): The system uses the specified Bill Code and then subtracts the cost associated with that Bill Code. That amount (profit or loss) is used in the percent computation. For example, if you choose Bill Code 004 (SUTA) and specify 5% of profits, and there was $1000 in sales and $960 in costs, the payout would be $2.00 ($40.00 x 0.05). - Percent of Target Amount: Percent commission based on the amount set in the Target Amount field. (This option only works with the Percent commission amount option, see above. The Flat or Percent field automatically defaults to Percent.) The following Based On options work with the Flat option (see above): - Fixed Amount Group 1–5: Sets a flat amount that a user can elect to include when running Compute Commissions. - Fixed per Paid Employee: Flat commission based on the number of employees paid in the commission cycle. Note: If you select a Based On option that is invalid for the selected Flat or Percent option an error message displays, “<Based On Option> ‘based-on’ codes will not support <XXX> Amounts.” Click OK to continue.
  • Gross Wages: Total gross wages of the client payroll invoice.
  • Admin Fees: Total amount of administrative fees on the client payroll invoice.
  • Benefit Commission Type: Benefits-based commission for a specific benefit type. (You must enter the benefit type code in the Based on Item field.)
  • Benefit Commission Percentage: Percentage based on the benefit list. (You must enter the specific group benefit plan ID in the Based on Item field.)
  • Workers Comp: Total amount of workers’ compensation billed to the client for the payroll period.
  • Workers Compensation Profit: Checks bill code 005 and computes the profit, and also checks for information entered on losses.
  • Average Number Lives: Average number of active employees as of the period end date of the payroll (regardless of whether the employees were paid in the payroll batch).
  • Gross Profit: Sales items minus the direct cost items. The value is shown on the Profitability Report in the backend system.
  • Tax Profit: Sales of tax-based items (such as FUTA and SUTA) minus the cost for those tax items. Tax profit is rarely used for commissions because when not passing shelter credits it can often show a loss for the month.
  • Percent of Bill Code: Fixed percentage for the amount charged on a specific bill code. (You must enter the percentage in the Based on Item field.)
  • Profits to Admin (Enter Bill Code in Based On): The system uses the specified Bill Code and then subtracts the cost associated with that Bill Code. That amount (profit or loss) is used in the percent computation. For example, if you choose Bill Code 004 (SUTA) and specify 5% of profits, and there was $1000 in sales and $960 in costs, the payout would be $2.00 ($40.00 x 0.05).
  • Percent of Target Amount: Percent commission based on the amount set in the Target Amount field. (This option only works with the Percent commission amount option, see above. The Flat or Percent field automatically defaults to Percent.)
  • Fixed Amount Group 1–5: Sets a flat amount that a user can elect to include when running Compute Commissions.
  • Fixed per Paid Employee: Flat commission based on the number of employees paid in the commission cycle. | Based on Item | Enter additional based-on types to the commission rule at the client level, such as Percent of Bill Code, Profits to Admin, Workers Compensation Profit, Benefit Commission Type, and Benefit Commission Percentage. | | For complex commission structures, enter a Based on Plan, which is the ID of the commission plan. Do not use the Amount, Flat or Percent, and Based On fields. (See Creating Commission Structures.) | | | Target Amount | Enter the commission’s target amount, which is used in conjunction with the Percent of Target Amount option. (You can enter up to two decimals in this field. The system truncates values with more than two decimals.) The Target Amount field value is an overall dollar figure the system multiplies by the Amount Percent value to calculate the salesperson’s commission payout amount. For example, a Target Amount set to 100,000 for a 30% of Target Amount Commission setup would pay out $30,000 as the Commission amount for the defined commission period. The system calculates the total commission as the Target Amount x Amount (percentage) defined on the Commission Maintenance form, divided by the number of invoices in the applicable timeframe. The calculated commission is split across all invoices in the Compute Commissions period (Begin Date - End Date), which corresponds to the configuration dates on the Commission Maintenance form. Note: If the commission cannot be split evenly across all invoices, then the sum of all commission amounts across all invoices will still total the exact commission amount. |
  1. Complete these fields as required in the Target Maintenance panel.
Field Name Description
From Date Enter the From Date when the target amount goes into effect.
To Date Enter the To Datewhen the target amount expires.
Type Categorize the target by selecting one of the following options: - Annual Projection: - Annual Projection Adjustment: - Annual Projection Final: - Amortization: - Termination:
  • Annual Projection:
  • Annual Projection Adjustment:
  • Annual Projection Final:
  • Amortization:
  • Termination: | Target Amount | Enter the commission’s target amount, which is used in conjunction with the Percent of Target Amount option. (You can enter up to two decimals in this field. The system truncates values with more than two decimals.) |

  1. Click Accept.

  2. Click Save.
