Setting Client Control Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Controls.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Controls.htm

Setting Client Control Parameters

You can set operational parameters for a client using the Control tab settings on the Client Details form.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Click the Control tab.

Setting Payroll Processing Parameters

Set payroll processing parameters for a client with the Process Controls panel.

To set these parameters:

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Do Not Service Select to disable payroll processing.
Worksite Time Sheet Entry Select if users at the worksite enter time sheet information.
Processing Schedule Enter a payroll processing schedule for this client.
Worksite Payroll Approval Select one of the following options to determine the payroll approval process for the client: - Disabled: Select this option if you do not want the client to approve payrolls before the payroll is finalized. Important! If you disable the Worksite Payroll Approval field even though the Payroll Finalization Notification field is set to Email, the system stops sending finalization emails to the client. - Standard: Select this option if you want the client to perform a one-step payroll approval before the payroll is finalized (that is, after calculation). - Multistep: Select this option if you want the client to perform at least two payroll approval steps before the payroll is finalized (that is, after calculation). Note: When you select the Multistep option, the following message displays: “Approval Steps must be defined under Approval Policies to fully activate Multistep Approvals. If steps are not defined, this setting will work the same as Standard worksite payroll approval.” Click OK to continue. The following scenarios describe what happens when you enable the Multistep option: - After selecting the Multistep option and then clicking Save, the following message displays, “Approval Steps must be defined under Approval Policies to fully activate Multistep Approvals. Would you like to define them now?” The following happens depending on your answer: - Yes: The Approval Policies form automatically displays. - No: The save process continues. - After setting up payroll approval using the Multistep option and defining approval steps at the approval-policy level and then changing the Worksite Payroll Approval setting to either Disabled or Standard, when you click Save a message displays, “Changing the Worksite Payroll Approval Type will clear all previously defined approval steps for Multistep Payroll Approval. Do you wish to continue?” The following happens depending on your answer: - Yes: The system clears the client-level approval policy record for multistep payroll approval. - No: The system cancels this setup change and returns the Worksite Payroll Approval option to the original Multistep setting.
  • Disabled: Select this option if you do not want the client to approve payrolls before the payroll is finalized.
  • Standard: Select this option if you want the client to perform a one-step payroll approval before the payroll is finalized (that is, after calculation).
  • Multistep: Select this option if you want the client to perform at least two payroll approval steps before the payroll is finalized (that is, after calculation).
  • After selecting the Multistep option and then clicking Save, the following message displays, “Approval Steps must be defined under Approval Policies to fully activate Multistep Approvals. Would you like to define them now?” The following happens depending on your answer:
  • Yes: The Approval Policies form automatically displays.
  • No: The save process continues.
  • After setting up payroll approval using the Multistep option and defining approval steps at the approval-policy level and then changing the Worksite Payroll Approval setting to either Disabled or Standard, when you click Save a message displays, “Changing the Worksite Payroll Approval Type will clear all previously defined approval steps for Multistep Payroll Approval. Do you wish to continue?” The following happens depending on your answer:
  • Yes: The system clears the client-level approval policy record for multistep payroll approval.
  • No: The system cancels this setup change and returns the Worksite Payroll Approval option to the original Multistep setting. | Minimum Check Amount | Enter the amount that the system will validate for a check. During payroll initialization, the system flags any checks with a gross pay amount less than this value. | | | | Maximum Check Amount | Enter the amount that the system will validate for a check. During payroll initialization, the system flags any checks with a gross pay amount greater than this value. | | | | Suppress Arrearage Vouchers | Select to suppress the global creation of arrearage vouchers. This is a zero voucher, created when an employee is active, but they do not get paid. This is a global setting that might be overriden at the client level (Client | Change | Client Controls). Note: The PrismHR Customer Support Team strongly recommends that you do not enable the Suppress Arrearage Vouchers field. | | Suppress Garnishment Checks | Select to suppress garnishment payments by check or EFT. | | | | Suppress Employee Direct Deposit | Select to suppress payments that are normally scheduled for direct deposit and print paper checks instead. | | | | Payroll Finalization Notification | Select Email from this drop-down if clients want notifications about payroll finalization. If notifications are enabled, it is possible to enter additional email addresses for people other than the payroll approver to receive notification, such as members of a finance team. Note: To ensure worksite managers/worksite trusted advisors with the Payroll Approver HR Role receive the finalization email, you must enable payroll approval and then approve the payroll. Email notices include a copy of the invoice, but are unrelated to payroll finalization approval policies that are defined at the system level or for the client. These notices are just used for payroll posting notification. | | | | ACH Hold Threshold | Enter the invoice total required to place all ACH transactions on hold. | | | | After Post, Pre Mail Hold | Select what needs holding after posting until the client wires the money: Unpaid Invoices (Not ACH), or All Invoices. | | | | After Post, Pre Mail Threshold | Enter the amount that makes invoices subject to After Post, Pre Mail Hold. For example, enter 2000 to hold invoices that are greater than $2000. | | | | Maximum ACH Amount | Enter the maximum allowable ACH amount to enforce a threshold amount requiring ACH approval. > Note: Note the following: - You can only enter positive numeric values in this field, which are limited to two decimal places. If you enter more than two decimal places, the system truncates them. For example, the system truncates 5000.7890 to 5000.78. - When processing payrolls where the Maximum ACH Amount is set and the client is configured for Service-service Payroll with Finalize & Print restrictions, submitted payrolls (those whose status is set to INITPEND) that exceed a set ACH amount are prevented from processing until they are approved by a Billing Representative. These submitted payrolls are those where the payroll processor clicks Yes when the “You cannot make any changes once you submit payroll for processing. Are you sure you want to submit?” message displays. (Note that this message does not display when payrolls are submitted in SuperBatch.) | | |
  • You can only enter positive numeric values in this field, which are limited to two decimal places. If you enter more than two decimal places, the system truncates them. For example, the system truncates 5000.7890 to 5000.78.
  • When processing payrolls where the Maximum ACH Amount is set and the client is configured for Service-service Payroll with Finalize & Print restrictions, submitted payrolls (those whose status is set to INITPEND) that exceed a set ACH amount are prevented from processing until they are approved by a Billing Representative. These submitted payrolls are those where the payroll processor clicks Yes when the “You cannot make any changes once you submit payroll for processing. Are you sure you want to submit?” message displays. (Note that this message does not display when payrolls are submitted in SuperBatch.) | Enable E-Verification? | Select Enable E-Verification? if you want to submit new hires to use E-Verify. After selecting this field a message displays, “E-Verify will be enabled for all locations. You can exclude a location from E-Verify on the Worksite Location under Other Parameters.” > Note: Note: E-Verification occurs after Form I-9 Section 2 is completed. | | Enable I9 Remote Verification | This field will only display if the Enable E-Verification? field above is checked. By default this box will be unchecked. Check this box if you want to enable Form I-9 remote verification for all worksites of this client who are authorized to remotely verify Form I-9s for their employees. When you check this box a message pops up stating E-Verify will be enabled for all locations. You can exclude a location from E-Verify on the Worksite Location under Other Parameters. Click OK to continue. > Note: Note: If you want to disable Form I-9 remote verification for any particular worksite for this client, you can do so using the Enable Remote Verification for this Location field under the Other Parameters section on the Worksite Locations form for that worksite. |
  1. Click Save.

Specifying General Ledger Template

Specify a general ledger template with the Employer Accounting panel that associates between benefit plans, retirement plans, billing codes, or pay codes to general ledger accounts.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

G/L Template — Click the G/L Template link to select a template to use for checking accounts, accounts receivable, or inter-company transaction accounts. These codes permit different clients to use different G/L account numbers for posting. Note: An error message displays if you enter or select an obsoleteG/L Template, and the system will not process a payroll when a client assigned with an obsolete G/L template tries to initiate payroll calculation or finalization.

G/L Code — Enter any required codes. Use G/L Code for general ledger distributions, and Misc G/L Codes for special transactions.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Client Accounting

Specify client accounting with the Client Accounting panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Generate Accounting — Select if a client uses accounting. This causes payroll initialization to fail if all revenues are not accounted for.

Accounting Template — Select an accounting template used by this client.

Override By Position — Select to override the G/L account by Pay Code or Job Code.

Generate ASO Client Accounting — Select for clients that have the Service Type set to Corporate or Staffing who want to use ASO Accounting. This field is hidden if the Service Type is set to something besides Corporate or Staffing. You can only check this field if the client has a set-up record in the ASO.GL.DEFN file. When you run the client accounting download, it prompts you for a payroll number instead of an invoice. It builds the client accounting file by reading and processing ASO Account Journals. > Note: Note: You can not have the Generate Accounting box checked if Generate ASO Client Accounting is enabled.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up New Hires and Employees

Set up new hires and employees with the New Hire/Employee Detail Controls panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Employee Number Required for EP NH Assign a unique number to new employees.
Auto Employee Number Automatically assign an employee number to new hires.
Allow First Period Hours The Allow First Period Hours drop-down displays if your organization has selected Enable New Hire First Period Hours in System Parameters. Indicates whether users of the New Hire form can enter prorated hours for the new employee for the client if their start date is in the middle of a pay period. This is used for salaried employees who have auto time sheet hours. - System Default: The New Hire form displays the First Pay Period Hours field only if Enable New Hire First Period Hours is selected in System Parameters. - Enabled: The First Pay Period Hours field is available in the New Hire form, regardless of the setting in System Parameters. - Disabled: The New Hire form will not display the First Pay Period Hours field, regardless of the setting in System Parameters.
  • System Default: The New Hire form displays the First Pay Period Hours field only if Enable New Hire First Period Hours is selected in System Parameters.
  • Enabled: The First Pay Period Hours field is available in the New Hire form, regardless of the setting in System Parameters.
  • Disabled: The New Hire form will not display the First Pay Period Hours field, regardless of the setting in System Parameters. | Department Required | Specify a department for employee time sheets and labor allocation. | | Shift Required | Specify a shift for employee time sheets and labor allocation. | | Work Group Required | Employees require a work group in the Work Group Required field. | | Division Required | Specify a division for employee time sheets and labor allocation. |
  1. Click Save.

Set up Benefits Enrollment and Onboarding with the Enrollment/Onboarding Controls panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Electronic Enrollment Indicate whether employees use PrismHR enrollment.
Electronic On-Boarding Indicate whether new employees use electronic on-boarding: - Always: Employees always do their own onboarding online. - Never: Employees never do their own onboarding. Instead, administrators enter employee information in the New Hire form. - Prompt: The New Hire form prompts you to indicate whether the employee will complete the onboarding process online, or if you will enter the information in the New Hire form. Note: If you set this field to Null or Never, these conditional fields do not display: - Electronic On-Boarding for Rehires - I-9 Business Address - Allow Upload of Supporting Documents (I-9) - Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll - Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit
  • Always: Employees always do their own onboarding online.
  • Never: Employees never do their own onboarding. Instead, administrators enter employee information in the New Hire form.
  • Prompt: The New Hire form prompts you to indicate whether the employee will complete the onboarding process online, or if you will enter the information in the New Hire form.

  • System Default: The system uses the Allow Upload of Supporting Documents (I-9) global onboarding option to determine whether to allow this client’s employees to upload supporting I-9 documents
  • Enabled: The system allows this client’s employees to upload supporting I-9 documents
  • Disabled: The system does not allow this client’s employees to upload supporting I-9 documents | Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll | Select one of the following options: - System Default: The system defaults to adding employees to payroll once they submit their Form I‑9 Section 1 (and the manager completes the Form I‑9 Section 2) and Employee Information form - Enabled: Prevents employees from being added to payroll until all onboarding forms are complete - Disabled: Does not prevent employees from being added to payroll until all onboarding forms are complete |

  • Enabled: Prevents employees from being added to payroll until all onboarding forms are complete
  • Disabled: Does not prevent employees from being added to payroll until all onboarding forms are complete | Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit | Select one of the following options: - System Default: Uses the configuration of the Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit field on the Onboarding Options form. For more information, see the System Administration User Guide. - Enabled: Employees can access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms. - Disabled: Employees are unable to access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms. |

  • Enabled: Employees can access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms.
  • Disabled: Employees are unable to access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms. | Decision Support | Select one of the following options: - Enabled: Enables the Decision Support form to appear in the enrollment workflow. - Disabled: Prevents the Decision Support form from appearing in the enrollment workflow. |
  • Enabled: Enables the Decision Support form to appear in the enrollment workflow.
  • Disabled: Prevents the Decision Support form from appearing in the enrollment workflow.
  1. Click Save.

Setting Pay Codes

Set pay codes and how they display on pay stubs with the Pay Codes panel. Pay codes entered in this panel are the only pay codes that can be used for the client.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Clone Pay Codes from another client — Copy pay codes from an existing client. This overwrites codes already set up for this client, but you can add codes afterward.

Pay Code — Enter or select each pay code.

Print on Pay Stub — Select a method for the pay code.

  1. Click Save.

Specifying Deduction Codes

Set deduction codes for the client with the Deduction Codes panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Clone Deductions from another client — Copy deduction codes from an existing client. This overwrites codes already set up for this client, but you can add codes afterward.

Deduction Code — Enter or select each deduction code.

Print on Pay Stub — Select a method for the deduction code.

Worksite User Selectable — Select Yes if this deduction code should be available to worksite users.

  1. Click Save.

Entering Voluntary or Employee Loan Deduction Codes

Enter or select voluntary or employee loan deduction codes that can go into arrears for a client with the Arrears panel.

To enter voluntary or employee loan deduction codes, do the following:

  1. Go to the Arrears panel and complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Clone Arrears codes from another client — Copy arrears deduction codes from an existing client. This overwrites codes already set up for this client, but you can add codes afterward.

Voluntary/Employee Loan Deductions To Arrears — Enter or select each code that can go into arrears.

  1. Click Save.

Specifying Employee Types

Enter or select the employee type codes you would like to display to this client. If you leave this table empty, the client can use any employee types in the system. If you specify one or more types here, the client will only be able to use the specified types.

Note: Note: The settings configured here will apply to any Service Provider, Worksite Manager, and Worksite Trusted Advisor users associated with the client.

To set up employee type codes:

  1. Locate the Employee Types panel.

  2. Enter or select the appropriate Employee Types. A Description will populate next to each type.

  3. Click Save.

Entering Employee Statuses

Enter or select the employee statuses in this panel that you would like to display to this client. If you leave this table empty, the client can see all employee statuses on the forms as any user type. If you specify one or more employee statuses in this panel, the client will only be able to see the specified statuses in the following forms: Reactivation, Rehire, Status Type Change, and Employee Termination.

Note: Note: The settings configured here will apply to any Service Provider, Worksite Manager, and Worksite Trusted Advisor users associated with the client.

To enter employee statuses:

  1. Locate the Employee Statuses panel.

  2. Enter or select the appropriate Employee Statuses. A Description will populate next to each type.

  3. Click Save.

Defining Reason Codes

Enter or select the reason codes in this panel that you would like to display to this client. If you leave this table empty, the client can use any reason codes in the system. If you specify one or more reason codes in this panel, the client will only be able to use the specified codes in the following forms: Leave of Absence, Pay Rate Change, Reactivation, Rehire, Status Type Change, and Employee Termination.

Note: Note: The settings configured here will apply to any Service Provider, Worksite Manager, and Worksite Trusted Advisor users associated with the client.

To define reason codes:

  1. Locate the Reason Codes panel.

  2. Enter or select the appropriate Reason Codes. A Description will populate next to each type.

  3. Click Save.

Reporting

Set allocation formats used by a client with the Reporting panel.

  1. Select each of the Allocation Formats Allowed.

  2. Click Save.

Specifying Client Custom Feature Codes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Custom_Feature_Codes.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Custom_Feature_Codes.htm

Specifying Client Custom Feature Codes

Customize feature codes in Client Details to set special features for the client. The codes in this table are the most common codes available for general use. To implement custom codes, contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

Custom Feature Code Description
ALLOWWMNEWHIREALTEMP.C Allows users of the New Hire form to select an alternate employer defined in Client Details instead of the client’s primary employer. This applies to worksite managers and trusted advisors associated with this client.
BATCH-PAYMENTS-CHANGE-DEFAULTS-C Allocates batch payments. Note: You must set the BATCH-PAYMENTS-CHANGE-DEFAULTS (system level) and BATCH-PAYMENTS-CHANGE-DEFAULTS-C (client level) custom feature codes to display the Edit Employee Details column on the Batch Payments form.
BLOCKHRAPPROVALNOTIFY Disables the notification email that is normally sent to the client’s users to let them know the results of their requested HR action (either denied or approved).
CLIENT-ADD-DEPENDENTS-REQ-FIELDS Sets the following additional fields as required on the Employee Dependents form: - First Name - Birth Date - Gender - Social Security Number - Relation
  • First Name
  • Birth Date
  • Gender
  • Social Security Number
  • Relation | DISABLE-MANUALCHK-TAX-EDIT-WSM | Prevents worksite managers and worksite trusted advisors from editing taxes in the Tax Wthld Amt column on the Tax Calculation Overrides form, which is accessible after calculating the totals in a manual check entry. (Service providers can continue to edit taxes in the Tax Wthld Amt column even if this custom feature code is applied.) | | | NO-BENEFIT-B4-401K | Overrides the global custom feature code BENEFIT-B4-401K at the client level. During payroll processing, if employee pay cannot cover both benefit and 401(k) deductions, the system prioritizes 401(k) deductions. | | | NOTIPALLOWANCE | The tip allowance does not display on pay stubs for employees whose home or work location is in New York or Oregon. If your organization does not set this custom feature code, the tip allowance displays on the pay stubs for employees in those states. Limitation: The system only supports this for one position per employee. If an employee has multiple tipped positions with the client, the tip allowance is the average for each of the tipped positions. | | | PAYCARD-AUTOMOVE.C | The client uses pay cards to pay employees until the employee specifies another account. | | | PAYOR-LOCATION | The system uses the Location Description as the payee on the face of checks. | | | SHIFTPAYRATES | Defines pay rates for shifts in the Shifts form. | | | SHOWHALFPAYHOURS-CLIENT | Displays hours for pay codes with Factor < 1.00 on the Batch Register Report. | | | SHOWOBSOLETE-LCDPDV | Displays obsolete locations when setting up Data Security for worksite manager and trusted advisor users (accessed from the Users form). | | | SUPPRESS-UNHANDLED-TAX-ERR | Suppresses the error messages for tax forms that were not handled during payroll initialization. Note: You can set this code at the system level, but it works appropriately only when set at the client level. | | | TS-LOAD.HRS.WORKED-C | Populates Hours Worked with the Hours from the Import file and Hours Paid from the Employee Default Hours in the Detail Time Sheet Entry form. | | | TS-SUPPRESS-SPECIAL-C | Suppresses users from accessing a payroll until after they perform a timesheet import for a special batch using the Time Sheet Import form, which is available on the Payroll | Change menu. Note: After the import finishes users can access the payroll batch, and only the employees that were imported into the batch are visible in the timesheets (instead of the whole pay group list of employees). | | VARSALMETHOD | Displays Variable Salary option in Pay Method field. When enabled, the Variable Salary option lets you track the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work. | | ========================================================================

Enabling Specialized Features

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Custom_Features.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Custom_Features.htm

Enabling Specialized Features

Set up specialized features for a client company with the Custom Features form.

Maintaining Third Party Payees Vendor Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Cutback_Payees.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Cutback_Payees.htm

Maintaining Third Party Payees Vendor Information

Use the Third Party Payees form to maintain vendor information.

To maintain vendor information, do the following:

  1. Enter the third party Payee ID.

  2. Enter the actual vendor Payee Name.

  3. In the Remit To Name field, enter the vendor name to print on the check.

  4. Enter the vendor’s address and ZIP Code. The system displays the vendor’s city and state automatically. If there is more than one city associated with that ZIP Code, select the location in the pop-up window.

  5. Click Save.

Maintaining Client Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Detail_Tab.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Detail_Tab.htm

Maintaining Client Information

Set company information with the Detail tab on the Client Details form.

Establishing Client Information

Specify basic information with the Name & Address panel.

To set client information, do the following:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Complete these fields in the Name & Address panel.

Field — Description

Client ID — Enter the unique identifier for the client.

Client Name (DBA) — Enter a ‘doing business as’ name.

Legal Name — Enter the client’s legal name.

Address — Enter the client’s main street address. If there is a separate mailing address, enter that information on the Account tab.

ZIP Code — Enter a zip code. If required, enter ZIP+4 in the Suffix field

City — Enter a city or town location.

State — Select a state from the State drop-down, or refine the search by entering a state name.

Telephone — Enter the main telephone number. Note: You can initiate a Zoom call to the client’s phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.

Fax Number — Enter a fax number.

Website — Enter a URL for the client’s web site.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Client Business Information

Set a client’s business information in the Contract panel.

To set business information, do the following:

  1. Complete these fields in the Contract panel.
Field Description
Status The current status of a client. Click the Edit icon [Image: screenshot] to open the form where you can change the client’s status (see Changing the Client Status). (Note: The system displays an error message if you do not save changes to the Client Details form before selecting the Edit icon [Image: screenshot] : “You must save your changes before changing company status.”) Note: This feature is only available to users with the correct permissions.
Status Date Date when the current status went into effect.
Contract Date Enter the date when the contract was signed.
First Pay Date Enter the first payroll date processed for a client. Note the following: - If you enter this date: The period start and end dates will not auto-populate. - If you leave this field blank: When the first, regularly scheduled payroll is posted, this field on the client details record updates with the first check date, period start date, and period end date. These values can all be changed. - The Period Start Date and Period End Date fields are hidden until after the first regular payroll posts. Once the payroll posts, the dates in these fields populate according to the following rules: - If the client’s Status Date is before the determined date of 10/14/2019, the Period Start Date and Period End Date fields will not populate or display. - If the client’s Status Date is equal to or greater than (=>) 10/14/2019, the Period Start Date and Period End Date fields will populate and display. After posting the first regular payroll and the dates populate in these fields, you can change the dates.
  • If you enter this date: The period start and end dates will not auto-populate.
  • If you leave this field blank: When the first, regularly scheduled payroll is posted, this field on the client details record updates with the first check date, period start date, and period end date. These values can all be changed.
  • The Period Start Date and Period End Date fields are hidden until after the first regular payroll posts. Once the payroll posts, the dates in these fields populate according to the following rules:
  • If the client’s Status Date is before the determined date of 10/14/2019, the Period Start Date and Period End Date fields will not populate or display.
  • If the client’s Status Date is equal to or greater than (=>) 10/14/2019, the Period Start Date and Period End Date fields will populate and display. | Service Type | The Service Type indicates the services provided to this client. (Note: The correct Service Type ensures accurate billing.) - Administrative Services Only: Also known as ASO, the client should be linked to an Employer that has the client’s information. Typically this is the record that was created when the client was entered in the system. The system assumes cost equals billed for taxes and workers’ compensation. - Corporate: This option provides a Corporate Accounting model that works best for corporate companies. In addition, using this option with the Product Version field set to Professional in the Global Configuration form runs corporate accounting for this client in a professional product version for the rest of the clients. (Note: No billing will ever occur for a corporate client; if the client should be billed, select a different service type.) - Staffing: This option provides a Staffing Accounting model that works best for staffing companies. In addition, using this option with the Product Version field set to Professional in the Global Configuration form runs staffing accounting for this client in a professional product version for the rest of the clients. (Note: No billing will ever occur for a corporate client; if the client should be billed, select a different service type.) - Professional Employer: Also known as PEO, this option indicates that the service provider manages human resources, employee benefits, payroll, workers’ compensation, accounts receivable, and COBRA administration for the new client. Cost is considered the PEO cost for taxes and workers’ compensation rates, which are entered at the system/employer levels. Billed rates can be higher (for profit centers) or lower than cost if appropriate, and rates are specified at the client level. - HR Services Only: The service provider manages only human resources for the client’s employees: hiring, termination, performance, compensation, training, policy, and OSHA-related services. There is no payroll. - Payroll Services Only: The employer of record provides only payroll and payroll tax services for the client. The employer does not provide human resources services. - Admin. Service/Alt. Billing: This option is similar to an ASO in that the system creates an employer record automatically. The system treats the client as a PEO for billing purposes. The organization can open profit centers on taxes and workers’ compensation. The system does not assume cost equals billed. |
  • Administrative Services Only: Also known as ASO, the client should be linked to an Employer that has the client’s information. Typically this is the record that was created when the client was entered in the system. The system assumes cost equals billed for taxes and workers’ compensation.
  • Corporate: This option provides a Corporate Accounting model that works best for corporate companies. In addition, using this option with the Product Version field set to Professional in the Global Configuration form runs corporate accounting for this client in a professional product version for the rest of the clients. (Note: No billing will ever occur for a corporate client; if the client should be billed, select a different service type.)
  • Staffing: This option provides a Staffing Accounting model that works best for staffing companies. In addition, using this option with the Product Version field set to Professional in the Global Configuration form runs staffing accounting for this client in a professional product version for the rest of the clients. (Note: No billing will ever occur for a corporate client; if the client should be billed, select a different service type.)
  • Professional Employer: Also known as PEO, this option indicates that the service provider manages human resources, employee benefits, payroll, workers’ compensation, accounts receivable, and COBRA administration for the new client. Cost is considered the PEO cost for taxes and workers’ compensation rates, which are entered at the system/employer levels. Billed rates can be higher (for profit centers) or lower than cost if appropriate, and rates are specified at the client level.
  • HR Services Only: The service provider manages only human resources for the client’s employees: hiring, termination, performance, compensation, training, policy, and OSHA-related services. There is no payroll.
  • Payroll Services Only: The employer of record provides only payroll and payroll tax services for the client. The employer does not provide human resources services.
  • Admin. Service/Alt. Billing: This option is similar to an ASO in that the system creates an employer record automatically. The system treats the client as a PEO for billing purposes. The organization can open profit centers on taxes and workers’ compensation. The system does not assume cost equals billed. | Employer | Enter the company that employs the client and its employees, or select a company from the Employer drop-down. You can also refine the search by entering the Employer Name. | | Federal Entity ID | If the client’s employer is certified as a PEO by the IRS, enter the Federal Entity ID or select an ID from the Search Entity lookup form. If the employer is not flagged as a certified PEO in Employer Details, this field does not display. | | Termination Reason | If the client is terminated, the Termination Reason field displays the reason for the termination. The field displays only when the status type is set to the Terminated class. To update a client’s termination reason, select an option from the Termination Reason field. | | Inactivation Reason | If the client is inactivated, the Inactivation Reason field displays the reason for the inactivation. The field only displays when the status type is set to the Inactivated class. To update a client’s inactivation reason, select an option from the Inactivation Reason field. |
  1. Click Save.

Setting Industry-related Information

Set industry-related information in the Classifications panel.

To define industry-related information, do the following:

  1. Complete these fields in the Classifications panel:

Field — Description

Business Entity Type — Select an entity type, which is used for tax reporting. For example, Limited Liability Corporation.

Business Description — Enter a brief description of the business.

Client Category — Enter a client category (such as CON for Construction), or select a category from the Select Client Category look-up form. You can also refine the search by specifying the Category Description.

SIC Code — Enter the SIC (Standard Industrial Classification) code for this client, or select a code from the SIC Codes Selection look-up form. You can also refine the search by specifying the SIC Code or the Classification. Note: Cursor over the SIC Code and NAICS Code descriptions to display their full name. (A truncated version of each description displays online using ellipses (…).)

NAICS Code — Enter the NAICS (North American Industry Classification System) code for a client’s primary business activity, or select a code from the Select NAICS Code look-up form. (You can also refine the search by specifying the NAICS Code or the Class Description.) Note: If there is no NAICS Code assigned here or the NAICS Code assigned here is obsolete then the EEO-1 build will fail.

International Client — Select to identify the client as an international client. Note: If you enable International Client, the ABA/Routing Number field on the Billing tab changes to Swift Code, so you can bypass standard ABA routing number validation by specifying a particular bank or branch for international money transfers (8-11 characters). (See Specifying Client Billing Options.)

Cost Center — Enter the cost center used to process accounting transactions, or select a cost center from the Cost Center Selection look-up form. You can also refine the search by specifying the Cost Center ID or the Cost Center Name.

Sub-Cost Center — Enter the sub-cost center assigned to a client or select a sub-cost center from the Sub Cost Center Selection look-up form.

Multi-Entity Group/Description — The first three multi-entity groups associated with the client display in alphabetical order. (You can scroll down to view other multi-entity groups.) See Organizing Similar Clients. Note: Select a multi-entity group to display its details in the Multi-Entity Group Details form. (See Creating New Multi-Entity Groups.)

  1. Click Save.

Managing Client Security Settings

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Details_Security.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Details_Security.htm

Managing Client Security Settings

Use the Security form (accessed through the Client DetailsActions menu

[Image: screenshot]

) to manage the client’s security settings.

Defining Client Security

You can define the Data Security Entities that users can access by specifying the defaults and the settings to which you want to restrict access. Data Security Entities are entities and work assignments that users can set for employees in the Employee Details > Work tab, specifically Location, Division, Department, Shift, or Work Group.

The Data Security Entities in the Client Security panel are the only ones by which your organization can restrict access for the client. If you do not list an item, you cannot restrict user access by that entity. As indicated below, you can restrict client access by location, division, and department. Because shift and work group are not listed, users can access all shift and work group information, if any.

[Image: screenshot]

If the client does not use an entity type (for example, they have only one location), there is probably no reason to include that entity.

For example, if some employees have division assignments and should have access to one or more departments, select Department from Data Security Entity and set Default Access to Full Access. If other employees do not have any department assigned, and you do not want them to have access to department information, set Default Access when Blank to No Access.

  1. For each entity:
a. Select each Data Security Entity.
  1. — Select the Default Access for the entity. This can be changed at the user level; see Defining Users for more information.

  • Full Access: Users can view, edit, and save data.

  • No Access: Users cannot see any information associated with the entity.

  1. — Select the Default Access when Blank to indicate the default behavior if an employee user does not have a value for the entity.

  • Full Access: Employees that have nothing set for the entity can view, edit, and save data.

  • No Access: Employees that have nothing set for the entity cannot see any information associated with the entity.

  1. Click Accept.

Maintaining Worksite Users

This panel allows you to manage the worksite users who have access to the client.

[Image: screenshot]

If available, you can:

  • Click the User ID link to edit the user account. (Note: The access information that displays is dependent on the user record’s current access for the selected client, which is available on the Users form.)

  • Click the Add User button to create a new user account to associate with the client.

See Defining Users for information on editing and creating user accounts.

Associating User and Human Resource Roles with a Worksite Manager

Select any role to associate with a worksite manager in the User Roles and Human Resource Roles panels, so managers who have access to set up other managers can only access these user and Human Resources roles for the client.

Suppressing Payroll Warnings

Suppress payroll warnings by selecting a warning in the Payroll Suppress panel.

Maintaining Security Settings for the Client

Use the options in the Access panel to maintain the client’s security settings.

  1. Set sMFA Device Expiration to use the global setting, a different interval period from the global setting, or disable multi-factor authentication for this client. (Note: This setting automatically defaults to 30 Days.)

When enabled, all users associated with this client who access the system will need to re-authenticate their access after the specified time interval, if any, for each device they use (computer, tablet, and so on). The system prompts them to request an authentication code, which it sends to the email address set on their user account (users must have email address). Then the user can enter the authentication code to regain access to the system.

  1. Select the sMFA Enabled for Trusted Advisor Password Reset option to enable multi-factor authentication for all worksite trusted advisors who have access to the client, requiring them to use multi-factor authentication when they use the Forgot Password link.

  2. Select Enable Security for Messages to restrict the users, companies, and so on, that the service provider can select in the New Message form to those that client users can access.

  3. Use the IP White List/Range table to restrict worksite users (managers, trusted advisors, and employees) to a specific IP address or range of addresses. See the examples below.

Using IP White List/Range Examples

Your organization can use the white list to grant the client’s users access only at a specific location, such as an office or home, by specifying the IP addresses or the IP range. In the example below, those users can only access PrismHR from the listed IP addresses (123.123.123.101, 123.123.123.102, and 123.123.123.103).

[Image: screenshot]

Your organization can also specify a range of permitted IP addresses.

  • In the example below, those users can only access PrismHR from IP addresses that begin with 123.123.123.

[Image: screenshot]

  • In the example below, those users can only access the system from IP addresses in the range (123.123.123.100, 123.123.123.101, 123.123.123.102, and so on).

Note: Note that if you enter a range, you must separate the first and last addresses with a dash and that the first three numbers must match. If the first three numbers do not match, that is not a valid IP address range and the system will ignore it.

[Image: screenshot]

Attaching Files to Client Records

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Documents.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Documents.htm

Attaching Files to Client Records

Attach files to a client record and open files attached to the record with the Client Documents form. To access, select Client > Client Details and select Documents from the Actions menu.

To attach a document:

  1. Click Load a Document.

  2. In the Upload a File window, click Browse.

  3. Navigate to the file that you want to upload and click Open.

  4. Click Submit.

  5. In the Confirm Document form, the Document Name field populates the file name.

  6. Enter any Document Notes.

  7. Click Accept.

Once a document is in the Client Documents list:

  • Click Document Name to download the document.

  • Click [Image: screenshot] next to a document to remove it from the company record.

Creating Events to Display in ESS

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Events.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Events.htm

Creating Events to Display in ESS

You can create events that display in Employee Portal (EP) using the Client Events form. The form lists existing events.

To filter the list of events, do the following:

Go to the Client Events form on the Client|Change menu and specify the filters as desired:

Field Description
Organizer Select the Organizer user name to view events by a specific organizer.
From Date/Thru Date Enter or select the From Date and Thru Date to view events within a specific set of dates.

To maintain events, do the following:

Go to the Client Events form on the Client|Change menu and do the following:

Button/Field Description
Add Click Add to create a new event. The Company Events editor displays.
Event Title Click an Event Title to edit an existing event.

Creating or Editing an Event

After clicking Add in the Client Events form, you can create or edit an event in the Company Events editor form that displays.

To create an event, do the following:

  1. In the Company Events editor form, complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Event Title — Enter the Event Title.

Event Date — Enter or select the Event Date.

Start Time/End Time — Enter the Start Time and End Time. You can enter the time by including AM or PM, or enter it in 24-hour format. For example, if you enter 10:00 or 10:00AM, the system displays 10:00AM. If you enter 14:00 or 2:00PM, the system displays 02:00PM.

Organizer — The Organizer field displays the name of the user who created the event.

Details — Enter the Details of the event. At this time, you can only upload documents to an existing event. If you are creating a new event and want to attach a document, you need to save it and reopen it.

  1. To attach documents to the event, click Upload Documents. See Attaching Event Documents

  2. You can limit the employees who will see the event information by specifying certain departments, divisions, shifts, locations, pay groups, and work groups. In the appropriate table, enter the Code for each entity that should see the event.

  3. Click Save.

Attaching Event Documents

You can attach documents to a new event or an existing event.

  1. In the Company Event panel, click Upload Documents.

  2. In the Upload a File window, click Choose File.

  3. Navigate to the file that you want to upload, and then click Open.

  4. Click Submit in the Upload a File form.

  5. In the Confirm Document form, the Document Name field populates with the file name.

  6. Enter any Document Notes.

  7. Click Accept. You return to the Company Event panel.

Once a document displays in the Documents list:

  • Click the Document Name to download the document.

  • Click the [Image: screenshot] next to a document to remove it from the event record.

  • Select ESS to share the document on Employee Portal (EP).

Using Client Notes

Use the Client Notes form to store notes.

  4. Enter an Employee ID to view notes for a specific employee.

Entering Note Details

Enter any notes regarding the client or a specific employee.

To enter a note:

  1. Enter the Employee ID if this note applies to an employee.

  2. Enter the note in the field provided.

  3. Click Save.

Storing Miscellaneous Client Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_Notes_Tab.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_Notes_Tab.htm

Storing Miscellaneous Client Information

You can store information about the client and the invoice using the Notes tab on the Client Details form. These notes are available to anyone (including clients) who can view the Notes tab.

Important! Any notes you enter in the Invoice Notes box automatically display on the client’s invoice.

Note: Select Audit Client Notes on the Actions menu to view updates to notes.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Click the Notes tab.

  4. Select a Category to associate a note at the client level with a category.

Note: Note the following:

  • The categories that display in the Category field are directly related to the categories created in the Client Notes Category form. (See Creating Notes Templates.)

  • All existing notes reside in the default OV - Overview category until they are changed.

  • If a category is deleted, the system deletes any notes associated with that category and the deleted category no longer displays in the Category field. (You cannot delete the OV - Overview category. If you try to delete this category, an error message displays.)

  1. Complete the notes as needed.

  2. Click Save.

Note: The system shortens the text-wrap length when you enter text in the Invoice Notes box and click Save.

  • Select the Disabled option to prevent the system from sending a notification for this client.
  • Enabling this field sends a notification to emails listed in the additional notification emails for Payroll Finalization Notifications on the Control tab and to worksite managers/worksite trusted advisors who have the Payroll Approver H/R Role.
  • If the Employee Payroll Voucher Reversal Notification field is set to Enabled and the Payroll Finalization Notification field on the Control tab is set to No, the system does not send the email notification.
  • If the Employee Payroll Voucher Reversal Notification field is set to Disabled and the Payroll Finalization Notification field on the Control tab is set to Email or No, the system does not send the email notification.
  1. Click Save.

Assigning Employees to Alternate Employers

Assign employees of the client to alternate employers (different from what is set on the Details tab) with the Alternate Employers panel. For example, if a PEO does business in different states, their employees can be assigned to alternate employers in those states. For these alternate employers, specify the cost center, accounts, and other information.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Employer ID Enter or select each alternate ID. > Note: Note: If a client is assigned to an employer that is a Certified Professional Employer Organization (CPEO) and you try to add a non-CPEO employer in the Alternate Employers panel, a warning message displays. Click Yes to save the record or click No to return to the Client Details form without saving.
Cost Center Enter or select the cost center for employees assigned to the alternate employer. If no value is entered, the system uses the primary cost center for the client.
Checking Account Select the payroll checking account used by the alternate employer.
ACH Account Select the ACH account used by the alternate employer.
New Hire Display Name Enter the new hire display name. When you enter a new hire, this name displays as one of the employer IDs you can select (depending on the Suppress Client Access setting).
Suppress Client Access Indicate whether to suppress access. Options are: - Yes: You cannot select the alternate employer in the New Hire form. - No: You are able to select the alternate employer.
  • Yes: You cannot select the alternate employer in the New Hire form.
  • No: You are able to select the alternate employer.
  1. Click Save.

Controlling Client Payroll Processing

You can control employee payroll processing and reporting using the Payroll tab in the Client Details form.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Click the Payroll tab.

Setting Parameters

Set payroll processing parameters with the Payroll Processing Options panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
One Payroll per Pay Group Do one of the following: - Enable One Payroll per Pay Group to create separate payroll control batches when multiple pay groups have the same pay date. - Disable One Payroll per Pay Group to create one payroll control batch when multiple pay groups have the same pay date. Note: If payroll control batches were created before enabling or disabling One Payroll per Pay Group, you must delete and recreate the batches for the change to take effect.
  • Enable One Payroll per Pay Group to create separate payroll control batches when multiple pay groups have the same pay date.
  • Disable One Payroll per Pay Group to create one payroll control batch when multiple pay groups have the same pay date. | FLSA ruleset | Select the Fair Labor Standards Act (FLSA) ruleset for processing payroll for this client. | | FLSA Work Week Ends On | Select the day when the work week ends for FLSA rulesets. Note: The Work Week Ends On option selected on the Pay Groups form for the employees’ assigned pay groups overrides this value if one is selected. | | Per Diem Rate Source | Select a source (department, position, shift ) for per diem calculations. | | Self-Service Payroll | Select if the client processes their own payrolls. | | Self-Service Restrictions | If you selected the previous option, indicate the Self-Service Restrictions. These are actions the client cannot perform. - Print: The client can calculate and finalize payrolls, but cannot print them. - Finalize & Print: The client can calculate payrolls, but cannot finalize or print them. |
  • Print: The client can calculate and finalize payrolls, but cannot print them.
  • Finalize & Print: The client can calculate payrolls, but cannot finalize or print them. | Paperless Delivery | Set a delivery option for the client: - Inactive: No paperless delivery; print everything for the client. - All Payroll Output: Receives all payroll output (invoice, reports, and so on) electronically. This disables the Delivery Method field since there is nothing to print or ship. - All Payroll Output, Delivery Sheet Only: Enforces paperless delivery, but still physically prints a Payroll Delivery Sheet so you can send checks to clients. - Checks/Advices Only: Receives checks and advices electronically and printed invoices and reports. Also, physically prints the Payroll Delivery Sheet. - Invoice/Reports Only: Receives invoice(s) and reports electronically and printed checks and advices. Also, physically prints the Payroll Delivery Sheet. |
  • Inactive: No paperless delivery; print everything for the client.
  • All Payroll Output: Receives all payroll output (invoice, reports, and so on) electronically. This disables the Delivery Method field since there is nothing to print or ship.
  • All Payroll Output, Delivery Sheet Only: Enforces paperless delivery, but still physically prints a Payroll Delivery Sheet so you can send checks to clients.
  • Checks/Advices Only: Receives checks and advices electronically and printed invoices and reports. Also, physically prints the Payroll Delivery Sheet.
  • Invoice/Reports Only: Receives invoice(s) and reports electronically and printed checks and advices. Also, physically prints the Payroll Delivery Sheet. | Deliver Checks To | Select a standard delivery location for the client’s checks. | | Suppress Delivery Sheet | Prevents the Payroll Delivery Sheet from physically printing when the Checks/Advices Only option or Invoice/Reports Only option is selected. | | Delivery Method | Select or enter a delivery method for the client’s checks. | | Recalculated O/T Pay Code | Select or enter a pay code for paying a recalculated overtime pay amount. | | Use Federal Min Rate (WV) | Used for locations in West Virginia that are subject to the federal minimum wage rate. |
  1. Click Save.

Setting Conditions for Tipped Employees

Determine how the system handles certain conditions when processing payrolls for tipped employees in the Tipped Employees panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Adjust Taxes to Cover Cash Shortfall Indicate the tax adjustment process to use if there is a shortage of cash earnings.
Adjust Reported Tips to Minimum Wage Selected: When you select this field, reported tips will be adjusted (increased) to meet the minimum wage level. If the employee’s total earnings (cash + tips) do not meet or exceed the state’s minimum wage (not the cash minimum wage), the reported tips will be rounded up so the total earnings will equal minimum wage. Default (not selected): Reported tips will not be adjusted (increased) to meet the minimum wage level. If the employee’s total earnings (cash + tips) do not meet or exceed the state’s minimum wage (not the cash minimum wage), the reported tips will remain as they were entered and the TIP CREDIT MAKEUP pay code will calculate to ensure the total earnings equal minimum wage. For more information, please visit the article Reported Tips are higher than entered on the Customer Resource Center.
Allocated Tip Calc Percent Enter the percentage if the system should use an allocated tips calculation process, otherwise leave this field blank (for example, enter 8.00 for 8%).
Days Until Prenote Auto-Activate Enter the number of days after which the system activates prenoted accounts that were successfully processed through a payroll. (If set to zero (0), this value indicates no prenote process.) Note: You can use the Days Until Prenote Auto-Activate and Prenote On Next Pay Date fields to override the system-level parameters at the client level.
Prenote On Next Pay Date Select the option to run a test case of direct deposit information before an EFT occurs: - No: Runs the prenote on the current date and does not wait until the next pay date. - Next Pay Date: Runs the prenote on the next scheduled pay date for an employee. - Next Banking Day: Runs the prenote on the next banking day.
  • No: Runs the prenote on the current date and does not wait until the next pay date.
  • Next Pay Date: Runs the prenote on the next scheduled pay date for an employee.
  • Next Banking Day: Runs the prenote on the next banking day.
  1. Click Save.

Field Description
Federal Employer ID Number Enter the federal ID number. This is usually the Employer Identification Number (EIN), or some clients might use a Social Security Number.
Tax Geocode Override Option Optional: Select to indicate the Vertex GeoCode the system should use for tax calculations instead of the default. Options are: - –Select–: No override; the system uses the worksite location for the GeoCode (default setting). - Override Work GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode, defined in the Employee Details > Address tab (either GeoCode or Override GeoCode) instead of the worksite location’s GeoCode. Select this when employees work from home. - Override Work GeoCode with Home Location GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s “home” location assignment to supply the GeoCode for the location-based portion of taxes. This selection is not routinely used. - Override T/S GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode from the Employee Details > Address tab in place of the GeoCode on the time sheet.
  • –Select–: No override; the system uses the worksite location for the GeoCode (default setting).
  • Override Work GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode, defined in the Employee Details > Address tab (either GeoCode or Override GeoCode) instead of the worksite location’s GeoCode. Select this when employees work from home.
  • Override Work GeoCode with Home Location GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s “home” location assignment to supply the GeoCode for the location-based portion of taxes. This selection is not routinely used.
  • Override T/S GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode from the Employee Details > Address tab in place of the GeoCode on the time sheet. | Tax Calculation Options | This field is not used and can be left blank. | | Railroad Employer | Select to calculate railroad taxes (and suppress Social Security and Medicare taxes) for all employees of the client. > Note: Note: Railroad taxes are not reported on Form 941 and are not posted to the Employer 941 Register. These taxes are included in the MasterTax RTS file. They need to be reported on the Form CT-1 Employer’s Annual Railroad Retirement Tax Return. PrismHR and MasterTax do not currently support the Form CT-1. | | Allow 1099 Income Tax Withholding | You will need to first enable the custom feature code 1099WITHHOLDING to display these new options. You will need to enable each state in which you wish to withhold income tax from a 1099 contractor. This box is unchecked by default. - When unchecked, no taxes are withheld for any employee who is a 1099 Contractor in PrismHR. - When checked: - PrismHR will withhold state and federal income tax for 1099 contractors hired for the selected client only. - PrismHR will only withhold federal and state income tax for that state (FG and all other states) if the field Allow 1099 Income Tax Withholding under State Tax Parameters section in the State Rules form is also checked. - PrismHR will honor any tax overrides for the 1099 contractors including Add to, Block, Percentage, and Fixed. > Note: Note: You should NOT have 1099 withholding from a contractor if you do not have the Custom Feature Code 1099WITHHOLDING in place. When leveraging the Custom Feature Code 1099WITHHOLDING, you will need to have a separate Employee ID for any 1099 contractor who has tax withheld. Previously, the system allowed a 1099 Contractor in one client and a W2 employee in a second client using the same Employee ID. For purposes of 1099 income tax withholding and reporting, the 1099 contractor must NOT be a W2 employee in any other client. |
  • When unchecked, no taxes are withheld for any employee who is a 1099 Contractor in PrismHR.
  • When checked:
  • PrismHR will withhold state and federal income tax for 1099 contractors hired for the selected client only.
  • PrismHR will only withhold federal and state income tax for that state (FG and all other states) if the field Allow 1099 Income Tax Withholding under State Tax Parameters section in the State Rules form is also checked.

To set up New York SDI Tax information, see Setting Up New York SDI Tax for complete information. |
  1. Click Save.

Updates to Form W-2 build

The State Income Tax Account Number on the employee Form W-2 will now be pulled from the Client Details form or the Employer Details form based on the tax deduction code, unless an override exists on the Employer Details form on the SIT Rpt Entity field for that state under the State Tax section.

The State Income Tax Account Number on the employee Form W-2 will be:

  • Client State Tax ID when:

  • Tax deduction code set to report at Client Level and ER Details, State is set to -select- or Client

  • Tax deduction code NOT set to report at Client Level, but ER Details, State is set to Client

  • Employer State Tax ID:

  • Tax deduction code NOT set to report at Client Level and ER Details, State is set to -select- or Employer

  • Tax deduction code set to report at Client Level, but ER Details, State is set to Employer

Note: If the State Tax ID is not available on the corresponding reporting entity, the Form W-2 will not be built, and an error will appear in the W2 Error Report stating State Employer ID not found for Client # for State XX OR State Employer ID not found for Employer for State XX

Selecting Taxes to Suppress

Select taxes to suppress for all the employees of a client with the Taxes to Suppress panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Tax Code Enter or select each Tax Code to suppress. The Tax Name displays.
Start Date/End Date Date range to suppress the tax code.
  1. Click Save.

Defining W/C Accrual Modifiers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Client_WC_Accrual_Modifiers.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Client_WC_Accrual_Modifiers.htm

Defining W/C Accrual Modifiers

You can define how the system handles when the wages of the client’s employees are not sufficient to support a benefit deduction. In these cases, use the W/C Accrual Modifiers form to define workers’ compensation accrual modifiers that the client uses.

W/C accrual modifiers are required:

  • For states that require client reporting of workers’ compensation accrual

  • When the workers’ compensation policy or Client Details stipulates the use of a modifier and discounts

To set W/C Accrual Modifiers:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select W/C Accrual Modifiers. [Image: W/C Accrual Modifiers form]

  3. Fill in the required information in these fields.

Field Description
State Code Enter a two-character state code, or select it from the drop-down.
Effective Date Enter a date when the bill rate goes into effect, or select an existing date to edit.
Discount/Debit Enter the employer’s Discount/Debit for this state.
Modifier Enter the employer’s Modifier for this state.
Effective Rate The Effective Rate is calculated by multiplying the Discount/Debit by the Modifier.
  1. Click Save.

Defining Payroll Shipping Methods for the Client

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Company_Delivery_Methods.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Company_Delivery_Methods.htm

Defining Payroll Shipping Methods for the Client

Use the Special Delivery form to define methods that users can select for shipping the client’s payrolls. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To define payroll shipping methods for the client:

  1. Select All Locations if the delivery method applies to all locations. If this is selected, the system charges a single fee for the whole client.

  2. If you did not select All Locations, click New and select one of the client’s worksite locations, or enter the code for a location that already has a delivery method defined for it.

  3. Enter the Company Override Method, which is one of the delivery methods defined in your system. This overrides the default.

  4. Enter the Method description, which is usually the name for the service (for example, US Postal Service or FedEx).

  5. Enter the Days In Transit, which is the delivery method’s number of days in transit.

  6. Enter the Bill Event Code, which is the ID of the billing event that the system uses to track this delivery method. If there is no charge for the delivery method, leave this field blank.

  7. Enter the Special Bill Amt to override the standard billing amount for this client, if appropriate.

  8. Use the Method Active drop-down to indicate the status of this delivery method:

  • Yes: This method is available for users to select.

  • No: Users cannot select this method.

  • Once: Select this if the method will be used only once. After it has been triggered, the system sets it to No.

  1. In the Method Until field, enter the date when delivery method should stop being used for the client. After this date, the system sets the Method Active to No.

  2. Select Create Spec Sheet to indicate that a special sheet will print during payroll processing. Do not select this option if a special sheet is not necessary.

  3. If a special sheet will print:

a. Enter the Special Message that prints on the sheet.
b. Enter the Name that should appear on the sheet.
— —
c. Enter the Address to print on the sheet.
— —
d. Enter the City, State, Zip Code to print on the sheet.
— —
  1. Select the Delivery Sheet Format, which ensures that the address on the printed delivery sheet displays in the window envelopes your organization uses. You can select one of the following:

  • The Standard and Expanded options display no line or indent adjustment fields when selected, and the delivery sheet prints the entered data in a fixed position.

  • The Large Window, Large Window (Expanded), Large Window - 1 Page, and Large Window - 1 Page (Expanded) options display adjustment fields for Delivery Sheet Address Line and Deliver Sheet Address Indent. The delivery sheet prints the entered data using these specified adjustments.

  • The 2 Window, 2 Window (Expanded), 2 Window - 1 Page, and 2 Window - 1 Page (Expanded) options display adjustment fields for Delivery Sheet Address Line, Deliver Sheet Address Indent, Client Information Line, and Client Information Indent. The delivery sheet prints the entered data using the specified adjustments.

  • The 2 Window (Shipping on Page 2) and 2 Window (Expanded) (Shipping on Page 2) options, in addition to the adjustment fields described above, print the address on the first piece of paper and the Special Message on the second sheet. The purpose is that the person who is preparing the envelopes can insert the address into the envelope and then read the instructions on the second page (what type of envelope to use, which mail bin to place the envelope in, and so on).

Note: If you select one of the 2 Window formats, you must enter the full address.

  1. Click Save.

After changing these settings, you can rerun the delivery sheet report from the Payroll Summary form, if needed.

Maintaining Company Contacts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Contacts.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Contacts.htm

Maintaining Company Contacts

Use the Contacts form to maintain the contacts for a company.

To maintain contacts for a company:

  1. Go to the Contacts form by selecting the contact link on the Account tab in the Client Details form.

  2. In the Contacts form that displays, complete the following fields:

Field Name — Description

Contact ID — Select New to create a Contact ID for a new contact person. Enter or select the ID of an existing contact to edit the information, or select the contact from the table on the right.

Contact Type — Enter or select the Contact Type ID. The Contact Type displays. Note: You can enter or select multiple Contact Type IDs.

Name — Enter the Name of the contact person.

Title — Enter the contact’s Title.

Address — Enter the contact’s Address.

Zip — Enter the Zip code for the contact’s Address. The City and State automatically display for that address/zip code.

Work Phone — Enter the contact’s Work Phone number. Note: You can initiate a Zoom call to the client’s work phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.

Extension — Enter the contact’s telephone Extension.

Cell Phone — Enter the contact’s Cell Phone number. Note: You can initiate a Zoom call to the client’s cell phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.

Email Address — Enter the contact’s Email Address.

Comments — Enter any Comments related to the contact person.

  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Departments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Departments.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Departments.htm

Maintaining Departments

Set the client department-related codes and benefits using the Departments form.

Establishing Department Codes

Set department codes with the Departments panel. Click Import to launch the Data Import Tool form, where you can upload a database file and import data. (This icon displays for all user roles except “Inquiry Only.”)

Note: Note: For more information about the Data Import Tool form, see the Payroll Administration Guide.

To set department codes:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Departments.

  3. Complete these fields as required.

Field Description
Department Code Enter a new code or search for an existing code. For new codes, use a brief but descriptive term, such as a department name abbreviation (for example, OPER for Operations). Do not use special characters. For existing codes, click Department Code. Select an existing code or search for a code from the Department Codes with User Security form. Note: Codes that list Yes in the Obsolete column cannot be used.
Department Name Enter a descriptive department name.
Per Diem Percent Enter the percentage of pay that is allocated to non-taxable per diem when per diem is paid with the percentage method.
G/L Segment For additional department accounting options, enter the G/L Segment 1 or G/L Segment 2 code for building general ledger accounts. (See the PEO Client Company Accounting task sheet in the Customer Resource Center for more information.) - In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as the G/L account number segment instead of the department code. This replaces EEDP instead of the actual code. - The G/L Segment 2 also allows for more accounting options based on the department code. It is rarely used and it ties to the EEDP2 mnemonic in the G/L interfaces. For any miscellaneous employee department G/L codes (EEMDP01-EEMDP99) that can be used to complete an account number in the client’s accounting system: - Enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMDP01 = REG pay and EEMDP02 = O/T. - Enter the information that replaces the Misc GL Count code in the Misc GL Segment field.
  • In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as the G/L account number segment instead of the department code. This replaces EEDP instead of the actual code.
  • The G/L Segment 2 also allows for more accounting options based on the department code. It is rarely used and it ties to the EEDP2 mnemonic in the G/L interfaces.
  • Enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMDP01 = REG pay and EEMDP02 = O/T.
  • Enter the information that replaces the Misc GL Count code in the Misc GL Segment field. | Obsolete | Select to deactivate the department if it is no longer used and no active employees are assigned to it. Selecting this option also hides the department from most search screens, such as the lookup field in New Hire. |
  1. Click Save.

Working with Miscellaneous GL Segments

Use the Misc GL panel to review Misc GL Counts (predefined wildcards) for this department and enter replacement Misc GL Segments.

Note: During payroll processing, the system substitutes the GL segments for wildcards in GL account codes. These wildcards must also be included in the following setups:

  • GL setup, which is processed into payroll accounting. (You can configure them in the G/L Setup form.)

  • PEO client accounting setup, which is processed into client accounting. (You can configure them in the PEO Client Accounting form.)

When working with miscellaneous GL segments:

  1. The predefined wildcards display in rows in the Misc GL Count column, where the codes are always EEMDP01 through EEMDP99. (The program that evaluates the wildcards only looks for those values.)

  2. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMDP01 = Segment 1 and EEMDP02 = Segment 2. (You can apply these misc segments to account codes for other areas besides pay, such as taxes or deductions.)

Important! Any changes made to the descriptions affect all departments in the active client.

Note: The Misc GL Description column’s values are for reference only. (These descriptions are stored in the client’s record.) The system automatically loads these values each time a department record displays.

  1. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Segment value that replaces the Misc GL Count wildcard.

  2. Click Save.

Establishing Department Benefits

Set department benefits with the Eligible Benefits panel.

Note: The Eligible Benefits panel allows you to select one or more Benefit Plan IDs only if you have selected Departments Restrict Eligibility option in the Eligible Benefits Determinant field in the Benefits tab on the Client Details form. If you do not select this option, then you will not be able to select any Benefit Plan IDs. A conditional message, “Access to this section requires the Eligible Benefits Determinant to be set to Departments Restrict Eligibility on the Client Details form.” also displays in the Eligible Benefits panel.

To establish benefit plans for this department, do the following:

  1. In the Eligible Benefits panel, complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Benefit Plan ID — Each Benefit Plan ID for the benefits that employees in the departments are eligible. Enter the Benefit Plan ID or select Benefit Plan ID to display the Select Benefit Plans list.

Plan Description — A description of the Benefit Plan ID.

Import Eligible Benefits — A quick link to import eligible benefit plans. Select this quick link to display the Eligible Benefits import template in the Data Import Tool.

  1. Click Save.

Note: If the Eligible Benefits panel is disabled and there is any existing data in this panel, then on saving the form, a message, “Do you wish to clear the existing Benefit Plan IDs in the Eligible Benefits section? Y/N” displays.

  • Click Yes to clear the existing Benefit Plans data listed in the Eligible Benefits panel and save the rest of the data on the form.

  • Click No to save all the data on the form.

Accessing Additional Information from the Actions Menu

You can access other information and settings using the Actions menu:

  • Viewing the Audit Log

  • User Fields: Enter any information in the fields defined by your organization.

  • Employees Assigned To: View a list of employees assigned to the location.
