Maintaining Divisions

You can maintain division codes, related benefits, and ACH information for each division at the client level using the Divisions form.

Note: Click the Data Import Tool icon (

[Image: screenshot]

) to upload a database file and import data. (This icon displays for all user roles, except “Inquiry Only.”)

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: For more information about the Data Import Tool form, see the PrismHR Payroll Administration Guide.

Establishing Code Parameters

Set code parameters that represent a division in the Divisions panel.

To set code parameters:

  1. Select the Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Divisions.

  3. Complete these fields as required.

Field Name Description
Division Code Enter a new code or select to search for an existing code. For new codes, use a brief but descriptive term, such as a division name abbreviation (for example, EAST for Eastern Division). For existing codes, click Division Code. Select an existing code or search for a code from the Division Codes with User Security form. Note: Codes that display “Yes” in the Obsolete column cannot be used.
Division Name Enter a descriptive division name.
Transfer Account (Corporate edition only) Enter the inter-divisional transfer account.
Obsolete Select if this division code is no longer in use and no employees are assigned to it. Selecting this option also hides the division from most search screens, such as the lookup field in New Hire. If there are non-active (Terminated) employees allocated to the division on the client’s HR\ Change\ Employee Details form’s Work tab, PrismHR generates a warning message: “If any of the employees with this division code are ever reactivated, there could be payroll errors generated. Do you wish to continue?” Click Yes to proceed or click No to terminate this process.
G/L Segment Enter any segment codes used to build general ledger account numbers: - In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as an alternative to the Division Code. - In G/L Segment 2, enter the G/L code that should substitute for EEDV2 in G/L code routines. - In G/L Segment 3, enter a third G/L code, if needed.
  • In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as an alternative to the Division Code.
  • In G/L Segment 2, enter the G/L code that should substitute for EEDV2 in G/L code routines.
  • In G/L Segment 3, enter a third G/L code, if needed.
  1. Click Save.

Working with Miscellaneous GL Segments

Use the Misc GL panel to review Misc GL Counts (predefined wildcards) for this division and enter replacement Misc GL Segments.

Note: During payroll processing, the system substitutes the GL segments for wildcards in GL account codes. These wildcards must also be included in the following setups:

  • GL setup, which is processed into payroll accounting. (You can configure them in the G/L Setup form.)

  • PEO client accounting setup, which is processed into client accounting. (You can configure them in the PEO Client Accounting form.)

When working with miscellaneous GL segments:

  1. The predefined wildcards display in rows in the Misc GL Count column, where the codes are always EEMDV01 through EEMDV99. (The program that evaluates the wildcards only looks for those values.)

  2. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMDV01 = Segment 1 and EEMDV02 = Segment 2. (You can apply these misc segments to account codes for other areas besides pay, such as taxes or deductions.)

Important! Any changes made to the descriptions affect all divisions in the active client.

Note: The Misc GL Description column’s values are for reference only. (These descriptions are stored in the client’s record.) The system automatically loads these values each time a division record displays.

  1. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Segment value that replaces the Misc GL Count wildcard.

  2. Click Save.

Establishing Division Benefits

Set division benefits with the Eligible Benefits panel.

Note: The Eligible Benefits panel allows you to select one or more Benefit Plan IDs only if you have selected Divisions Restrict Eligibility option in the Eligible Benefits Determinant field in the Benefits tab on the Client Details form. If you do not select this option, then you will not be able to select any Benefit Plan IDs. A conditional message, “Access to this section requires the Eligible Benefits Determinant to be set to Divisions Restrict Eligibility on the Client Details form.” also displays in the Eligible Benefits panel.

To establish benefit plans for this division, do the following:

  1. In the Eligible Benefits panel, complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Benefit Plan ID — Each Benefit Plan ID for the benefits that employees in the divisions are eligible. Enter the Benefit Plan ID or select Benefit Plan ID to display the Select Benefit Plans list.

Plan Description — A description of the Benefit Plan ID.

Import Eligible Benefits — A quick link to import eligible benefit plans. Select this quick link to display the Eligible Benefits import template in the Data Import Tool.

  1. Click Save.

Note: If the Eligible Benefits panel is disabled and there is any existing data in this panel, then on saving the form, a message, “Do you wish to clear the existing Benefit Plan IDs in the Eligible Benefits section? Y/N” displays.

  • Click Yes to clear the existing Benefit Plans data listed in the Eligible Benefits panel and save the rest of the data on the form.

  • Click No to save all the data on the form.

Working with Miscellaneous GL Segments

Use the Misc GL panel to review Misc GL Counts (predefined wildcards) for this division and enter replacement Misc GL Segments.

Note: During payroll processing, the system substitutes the GL segments for wildcards in GL account codes. These wildcards must also be included in the following setups:

  • GL setup, which is processed into payroll accounting. (You can configure them in the G/L Setup form.)

  • PEO client accounting setup, which is processed into client accounting. (You can configure them in the PEO Client Accounting form.)

When working with miscellaneous GL segments:

  1. The predefined wildcards display in nine rows in the Misc GL Count column, where the codes are always EEMDV01 through EEMDV09. (The program that evaluates the wildcards only looks for those values.)

  2. The Misc GL Description column’s values are for reference only. (These descriptions are stored in the client’s record.) The system automatically loads these values each time a division record displays.

Important! Any changes made to the descriptions affect all divisions in the active client.

  1. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Segment value that replaces the Misc GL Count wildcard.

  2. Click Save.

Establishing Banking Information

Set banking information specific to this division with the Division ACH and ACH Details panels. These values override those defined in Client Details.

Note: For Reverse Wire clients, the ACH Details panel name changes to Reverse Wire Details.

To set banking information:

  1. Complete the following fields in these panels:

Field Name — Description

Payroll Check Account — Enter or select the account from which to draw payroll checks.

Direct Deposit Account — Enter or select the account from which to draw direct deposit transactions.

Garnishment Check Account — Enter or select the account from which to draw garnishment checks.

Reverse Wire Account — Enter or select the account from which to draw reverse wire checks. Note: The client must be set up with the Break Field set to Division and Create REAL Invoice Break enabled on the Billing tab in the Client Details form to invoice correctly by division.

Division’s ACH Status — Set a division’s ACH status. For example, Inactive.

ABA/Routing Number — Enter a nine-digit ABA/routing number for the division’s depository bank.

Account Number — Enter a division’s bank account number.

Account Type — Select the account type (checking or savings) from the drop-down.

  1. Click Save.

Using ESS Menu Builder

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/ESS_Menu_Builder.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/ESS_Menu_Builder.htm

Using ESS Menu Builder

Set up menus for EP with ESS Menu Builder. Select Use Default Menu Structure to use the default menu structure configured by the organization for a client’s EP. You cannot edit the ESS menus for a client.

If the default menu structure is not specified, future changes to the default menu will not update the client menu.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select ESS Menu Builder from the Actions menu.

  4. When you finish editing the menu, click Save and then click Close.

Using Menu Headers

Menu headers are the top-level menus seen by EP users.

To add a new header:

  1. Click New Menu Header. The Add a New Menu Structure window opens.

  2. Enter the label for the menu header in the Text field. This displays in EP on the menu tile.

  3. In the Tool-Tip Text field, enter the text that appears when a user hovers the mouse over the menu header.

  4. Enter the web URL of the main page for that menu in the Link field.

  5. To display an icon for that menu, enter the Image URL, which is the file path for the image. For example, img/flatimages/svg/tiles/myself.svg. See ESS Menu Tile Images for a list of available images and the associated file path.

  6. Click Save to return to the ESS Menu Builder form. The new header displays.

  7. Repeat steps 1 through 6 to create a new menu, or click Save.

To edit header text:

  1. Click the edit icon [Image: screenshot] in the header box. The Edit Menu Header window opens. The fields are the same as in the Add a New Menu Structure form.

  2. Edit the any of the items as needed.

  3. Click Save.

Using Menu Items

Drag items from the Available Menu Items list to the rectangle below the appropriate header.

  1. Click the name of a menu header to expand it.

  2. Click and hold the available menu item you want to add.

  3. Drag the item to the rectangle under the menu header.

  4. Release the mouse button. The menu item now appears in the menu structure.

  5. Rearrange the sub-menu items by dragging and dropping them within the list.

  6. Click Save.

[Image: screenshot]

Establishing Codes for Event Types

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Events.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Events.htm

Establishing Codes for Event Types

You can establish codes for the types of events held by the client using the Events form.

To establish codes for the client’s event types, do the following:

  1. Go to the Events form on the Client|Change menu and complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Event — Enter or select an Event code that represents a type of client event. When creating a new code, use something brief but descriptive, for example, TR.

Event Classification — Enter an Event Classification that describes the type of event, for example, Training.

  1. Click Save.

Adjusting FUTA Credit Amounts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/FUTA%20Credit%20Adjustment%20Entry.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/FUTA Credit Adjustment Entry.htm

Adjusting FUTA Credit Amounts

Use the FUTA Credit Adjustment Entry form to appropriately adjust FUTA credit amounts to adjust FUTA Credit in the RTS without using tax adjustments for a client. The values within the RTS will reflect the adjustments made in this form. The FUTA Credit Adjustment Entry form will only accept employee IDs for the selected Client as this is a Client based form.

Note: Note:

  • Worksite Managers, Worksite Trusted Advisors, and Service Providers can access the form.

  • Client Access Group Security and Data Entity security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, click Client. Under Client|Change, click FUTA Credit Adjustment Entry.

To enter FUTA Credit Adjustment:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

Reporting Year — Select the year for which you want FUTA credit adjustment. By default the current year displays.

Employee ID — Enter the employee IDs for the client for whom you want to adjust FUTA credit or click the Employee ID and select them from the Select Employee dialog box. > Note: Note: Only employee for the selected client will appear in the dialog box. If you are manually entering employee IDs, you can only enter employee IDs for the selected client.

Employee Name — This field will auto-populate based on the selection above.

Year — This field will auto-populate based on the reporting year selected above.

State Tax Code — Enter the state tax code for example enter 05-17 for the state of California.

State — This field will auto-populate based on the selection above.

Wage Adjustment Amount — Enter numeric values only for tax adjustment amount as positive or negative to offset the FUTA Credit reduction amount within the FUTA Credit Register Build.

  1. Click Save to save the record. Adjustments to the xx-17 record will be saved and stored in the FUTA Credit Adjustment Entry grid for each employee.

  2. Click Clear to clear this form at any time.

Defining Hazardous Materials

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Hazardous_Materials.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Hazardous_Materials.htm

Defining Hazardous Materials

You can specify hazardous material information used by a company with the Hazardous Materials form.

To define hazardous materials, do the following:

  1. Go to the Hazardous Materials form on the Client|Change menu and complete the following fields:
Field Description
Material Code Enter or select the hazardous material code.
Material Name Enter the name of the material, which is found on the product label.
Manufacturer Enter the product’s manufacturer name.
Emergency Phone Enter a phone number to call if there is a hazardous material emergency.
SDS On File Select if there is a Safety Data Sheet on file.
Date Prepared Enter the date the Safety Data Sheet was prepared for this material.
SDS Location Enter the physical location of the Safety Data Sheet.
Course Code/Retake Days For each course that instructs employees in hazardous material handling: - Enter a code for each safety course that employees must take before handling this hazardous material. - If the course must be completed periodically, enter the number of days between completion.
  • Enter a code for each safety course that employees must take before handling this hazardous material.
  • If the course must be completed periodically, enter the number of days between completion.
  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Labor Union Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Labor_Unions.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Labor_Unions.htm

Maintaining Labor Union Information

Set up union codes, dues, fringe benefits, and union addresses with the Labor Unions form. You can allow assignment of union dues table rules to a unique union deduction code. When using Position Union overrides, you can apply multiple union deduction codes to an employee based on the timesheet sensitive position code entered and allocate deductions amounts appropriately. When using position based Union code overrides, you can differentiate between the union deductions without requiring the union applied to the employee directly.

Set Labor Union Code Parameters

Set up code parameters with the Labor Unions panel.

To set labor union code parameters:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Labor Unions.

  3. In the Labor Unions panel, complete these fields.

Field Name Description
Union Code Enter a new union code or select an existing code by clicking the Union Code link.
Union Name Enter the name of the union.
Union Bill Template Select a template for employees that belong to this union. For default bundled clients, this changes the billing amounts.
Union Deduction Code This table has the following columns: - Union Deduction Code: Enter a deduction code for reporting or click the Union Deduction Code hyperlink and select it from the Deduction Codes dialogue box. - Union Deduction Description: A description of the union deduction code displays here after a union deduction code is entered above. - Union Dues Table Rules: You can now have unique union deduction codes with the calculation of union dues table to differentiate between various union deductions allowing unique deductions for better visibility to clients and employees. - For a Union Deduction Code that does not have Union Dues Table set as the Calculation Method for that union deduction code on the Deduction Codes form, you will see the [Image: screenshot] icon. If you click the [Image: screenshot] icon, you will get an error message stating The deduction code has an invalid calculation method. The code must have a calculation method of Union Dues Table to be assigned union dues table rules. - For a Union Deduction Code that has Union Dues Table set as the Calculation Method for that union deduction code on the Deduction Codes form, you will see the [Image: screenshot] icon. When you click it, a new form named Included Union Dues Table Rules will open. This form allows you to associate specific Union Dues Table Rules with a specific Union Deduction Code. - On the Included Union Dues Table Rules form, you can: - Add any Union Dues Table Rules from the drop-down for the selected Union Deduction Code provided it is listed in the Union Dues Table below on the Labor Unions form. Click Accept to close the form. Click the Save button on the Labor Unions form to file the changes. - Delete any Union Dues Table Rules from the drop-down for the selected Union Deduction Code. Click Accept to close the form. Click the Save button on the Labor Unions form to file the changes. - If from the Included Union Dues Table Rules drop-down you are trying to select a rule that is not included in the Union Dues Table, you will get an error message stating Not allowed, this Rule and Rate is not included in the Union Dues Table. Click OK to acknowledge the error and redo your selection. - Periods: This refers to the deduction period which references the week of the month for the payroll. For instance, if your weekly Pay Date was 02-28-2025 which was a Friday and 02-28-2025 was the 4th Friday of February, so Deduction Period will be 4. The Periods column indicates this Deduction Period for the corresponding Union Deduction Code. - This column cannot be empty for any given Union Deduction Code. If you try to delete the Periods column for any Union Deduction Code and save the Labor Unions form, you will receive an error stating The Union period(s) must be specified. - The default value is 12345. You can edit appropriately for the corresponding Union Deduction Code. So if there is a union deduction override on the Labor Unions form that would apply to the employee’s recurring deduction record, then just removing the GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD on the Systems Parameters form will not reset it to the default value of 12345. Any employee that already has a recurring deduction record that is updated for a certain period will remain in place. Before removing the GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD on the Systems Parameters form, you need to update the labor union record itself if you intend to go back to all periods. So please ensure the labor Union deduction periods are accurate before removing the custom feature code. - You can enter multiple deduction periods once between 1-5 or O. O indicates a one-time deduction for the month. - You cannot enter the same deduction period twice for the same Union Deduction Code. If you try to enter the same deduction period twice for any Union Deduction Code and save the Labor Unions form, you will receive an error stating A period can only be entered once. Note: The GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD must be turned on for this field to display and being editable. If the GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD is turned off the Periods column will still display, but you cannot edit it.
  • Union Deduction Code: Enter a deduction code for reporting or click the Union Deduction Code hyperlink and select it from the Deduction Codes dialogue box.
  • Union Deduction Description: A description of the union deduction code displays here after a union deduction code is entered above.
  • Union Dues Table Rules: You can now have unique union deduction codes with the calculation of union dues table to differentiate between various union deductions allowing unique deductions for better visibility to clients and employees.
  • For a Union Deduction Code that does not have Union Dues Table set as the Calculation Method for that union deduction code on the Deduction Codes form, you will see the [Image: screenshot] icon. If you click the [Image: screenshot] icon, you will get an error message stating The deduction code has an invalid calculation method. The code must have a calculation method of Union Dues Table to be assigned union dues table rules.
  • For a Union Deduction Code that has Union Dues Table set as the Calculation Method for that union deduction code on the Deduction Codes form, you will see the [Image: screenshot] icon. When you click it, a new form named Included Union Dues Table Rules will open. This form allows you to associate specific Union Dues Table Rules with a specific Union Deduction Code.
  • On the Included Union Dues Table Rules form, you can:
  • Add any Union Dues Table Rules from the drop-down for the selected Union Deduction Code provided it is listed in the Union Dues Table below on the Labor Unions form. Click Accept to close the form. Click the Save button on the Labor Unions form to file the changes.
  • Delete any Union Dues Table Rules from the drop-down for the selected Union Deduction Code. Click Accept to close the form. Click the Save button on the Labor Unions form to file the changes.
  • If from the Included Union Dues Table Rules drop-down you are trying to select a rule that is not included in the Union Dues Table, you will get an error message stating Not allowed, this Rule and Rate is not included in the Union Dues Table. Click OK to acknowledge the error and redo your selection.
  • Periods: This refers to the deduction period which references the week of the month for the payroll. For instance, if your weekly Pay Date was 02-28-2025 which was a Friday and 02-28-2025 was the 4th Friday of February, so Deduction Period will be 4. The Periods column indicates this Deduction Period for the corresponding Union Deduction Code.
  • This column cannot be empty for any given Union Deduction Code. If you try to delete the Periods column for any Union Deduction Code and save the Labor Unions form, you will receive an error stating The Union period(s) must be specified.
  • The default value is 12345. You can edit appropriately for the corresponding Union Deduction Code. So if there is a union deduction override on the Labor Unions form that would apply to the employee’s recurring deduction record, then just removing the GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD on the Systems Parameters form will not reset it to the default value of 12345. Any employee that already has a recurring deduction record that is updated for a certain period will remain in place. Before removing the GLOBAL custom feature code UNIONDEDPERIOD on the Systems Parameters form, you need to update the labor union record itself if you intend to go back to all periods. So please ensure the labor Union deduction periods are accurate before removing the custom feature code.
  • You can enter multiple deduction periods once between 1-5 or O. O indicates a one-time deduction for the month.
  • You cannot enter the same deduction period twice for the same Union Deduction Code. If you try to enter the same deduction period twice for any Union Deduction Code and save the Labor Unions form, you will receive an error stating A period can only be entered once. | Custom Option Code | Do not use this field unless directed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team. In that case, enter the custom code provided to you in this field. | ## Entering Union Dues

Set up union dues with the Union Dues Table panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name — Description

Rule — Select a rule to calculate union dues. For example, Fixed Amount charges the same amount for each pay period.

Rate/Perc — Optional: Enter a rate/percentage to calculate dues if either the Percentage of Wages or Rate x Hours Worked rule was selected.

Rate Group — Enter a rate group if the rule line should be grouped with others for performing calculations. Any Cap or Min/Max should point to this grouping code.

Rate Ge — If the rule applies to rates between two points, enter that level in the Rate Ge (greater than or equal to) field.

Rate Le — If the rule applies to rates that are less than or equal to a certain amount, enter the amount in the Rate Le field.

Rule Start — Enter the rule start date. Leave this blank to put the rule into effect immediately.

Rule End — Enter the rule end date, if any. Leave this blank if the rule never expires.

Pay Codes Included — To specify the pay codes that are subject to this rule, click the Edit icon [Image: screenshot] in Pay Codes Included; see Including Pay Codes Maintenance for Labor Unions. If you add a rule on the Union Dues Table which does not use pay codes, you will get the [Image: screenshot] icon for that rule under the Pay Codes Included column. When you click the [Image: screenshot] icon, you will get an error message stating This rule does not use pay codes.

Pay Codes Excluded — To specify the pay codes that are not subject to this rule, click the Edit icon [Image: screenshot] in Pay Codes Excluded; see Excluding Pay Codes Maintenance for Labor Unions. If you add a rule on the Union Dues Table which does not use pay codes, you will get the [Image: screenshot] icon for that rule under the Pay Codes Excluded column also. When you click the [Image: screenshot] icon, you will get an error message stating This rule does not use pay codes.

From the Union Dues Table on the Labor Unions form, you cannot delete a rule if it is associated with a Union Deductions Code on the Labor Unions form. After deleting, when you click Save on the Labor Unions form, you will get an error message stating This rule is assigned to Union Deduction Code, and must be removed prior to removing from the Union Dues Table. You must delete the Union Deductions Code first to be able to delete any associate rule from the Union Dues Table on the Labor Unions form.

OR

Using the Included Union Dues Table Rules form noted above, if you remove the rule assigned to any Union Deduction Code on the Labor Unions form for the Union Code, you can remove the rule from the Union Dues Table.

Maintaining Union Benefits

Set up union benefits in the Fringe Benefits panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name Description
Pay Code Select a code to pay a fringe benefit. Note: An error message displays if you enter an invalid pay code, “The pay code has an invalid calculation method. Please see the in product help for valid calculation methods for this process.” To prevent this error, only select one of the following calculation methods when applying the pay code: - NRE: % of Gross Earnings - NRH: Rate X Hours Worked - NRHFW: Rate X Full Hours Worked - NRHP: Rate X Hours Paid - NRHPS: Rate X Hours Paid (selected codes) - NRHSW: Rate X Straight Hours Worked
  • NRE: % of Gross Earnings
  • NRH: Rate X Hours Worked
  • NRHFW: Rate X Full Hours Worked
  • NRHP: Rate X Hours Paid
  • NRHPS: Rate X Hours Paid (selected codes)
  • NRHSW: Rate X Straight Hours Worked | Rate | Enter a rate for this fringe benefit. The value multiplied against this rate is determined by the calculation method defined on the pay code. | | Start Date | Pay date when the benefit starts. | | Stop Date | Pay date when the benefit ends. | ## Maintaining Union Address and Contact Information

Set address and contact information with the Union Address panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name Description
Address Line 1 and 2 Enter an address for the union, using two lines if required.
City Enter the city where the union is located.
State Enter the state where the union is located.
Zip Code Enter the zip code for the location of the union.
Phone Enter a phone number, as well any extensions or directives.
Contact Enter a contact name for the union.

  1. Click Save to save changes to the record.

  2. Click Delete to delete a record.

  3. Click Cancel to clear the form.

Setting Up Manager Users

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Manager_User_Setup.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Manager_User_Setup.htm

Setting Up Manager Users

You can use the Manager User Setup form to set up other worksite managers within their organization, who will be accessed in this form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • This form is only available for service providers and worksite managers. In addition, worksite managers must have access to the worksite managers they are setting up.

  • Information entered in this form is client-specific, so you can only set up a manager for the current client where you have access.

  • If the worksite manager has access to multiple clients and you try to set them up an error message displays, “This user has access to more than one client and needs to be managed under the System User form.”

  • If you enter a user ID that does not exist, the system automatically populates the User Type as Worksite Manager.

To set up worksite manager users:

  1. Perform the following setups:
  1. — On the Client Security form select User Roles and Human Resources Roles that will be allowed for the current client. (These will be the only roles that are available on the Manager User Setup form for worksite managers to assign to worksite managers they are creating.)
b. On the Users form, enable the Manager User Setup Human Resources Role.
2. Go to the Manager User Setup form on the Client Change menu.
  1. Do one of the following:

  1. — Select the User ID of an existing worksite manager or worksite employee to setup as a worksite manager user. The form populates the user’s information.

  2. — Enter a newUser ID, which automatically sets the User Type to Worksite Manager. (Continue entering information pertaining to the new user.)

Note: Note the following:

  • A message displays if you select an ID whose roles are not set at the client level, “User <ID> has Roles assigned that are not set at the client level. The user must be accessed with the User Maintenance screen.” To resolve this issue, you must assign roles that you will have access to in the Client Security form before you can set up users. (Access this form by selecting Security on the Actions menu in the Client Details form.)

  • If you select a worksite employee a message displays, “Do you wish to promote this user to a Worksite Manager?” Click Yes to promote that employee. (The form populates with the employee’s information and the User Type is automatically set to Worksite Manager.)

  1. Select Requires New Password to set a new password for an existing user or for a new user.

  2. Select an MFA option in the sMFA Device Expiration field.

  3. Define the User Role ID and the Human Resources Role to indicate which roles the worksite manager user is allowed to access.

Note: You must assign at least one user role to save the record.

  1. Select Add Client to define the Data Security entities for the client. The entities display in the Data Entity Security panel.

Note: Only the clients where you have access display. If you have access to multiple clients, you must select Add Company to manage the list of clients. (You must have access to at least one client to save the record.) Also, you can delete a client by selecting the icon in the Delete column.

  1. Do the following in the Data Entity Security panel:
  1. — Select an option in the Security by field to configure a user’s Data Security access hierarchy, so you can easily manage which employees the user can access:

  • Entity: User is granted access to all entries.

  • Manager Direct Reports: User is only granted access to their direct reports.

  • Manager Full Hierarchy: User is granted access to their direct reports and any employees that are assigned to or inherited by their direct reports.

  • PTO Approver Direct Reports: User is only granted access to their direct reports.

  • PTO Approver Full Hierarchy: User is granted access to their direct reports and any employees that are assigned to or inherited by their direct reports.

  1. — Select an entity to change the access. For example, if Full Access displays, selecting it changes the access to No Access.
  1. Click Save.

Using the Actions Menu Options

The Manager User Setup form provides additional options on the Actions menu:

  • Change to a Worksite Employee: Displays a message, “Are you sure you want to change the User Type for <user ID> to a Worksite Employee?” Click Yes to change the selected worksite manager to a worksite employee. (This option is unavailable when the user role is set to INQUIRY.)

  • Client Device IP Range: Displays the IP Restrictions form where you can restrict the selected user to a specific IP address or range of addresses.

Maintaining Mileage Rates

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Mileage_Rates.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Mileage_Rates.htm

Maintaining Mileage Rates

Set mileage codes and rates used in daily allowance of vehicle mileage calculations with the Mileage Rates form.

To set mileage rates:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Mileage Rates.

  3. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Mileage Code — Enter a mileage code.

Description — Enter a mileage rate description.

Mileage Rate — Enter the mileage rate, which is the gross amount paid per mile

Per Diem Rate — Enter the per diem rate, which is the portion of the total rate that makes up the non-taxable daily allowance.

Taxable Rate — The Taxable Rate is the difference between the mileage and per diem rates.

  1. Click Save.

Overriding Global Required New Hire and Employee Dependent Fields

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/New_Hire_Optional_Fields_Maint.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/New_Hire_Optional_Fields_Maint.htm

Overriding Global Required New Hire and Employee Dependent Fields

Based on the needs of the client, you can indicate which fields on the New Hire and Employee Dependents forms are required, if any, by using the New Hire and Dependent Field Overrides form. The settings you configure here override the settings configured on the New Hire and Dependent Required Fields (Global) form for the specific client.

Note: Note: The New Hire and Dependent Field Overrides form indicates whether the client uses global settings exclusively or uses custom settings (which may be a mixture of overrides and global settings) by displaying a message at the top of the New Hire and Dependent Field Overrides form above the New Hires table. Additionally, the Global Setting column displays the global settings defined on the New Hire and Dependent Required Fields (Global) form (for reference only) as shown in the example below.

[Image: screenshot]

To indicate required fields for the New Hire and Employee Dependents forms at the client level:

  1. Go to the New Hire and Dependent Field Overrides form on the Client|Action menu.

  2. For each of the New Hires fields, indicate whether it is mandatory:

  • Required for Standard New Hire - Client Setting column: Indicates whether the field is required when the client enters new hire information without electronic onboarding.

  • Required for Onboarded New Hire - Client Setting column: Indicates whether the field is required when the client has their new hires onboard electronically.

  1. For each of the Dependents fields, indicate whether it is mandatory:

  • Required for Dependent - Client Setting column: Indicates whether the field is required for data entry when the client completes the Employee Dependents form.

  • Required for Dependent - Global Setting column: Indicates whether the field is required for data entry when the client completes the Employee Dependents form at a global level.

  1. Click Save.

Note: Clicking Cancel while editing this form reverts the form to the state it was initially in when you first accessed the form (if you have performed no Save actions) OR reverts the form to the state it was in after the most recent Save action (so that only unsaved changes are removed).

Defining Pay Grades

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Pay_Grades.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Pay_Grades.htm

Defining Pay Grades

Pay grades are a type of earning or payroll disbursement. Set pay range information for a pay grade with the Pay Grades form.

To set pay grades:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Change select Pay Grades.

  3. Fill in these fields as required:

Field — Description

Pay Grade — Enter a pay grade code. Use something brief but descriptive, such as REG for Regular or OT for overtime.

Description — Enter a pay grade description.

Minimum Annual — Enter a minimum annual pay amount.

Maximum Annual — Enter a maximum annual pay amount.

  1. PrismHR calculates the other values:

  • Minimum (Hourly): The minimum hourly pay, based on the minimum annual pay.

  • Maximum (Hourly): The maximum hourly pay, based on the maximum annual pay.

  • Midpoint: The calculated midpoint annual pay.

Formula=(((Maximum – Minimum) / 2) + Minimum)

  • Percentage Range: The percentage range for the Midpoint value.

Formula= (((Maximum - Minimum) / Minimum) * 100)

  1. Click Save.

Payroll Accounting

Use the Payroll Accounting panel in ASO Global G/L Account Setup to maintain ASO client accounting for pay codes.

  2. — Enter the Overtime Debit Account to debit with any overtime amount (premium portion only) for all pay codes. The account name displays.

  1. If any pay codes should post to different accounts:
a. Enter each Except Pay Code. The Pay Code Description displays.

  1. — Enter the Debit Account to debit with any amount paid for the pay code. The Account Name displays.

  2. — Enter the Overtime Debit Account to debit with any overtime amount (premium portion only) for each pay code. The Account Name displays.

  1. Click Save.

Working with PEO Client Accounting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PEO_Client_Accounting_Setup.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PEO_Client_Accounting_Setup.htm

Working with PEO Client Accounting

You can define the accounts that PrismHR uses to build the client accounting download file and the report that breaks out invoice numbers using the client’s own account numbers. To do this, enable the settings on the PEO Client Accounting form using the following instructions.

Note: Note: If you are building onto a global template, enter only differences or additions. The system uses the data from the global template for any field you leave blank. See Setting Up PEO Client Accounting for general information about the PEO Client Accounting process.

Establishing the General Ledger Chart of Accounts

Use the PEO Client Accounting panel to establish the general ledger Chart of Accounts and various accounting system parameters used for this client. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To establish the general ledger Chart of Accounts:

  1. Go to the PEO Client Accounting form and complete the following fields:
Field Description
Account Template Enter the client’s Account Template ID.
Client Accounting System Select the Client Accounting System type.
QuickBooks Billing Template If you have been instructed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team, enter the QuickBooks Billing Template used for billing export. The QuickBooks Billing Template field is for Quick Book Extracts custom logic implementation. The system uses this instead of the Accounting Template. Use this field.
Global Account Template Enter the Global Account Template used for this client.
Name of Output File Enter the Name of Output File to specify the name of the file that the system creates for the client accounting download, if any. If you define an output file, PrismHR creates the file automatically during payroll processing and users can download it from the Payroll Reports form or the Payroll Summary form. Note: Do not use the following invalid characters when naming the output file: “*/:<>?\ . Instead, you can use uppercase A-Z, lowercase a-z, and a space.
Company Offset Account Enter the Company Offset Account number used to handle the accounting offset for this client.
Exclude Non-Cash Pay Codes from Tax Allocation Base Select to exclude non-cash fringe benefit pay codes from the tax allocation base. For example, $0 allocation lines are not included.
Earn Code/Earn Account Format For each pay code that should use a different format: a. — Enter a pay code in the Earn Code field to build an accounting format. You can also enter an asterisk (*), which indicates “all remaining” pay codes. The asterisk must be the last item on the list, if used; the system ignores all items listed after the asterisk row. b. — Enter the Earn Account Format. This overrides any format defined in the template and the common format.

Using Billing Codes

Using PEO Client Accounting Wildcards

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PEO_Client_Accounting_Wildcards.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PEO_Client_Accounting_Wildcards.htm

Using PEO Client Accounting Wildcards

Since you use the accounts set up in PEO Client Accounting Setup as a global template for all clients, fill in the specified Code and Account Format fields. PrismHR uses the four Common {type} Earn Account entry boxes to associate a general ledger account number with a PrismHR code.

Your use of a delimiter (such as a period, asterisk, or dash) in the Common {type} Earn Account fields depends on your client’s preference or accounting package. If there is a wildcard substitution indicated, but the code’s G/L code segment is blank, the system uses the actual code instead.

You can use the following wildcards in the Common {type} Earn Account fields:

Wildcard Description
CLBROKER Client Broker*
CLCC Client Cost Center
CLGRPCD Client Group Code*
CLID Client ID*
CLNAISC Client NAISC*
CLSALES Client Sales Person*
CLSCC Client Sub Cost Center
CLSTATE Client State*
CLSVRT Client Service Type*
CLWC Client WC Policy*
EEDP Employee Department
EEDP2 Employee Department Two
EEDV Employee Division
EEDV2 Employee Division Two
EEDV3 Employee Division Three
EEJB Employee Position
EEJB2 Employee Position Two
EEJB3 Employee Position Three
EELCC Employee Location Code
EELC Employee Location
EELC2 Employee Location Two
EELC3 Employee Location Three
EEPR Employee Project
EESCC Employee Sub Cost Center
EMCC Employer Cost Center
EMID Employer ID*
EMSCC Employer Sub Cost Center
MSCL01 - MSCL99 Client Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99
EEMDP01 - EEMDP99 Employee Department Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99
EEMDV01 - EEMDV99 Employee Division Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99
EEMJB01 - EEMJB99 Employee Position Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99
EEMLPJ01 - EEMLPJ99 Employee Project Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99
EEMLC01 - EEMLC99 Employee Worksite Location Miscellaneous GL Codes 01-99

Note: Note that the following information only applies to the wildcards indicated above with an asterisk (*) in its Description column:

  • External Journal Post (located on Operations|Action menu): After applying the wildcard values within the G/L Setup, the system presents the substituted variable on the External Journal Post.

  • External Invoice Post (located on Operations|Action menu): After applying the wildcard values within the G/L Setup, the system presents the substituted variable on the External Invoice Post.

  • External Third Party Checks Journal Post (located on Operations|Action menu): After applying the wildcard values within the G/L Setup, the system presents the substituted variable on the External Third Party Checks Journal Post.

  • Regenerate Payroll Cost Accounting (located on Operations|Action menu): After adding or changing the wildcards within the G/L Setup, the system applies them during the regenerate cost accounting process.

  • Regenerate Invoice Accounting (located on Operations|Action menu): After adding or changing the wildcards within the G/L Setup, the system applies them during the regenerate invoice accounting process.

Defining Positions

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Positions.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Positions.htm

Defining Positions

You can define position-related information for the client using the Positions form.

To access this form:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Positions.

Click the Import button

[Image: screenshot]

to display the Data Import Tool form, which allows you to import a Position Codes template file that contains the following fields (this file must be in .txt format):

  • Position Code (Required)

  • Position Title (Required)

  • Position Classification (Required): An error displays if the imported code does not exist on the Position Classifications form (accessed from the System|Change menu)

  • Supervisory: Accepted values are Y (True) or N (False)

  • Tipped Position: Accepted values are Y (True) or N (False)

  • G/L Segment Code 1

  • G/L Segment Code 2

  • G/L Segment Code 3

Note: Note the following:

  • The Import button displays on the Positions form for all Service Provider user roles except the “Deny” and “Inquiry-only” roles. Imports can be performed for multiple clients at the operations level.

  • For more information about the Data Import Tool form, see the PrismHR Payroll Administration Guide.

Defining Position Information and Requirements

You can define basic position information and requirements using the Positions panel.

Note: Note: If the position exists in several departments, use a job code that easily identifies the department in which the position is assigned.

Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Position Code — Enter or select a unique code that briefly describes the position. For example, OPER for vehicle operator. (Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters.) If you select this field, a selector form displays where you can search for a specific position code, title, or classification or select a code from the list that displays. When this form opens, the position code, its title, classification, FLSA exempt status, and obsolete status display. To filter the search further by displaying only obsolete codes, select Yes in the Obsolete field (the default is No). To refresh the list, select Reset Search and Filters.

Position Title — Enter an official title. For example, General Manager.

Position Classification — Enter the position classification. The system displays the FLSA Exempt settings, EEO position group, and the EEO class, if they apply to this position. FLSA Exempt indicates whether the selected position classification is set to be exempt from both the minimum wage and overtime provisions of the FLSA. You cannot change it here.

Remote Position Classification — Enter an alternative position classification for remote employees of the same position. The system displays the FLSA Exempt settings, EEO position group, and the EEO class, if they apply to this remote position. FLSA Exempt indicates whether the selected remote position classification is set to be exempt from both the minimum wage and overtime provisions of the FLSA. You cannot change it here.

Position Description — Enter a description for this position.

The second panel defines requirements for the position and the pay.

Supervisory — Select if this is a supervisory position. The system uses this field for statistical reports.

Hierarchy Tier — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

Pay Grade — Select to use as a default for new hires assigned this job code, if applicable. The Special Requirements option identifies those positions that have physical or mental requirements. The Americans with Disabilities Act prohibits employers from discriminating against qualified individuals with disabilities. Not including a particular person in the applicant pool for a certain job is only nondiscriminatory if that person does not meet any physical or mental requirements identified as essential functions of the position. Applications for these jobs must be closely monitored for ADA compliance.

Special Requirements — Select if this position requires any special physical or mental requirements.

Probation Days — Optional: Enter the number of days from hire that a new employee is on probation.

Sales Position — Select if this is a sales position.

Tipped Position — Select if this is for a position that receives tips.

D.O.T. Requirements — Select if this job code has special requirements as set forth by the U.S. Department of Transportation. The G/L Segment Code can be used as an alternative to this position when using G/L Accounting. See the PEO Client Company Accounting Task Sheet on the Customer Resource Center for more information.

G/L Segment Code — If applicable, enter the G/L Segment Code 1, G/L Segment Code 2, or G/L Segment Code 3 to use as the general ledger account number segment(s) in place of the Position Code. These are defined in PEO client accounting wild cards as EEJB, EEJB2, and EEJB3.

Defining Codes and Other Pay Information

You can define codes and other pay information for the position in this panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Description
Union Code Override Select this override for table-based union calculations. If an override is not selected, the system uses a union code in Employee Details, if any.
PR Chauffeur Tax Applies Select if this position is subject to Puerto Rico chauffeur’s tax and the primary work location is Puerto Rico.
Indirectly Tipped Select if this a tipped position that receives tips indirectly. For example, a busboy, cook, or service bartender.
Alaska Position Code If applicable, enter the Alaska Position Code sent to MasterTax for quarterly tax reporting to the state of Alaska.
SOC Code Enter the Standard Occupational Classification (SOC) Code, if appropriate. Enter only numbers, no dashes. This information is included in the MasterTax RTS file and the Employer and Client LA quarterly files in place of the position’s description. If you do not define a SOC code for the position, the files include the description.
TTO Code Define which positions are considered eligible tipped positions to comply with the tips requirements of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act. You can enter the TTO Code (Tipped Taxable Occupation Code) manually or you can click the TTO Code hyperlink and select it from the TTO Codes dialog box. The TTO Description displays upon entering a valid TTO Code. The system will validate when you enter a TTO Code against the SOC Code during the creation or updation of a Position. - Invalid TTO/SOC combinations result in an error message: TTO code [] and SOC code [] are not related. - Invalid TTO code results in an error message: TTO Code [] does not exist. - Invalid entries cannot be saved. - Valid TTO code entry is allowed when the SOC code is blank.
  • Invalid TTO/SOC combinations result in an error message: TTO code [] and SOC code [] are not related.
  • Invalid TTO code results in an error message: TTO Code [] does not exist.
  • Invalid entries cannot be saved.
  • Valid TTO code entry is allowed when the SOC code is blank. | Indiana Seasonal Employee Code | Enter a two-digit code if the position is seasonal and located in Indiana. This code is required for SUI electronic filing in Indiana. | | Prevailing Wage | Enter the Prevailing Wage for this position. This can print on pay stubs as a pay detail field. | | Price Code | Select a price code that overrides the Bundled Rate for any employee who has been assigned to this position. Note: Only use Price Code with Bundled Billing if an employee is granted special pricing rather than using the standard bundled rate and with an employee-specific add-on unbundled rule. | | Per Diem Percent | Enter this percentage to allocate to non-taxable per diem when paid using the percentage method. | | W/C Time Sheet Loc Based | Select to calculate workers’ compensation accruals for each employee’s actual work location. This overrides the setting in Client Details. | | Obsolete | Select if this position is no longer used. | | Suppress Work State Tax | Select if employees in this position are subject to taxes in their state of residence rather than state of work. During calculations, the system will use the employee’s residence for the basis of state taxes. | | Benefit Group | Assign a default benefit group for employees hired in this position. | | Agricultural Worker | Select if the employee is an agricultural worker and the employee’s income will be reported to the IRS on Form 943. (Note: All employees assigned to this position are auto-assigned this designation during the new hire process.) | ## Tracking Hazardous Materials

You can track any hazardous materials associated with this position using the Hazardous Material Exposure panel. Enter each hazardous materials Material Code that people in this position encounter.

Working with Miscellaneous GL Segments

Use the Misc GL panel to review Misc GL Counts (predefined wildcards) for this position and enter replacement Misc GL Segments.

Note: During payroll processing, the system substitutes the GL segments for wildcards in GL account codes. These wildcards must also be included in the following setups:

  • GL setup, which is processed into payroll accounting. (You can configure them in the G/L Setup form.)

  • PEO client accounting setup, which is processed into client accounting. (You can configure them in the PEO Client Accounting form.)

When working with miscellaneous GL segments:

  1. The predefined wildcards display in rows in the Misc GL Count column, where the codes are always EEMJB01 through EEMJB99. (The program that evaluates the wildcards only looks for those values.)

  2. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMJB01 = Segment 1 and EEMJB02 = Segment 2. (You can apply these misc segments to account codes for other areas besides pay, such as taxes or deductions.)

Important! Any changes made to the descriptions affect all positions in the active client.

Note: The Misc GL Description column’s values are for reference only. (These descriptions are stored in the client’s record.) The system automatically loads these values each time a position record displays.

  1. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Segment value that replaces the Misc GL Count wildcard.

Note: When the system evaluates the Misc GL Segments, they are always based on the “home” job, which is different from the way segments are evaluated for departments (that is, for account codes for payment, the system uses the timesheet “worked-for” department).

  1. Click Save.

Maintaining ADA Parameters

You can maintain the American with Disabilities Act (ADA) parameters associated with this position using the ADA Position Description panel. Under the ADA, written job descriptions can be considered evidence of essential job functions, if they are accurate.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name Description
Last Updated Enter the date when the position description was last updated.
ADA Review Date Enter the date when the position was last reviewed for compliance with ADA standards.
ADA Reviewer’s Name Enter the name of the person who performed the ADA review.

Defining Position Code Cost/Bill Information

You can define pay codes, pay rates, and bill rates associated with this position using the Position Code Cost/Bill Maintenance panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name — Description

Pay Code — Enter each pay code to which rates apply.

Pay Rate — Enter the pay rate that the client pays for this position. If left blank, the system uses an employee’s standard pay. The pay rate might be overridden by a value set in Employee Details.

Bill Rate — Enter the rate to bill for the pay code. This bill rate might be overridden by a value set in Employee Details.

Override Method — Select the override method to indicate whether the code is used as a rate or markup. This overrides the standard client selection.

Defining Skills

You can define the skills associated with this position using the Required Skills panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field Name Description
Skill Code Enter each code for the skills required for employees in this position.
Competency Level Enter the required competency level.
Recertify Days Enter the interval for recertification.

Defining Hours Worked

You can define the hours worked for this position using the Equivalent Hours Worked panel.

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field Name — Description

Autofill Hours — Enter the default number of equivalent hours to use. Click Autofill to enter that value for all pay code Hours in the table.

Pay Code — Enter each pay code to define equivalent hours for this position. You must set the pay reason for these pay codes to Units.

Hours — Enter the equivalent number of hours worked when paying an employee for this position with the pay code.

  1. Click Save.

Using Print Service Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Print_Service_Options.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Print_Service_Options.htm

Using Print Service Options

Use this form to set up check printing for the client with the Print Service Options form.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select Print Service Options from the Actions menu.

  4. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Number Of Days Before Check Is Void Enter a value between -1 and 180. Enter 0 if the void information should not print on checks. Enter -1 to use the system default.
SSN Print Option (Checks Only) Set up a printing format for displaying SSN on check stubs. You can suppress printing, display the full SSN, or mask all but the last four digits.
Check Print Form Order Formats printing of multi-form checks when building mvQuery check reports.
Check Stub Header Line Format mvQuery check format. Default setting is Client Name, Location, ID. Other options include Client Name or Employer Name.
Benefit Print Mode Displays the desired benefits on checks. Options are not printing benefits, printing retirement benefits only, or printing all benefits.
Sort Field 1, Sort Field 2 Select sorting order on checks. For example, Sort Field 1 can be set to EE Home Department and Sort Field 2 can be set to EE Home Shift.
Separate Check Print File If Sort Field 1 is set to EE Home Division, the Separate Check Print File field displays. Select this field to generate a separate check print file for each home division when payroll is processed. When you enable the Separate Check Print File field and process a payroll that includes employees with multiple, different home divisions, the system produces a check print file for each home division in the payroll reports labeled with the Print Service Check Report Name, the number of checks, the division code, a dash (-), and the division name. For example, “Regular Paychecks 2 Division NW1 - Northwest.” However, if the employee does not have an assigned home division (blank/null) the system labels the check print file with the Print Service Check Report Name, the number of checks, and “No Home Division.” For example, “Regular Paychecks 11 No Home Division.” Note: You do not need to set the Print Deposit Advices Separately field to Yes to use this feature because the separate Deposit Advices file is not expected to be divided by Employee Home Division (only the live checks that occur).
Source of Name/Address on Checks Determines where contact information is retrieved from to include on checks. Options are client address, employer address, or checking account address. This overrides the setting in System Parameters.
Print Full Address Only On Check Stub Select Yes to only include the full address on check stubs. Select No to include all information, including the name of the client.
Suppress Rate/Hours On Salaried Check Stub Sets whether hourly rate and hours worked will print on check stubs for salaried workers.
Current Details Hours To Use On Check Stub Determines whether hours worked, hours paid, both hours worked and paid, or units paid are listed on check stubs.
Current Earnings Sort Order On Check Stub Sets the default format for sorting current pay information on check stubs.
Check Pay Detail Summary Format Determines whether pay details are summarized by code, pay rate, or other factors.
Check Ytd Information With Earnings Specifies whether custom mvQuery checks should show current pay and YTD information in the same area of the check stub.
Include Sort Information In Check Address Window Displays check sort information above employee information in the check address window.
Include Memo Pay Codes In Current Earnings Section Select Yes to display memo pay codes in the current pay section.
Exclude suppressed Pay Codes in Gross Pay on Checks? When on the Pay Codes form, the Print on Paystub field is set to NO, Suppress Printing on Pay Stub, the following grid illustrates the functionality of this field: [Image: screenshot]
Use Benefit Plan Description For Deductions With Benefits Includes the benefit deduction description instead of the code on employee pay stubs.
Display Employee Pay Method With Current Earnings Select Yes to print an employee’s pay method in the Current Earnings section of their check.
Pay Detail Select up to two pay detail field names to print on employee pay stubs for this client. Enter a Column Name to print on the pay checks for the pay detail value. > Note: Note the following: - When you set the project parameter with its prevailing wage, if the prevailing wage for the project is greater than the prevailing wage for the position, the check stub displays the prevailing wage from the project. - You can enter anything in the Column Name field, but the check format might not support many characters. Re-print a check to test whether the column names display correctly.
  • When you set the project parameter with its prevailing wage, if the prevailing wage for the project is greater than the prevailing wage for the position, the check stub displays the prevailing wage from the project.
  • You can enter anything in the Column Name field, but the check format might not support many characters. Re-print a check to test whether the column names display correctly. | Check Options | Controls what is printed on checks. You can suppress values, include values that should print, add page breaks, and so on. | | Print Service Check Report Name | Selects a format for printing checks. For example, you can select Regular ACH Paychecks as a format. | | Print Service Garnishment Check Report Name | Selects a format for printing garnishment checks. For example, you can select New HRP Garnishment Check Series as a format. | | Print Deposit Advices Separately | Creates separate print files for ACH checks and regular checks. | | ACH Report Name | Use this option to print ACH checks separately and select an mvQuery. | | Check File (PDF) Password | Set a password to secure the PDF check file. | | Check Print Service Logo Filepath | Set a complete path to the location on the mvQuery print server for check logo images. For example, C:.gif. | | Check Print Service Signature Filepath | Set a complete path to the location on the mvQuery print server for check signature images. For example, C:.gif. | | Check Envelope Fields | Select fields to print outside the envelope area of checks. This option is only used for duplex and legal formats. |
  1. Click Accept.

Defining the Project

Define projects with the Projects panel.

To define the project, do the following:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Projects.

  3. Complete these fields as required.

Field Description
Project Enter or select a unique project code. If you select this field, a selector form displays where you can search for a specific project code or its description or select a code from the list that displays. When this form opens, the project code, its description, certified payroll status, and obsolete status display. To filter the search further: - Select a Certified Payroll status (the default is All). - Display only Obsolete codes by selecting Yes (the default is No). To refresh the list, select Reset Search and Filters.
  • Select a Certified Payroll status (the default is All).
  • Display only Obsolete codes by selecting Yes (the default is No). | Description | Enter a project description. | | Certified Payroll | Select if this project is subject to certified payroll regulations. The Certified Payroll Details, Certified Pay Rates, and Certified Payroll Report Setup panels display. Enter all relevant information. | | G/L Segment | Enter any segment codes used to build general ledger account numbers: - In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as an alternative to the Project Code. - In G/L Segment 2, enter the G/L code that substitutes for EEPR2 in G/L code routines. - In G/L Segment 3, enter a third G/L code, if needed. Note: Only G/L Segment 1 is available for ASO Client Accounting using wild card EEPR. |
  • In G/L Segment 1, enter the code to use as an alternative to the Project Code.
  • In G/L Segment 2, enter the G/L code that substitutes for EEPR2 in G/L code routines.
  • In G/L Segment 3, enter a third G/L code, if needed.
  1. Click Save.

Working with Miscellaneous GL Segments

Use the Misc GL Count, Misc GL Description, and Misc GL Segment fields to review Misc GL Counts (predefined wildcards) for this project and enter replacement Misc GL Segments.

Note: During payroll processing, the system substitutes the GL segments for wildcards in GL account codes. These wildcards must also be included in the following setups:

  • GL setup, which is processed into payroll accounting. (You can configure them in the G/L Setup form.)

  • PEO client accounting setup, which is processed into client accounting. (You can configure them in the PEO Client Accounting form.)

When working with miscellaneous GL segments:

  1. The predefined wildcards display in rows in the Misc GL Count column, where the codes are always EEMLPJ01 through EEMLPJ99. (The program that evaluates the wildcards only looks for those values.)

  2. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Description. For example, EEMLPJ01 = Segment 1 and EEMLPJ02 = Segment 2. (You can apply these misc segments to account codes for other areas besides pay, such as taxes or deductions.)

Important! Any changes made to the descriptions affect all projects in the active client.

Note: The Misc GL Description column’s values are for reference only. (These descriptions are stored in the client’s record.) The system automatically loads these values each time a project record displays.

  1. If appropriate, enter the Misc GL Segment value that replaces the Misc GL Count wildcard.

  2. Click Save.

Enter other project information with the Options panel.

Field — Description

Track Phases — Select if the phases of this project are tracked for job costing purposes.

Project Class — Enter a class code for the project.

Other W/C Coverage In Place — Select if Workers’ Compensation Insurance Coverage has been established for this project. Note: This field only displays if you enable specific custom suffix codes.

Obsolete — Select if this project is no longer used.

Union Project — Select if this project is associated with a union. When you select this option, the Union Code field displays and you can enter the union that owns the project.

  1. Click Save.

Defining the Certified Payroll Information

Use three panels to define certified payroll information for projects that are subject to certified payroll regulations.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Certified Payroll Report Format Select a report format. For example, select MA, or Massachusetts mvQuery.

Wage Decision Date Enter a date for wage decision.
Project City Enter the city where the certified project is located.
Project State Enter the state where the certified project is located.
  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Pay Rates

Set the pay rates for each employee that works on a certified project in the Certified Pay Rates panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Job Code — Enter each job code (position) associated with this certified project.

Base Rate — Enter the portion of the job rate used to calculate the overtime rate. Note: In addition to the Base Rate, the system adds the Fringe Rate (see below) to the overtime calculation according to the system design for the certified payroll.

Fringe Rate — Enter the fringe portion of the job rate.

Prevailing Wage Rate — The Prevailing Wage Rate is calculated based on the Base Rate and Fringe Rate values.

Use Higher of Prevailing Wage Rate or Employee’s Pay Rate — Select if the system should use whichever rate is higher, otherwise, the system uses the job rate defined here.

Subtract Benefit/Hour from Calculate Pay rate — Select to subtract the employee’s cost of benefits per hour from the employee’s rate when determining the higher rate.

Filter Cert Pay on Jobs — Select to filter certified payroll on job codes.

Defining Certified Payroll Report Setup Information

Specify information that appears on reports with the Certified Payroll Report Setup panel.

Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Contractor Or Subcontractor — Select whether a contractor or subcontractor is working on this project.

Name of Contractor — Enter the contractor’s name as listed on the certified payroll form.

Address — Enter the contractor’s address to print on the certified payroll report.

Project and Location — Enter the project and location where the project work takes place.

Project or Contract No. — Enter a project or contract number.

Signatory Name — Enter the name that appears on the certified payroll form, which is the person responsible for this project.

Signatory Title — Enter the title that appears on the certified payroll form.

Fringe Benefits Paid to Approved Plans — Select if fringe benefits are paid to approved plans, funds, or programs.

Fringe Benefits Paid In Cash — Select if fringe benefits are paid in cash.

Report Hours Paid Instead Of Hours Worked — Select if you want to include pay codes in the report that are flagged as being included in the certified payroll report but do not represent hours worked, such as sick time.

Submitting Certified Payrolls to California

Enter information in the Certified Payroll California XML Parameters panel if the client must submit certified payrolls in an XML file to the state of California.

Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

CA License Type — Select the license type: Contractor State License Board, Professional License, or Other License.

CA License No — Enter the license number that should be presented in the CA XML file. If you selected Other License as the CA License Type, you should enter License Type : License Number in this field.

CA PWCR — Enter this value, which is the California Public Works Contractor Registration number. It should be a 10-digit number, or enter NA for not applicable.

CA Ins Number — Enter the number to be presented for this project in the CA XML file.

CA Contractor Email — Enter the email address to use in the CA XML for this project.

Awarding Body — Enter the name of the public entity that owns the project.

Contract Agency Id — > Note: Note: (1/21/21) - Because this field relies on other development work that is still in progress, it will not display on the output file until after the February 2021 release (even though it is visible on the form). Enter the ID of the contract agency.

Contract Agency — Enter the name of the contract agency.

CA Awarding Body Id — Enter the ID number of the public entity that owns the project.

CA Awarding Body Proj No — Enter the project number assigned to the project by the public entity.

CA Awarding Body Contract Id — Enter the contract ID given by the public entity to the project.

CA Stmt of NonPerformance — Select only if you need to indicate a state of non-performance. The system will not include employees in the file. Do not select this option if you want to include employees in the file (this is typical, and the value in the CA XML file will be false for this option).

Creating Time Off Absence Codes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Absence_Codes.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Absence_Codes.htm

Creating Time Off Absence Codes

Absence codes are specialized reason codes that describe the reason for employee absences. Use the Time Off Pay form to create codes for all absences, paid and unpaid. Paid absence codes are associated with a Time Off Accrual Schedule.

Note: Note: Define the Time Off Accrual Schedule before creating any Absence Codes. (See Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules.)

Use the Time Off Pay panel to create codes for all absences.

To create an absence code:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Pay. The Time Off Pay form opens.

  3. Complete the following:

Field — Description

Absence Code — Enter a brief, descriptive code that represents the absence type, such as SICK for an employee illness.

Reason — Enter a descriptive reason for the absence, such as Sickness. This reason appears on absence report.

Updates Time Off Register — Select whether to post the hours under taken this absence code to employee Time Off accrual schedules (Time Off accrual schedules are associated with this absence code in the Accrual Schedule panel; see below). Note: If the absence type accrues time, it will update the Time Off accrual schedule in order to keep track of the hours an employee has taken, such as vacation and sick time. Some absence codes, such as jury duty or bereavement, might not update a Time Off accrual schedule.

FMLA Leave — Select for an absence that is a Family Medical Leave Act event.

Use Global PTO — Displays the Global Register Types panel instead of the Accrual Schedule panel (see below).

Pay Code/Pay Description — Enter a value that, when paid to employees, deducts the hours paid from the Time Off Accrual Schedule for this absence code. The Pay Description displays. Note: You can only enter or select restricted pay codes (that is, pay codes assigned to the client in the Control tab on the Client Details form) when you select the Pay Code link.

Accrual Schedule/Description/Time Off Type — If this absence updates a Time Off Accrual Schedule, enter each of the relevant Accrual Schedule. The Description and Time Off Type displays. Note: All accrual schedules specified for an absence code must have the same Time Off Type Code.

Global Register Types/Description — If you selected Use Global PTO above, this panel replaces the Accrual Schedule panel. Assign system-level paid sick leave accrual schedules here.

  1. Click Save.

These settings define whether or not employees can make leave requests in advance, how many hours of leave they may request, and how far in advance they may make the request.

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Pay to view the Portal Configuration panel.

  3. Fill in these fields as required.

Field Description

If you select this option, you must enter the Leave Pay Code as well as a Leave Offset Pay Code for the relevant Pay Methods.
Minimum Hours per request Enter the minimum number of hours that employees can request in advance for this absence type.
Maximum Hours per request Enter the maximum number of hours that employees can request in advance for this absence type.
Can request this many days in advance Enter the maximum number of days into the future for which employees can request this type of absence.
May request with a negative balance Select if employees are allowed to submit leave requests when they have a negative hours balance.
Maximum Negative Hours Enter the maximum amount of hours that an employee’s balance is allowed to be in the negative when a leave request is submitted.
After leave is taken, requests can be made Specify how soon a leave request can be made after an employee has completed a previous leave with one of these options: - Up to this many days in the past - Do not allow requests that occurred prior to the payroll in progress - At Any Time (no restrictions)
  • Up to this many days in the past
  • Do not allow requests that occurred prior to the payroll in progress
  • At Any Time (no restrictions) | Suppress Notifications for Leave Requests | Select to suppress email notifications for leave request approvals. | | Auto-accept Leave Requests on time sheet | Select to have the system automatically accept leave request hours in the timesheet after the manager approves them, so the payroll processor is not required to accept these hours in the timesheet. These pay methods are not just limited to hourly or salary pay; you can also associate them with any pay codes used for mileage, commissions, units, and other such special cases. - A Leave Offset Pay Code to associate with each pay method. When the employee requests leave, the system will deduct hours from this pay code to account for this. For example, if you enter REG in this field and a standard 40-hour employee requests 8 hours under the associated Time Off absence code, the system will reduce the number of REG time sheet hours to 32. > Note: Note the following: - The Variable Salary option in the Pay Method field only displays if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form. - Only the first Detail Time Entry set to Leave Offset Pay Code will be reduced. - If you enable Auto-accept Leave Requests on time sheet, the Leave Offset Pay Code must be the same for all Pay Methods. |
