Defining Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans.htm

Defining Time Off Plans

To define Time Off plans that determine employee time off accruals, use the Time Off Plans form.

Note: Note: The Time Off Register is where the system tracks how much paid time off the employees have accrued and taken. For more information, see Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules.

Each Time Off plan establishes limits on the amount of paid time off that employees can earn and how many hours they can carry over from one plan period to the next. Clients often have more than one Time Off plan. For example:

  • Vacation

  • Sick

  • Personal

  • Other plan types taken according to client policy that can represent time accrued

Note: Note: To copy a Time Off plan from another client, use the Copy from another plan option, and then edit the Time Off plan information as required.

To define a Time Off plan:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. Complete the following:

Field Description
Plan Code Enter a unique code for this plan.
Description Enter a description for this plan.
Accrual Method Select the Accrual Method used to accrue time off. Additional fields become available based on this selection. If you specify Unlimited, many of the fields are disabled and you cannot enter values in them. Note: If the client’s employees cannot carry over unused Time Off hours, enter 0 in the Annual Carryover and Lifetime Carryover fields. If there is no maximum number of hours, enter 9999.99.
Linked Accruals Maximum Balance Define the maximum number of hours the plan is allowed to hold, which the system will use to limit how many hours are transferred from the linked Accrual Register each year based on the Balance Plan’s Annual Carryover (only the hours needed to bring the balance up to (but not exceeding) the stop balance are transferred). (Leaving the Linked Accruals Maximum Balance field blank indicates that any amount can be transferred.) > Note: Note the following: - If you Copy from another plan that contains a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance amount, the system populates that field in the duplicated plan. - If you configure a Balance Time Off Plan with a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit and the employee’s current balance is below that limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer only the hours needed to bring the balance up to (but not exceeding) the stop balance are transferred. - If you configure the Balance Time Off Plan with a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit and the employee’s current balance is already at or above that limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer no hours are transferred to the Balance Time Off Plan. - If you configure a Balance Time Off plan with no Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer the system applies the full transfer amount with no change to current behavior.
  • If you Copy from another plan that contains a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance amount, the system populates that field in the duplicated plan.
  • If you configure a Balance Time Off Plan with a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit and the employee’s current balance is below that limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer only the hours needed to bring the balance up to (but not exceeding) the stop balance are transferred.
  • If you configure the Balance Time Off Plan with a Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit and the employee’s current balance is already at or above that limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer no hours are transferred to the Balance Time Off Plan.
  • If you configure a Balance Time Off plan with no Linked Accruals Maximum Balance limit, when the system processes a Linked Accrual transfer the system applies the full transfer amount with no change to current behavior. | Annual Carryover | Enter the maximum number of unused hours that employees can carry over from one year to the next. | | Lifetime Carryover | Enter the maximum number of unused hours that employees can carry over for the duration of their employment. This includes all plan years, starting from each employee’s start date. Note: A warning message displays if you set the Lifetime Carryover field to zero (0), “Zero designates no limit. If you do not want carryover then enter zero in the Annual Carryover field instead of here.” (Since a value of zero designates no limit in this field, the system does not apply this value when it calculates carryover at the end of a plan year.) | | Allow Negative Carryover | Select if employees’ negative hours can carry over from one year to the next. | | Carryover Expiration | Enter the number of days, months, or pay periods after the beginning of a new year that any unused carryover hours expire. Leave this field blank if hours do not expire. Note: The system calculates the carryover expiration hours by the year-end date to determine where it takes the hours. | | Expiration Period | Select for the Carryover Expiration: Days or Months, or Pay Periods. | | Suppress Pay Stub Print For | Suppress printing on pay stubs. Enter the number of days or months (from the Time Off start date) to suppress printing the Time Off balance of this plan on each payroll check stub and to suppress the display in Employee Portal. From the list, select Days or Months. | | Accrue On Arrear Voucher | Select if hours should accrue on arrearage vouchers. | | Add Hours To Balance on First Accrual | Enter a flat amount to add on the accrual balance when the first accrual triggers for any employee assigned to this Time Off plan. Note: This amount only applies to plans with an Accrual Method of Flat Amount, Flat Amount by Hours Worked, or Months Worked. (Plans that use other Accrual Method options will display Add Hours To Balance On First Accrual as read-only.) > Note: Note the following: - If you enter a Add Hours To Balance On First Accrual amount before selecting an Accrual Method option, after selecting an option the system removes that amount. - If a current Time Off Plan contains a Add Hours To Balance On First Accrual amount, when you access that plan and select an unsupportedAccrual Method option and click Save, the system removes that amount. |
  • If you enter a Add Hours To Balance On First Accrual amount before selecting an Accrual Method option, after selecting an option the system removes that amount.
  • If a current Time Off Plan contains a Add Hours To Balance On First Accrual amount, when you access that plan and select an unsupportedAccrual Method option and click Save, the system removes that amount. | Obsolete Date | Enter the Time Off benefit expiration date. After this date, no paid time off accrues for this plan. | | Usage Waiting Period | Specify a usage waiting period to suppress employees’ accrued time off until after the specified period of time after their original hire date. Using the field and the drop-down, enter the number of hours, days, or months until employees can request time off, if appropriate for this Time Off plan. (This must be a whole number.) Note: Accrued time off will not display as available on pay stubs/advices until that many hours, days, or months have passed since their original hire date. If an employee leaves the company and is then rehired, the system uses the original hire date, not the rehire date. | | Maximum Usage Limit Per Plan Year | Enter the maximum use limit per register year that the employee can use. | | Display Payroll Warning when Maximum Usage Limit Exceeded | Select if the system must stop the calculation/initialization step of the time sheet process when one or more employees exceed the Maximum Usage Limit Per Plan Year. | | Display Used Amount on Pay Stub | Select this setting to display, on the memo line of the employee pay stub, the hours that the employee already used (year-to-date). Use this field to comply with California’s COVID-19 sick leave requirements for 2022. To use this setting, you must have Print On Check enabled for the corresponding Time Off Register Type. For details, see Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules. > Note: Note the following: - Most check formats support a maximum of four memo lines. - The system prints the Pay Stub Name from the Time Off Register Type in the memo line, if it exists. Otherwise, the Plan Description displays here. |
  • Most check formats support a maximum of four memo lines.
  • The system prints the Pay Stub Name from the Time Off Register Type in the memo line, if it exists. Otherwise, the Plan Description displays here.
  1. If you want to perform Time Off adjustments for the selected employee, select one of the following shortcuts:

  • Time Off Types: Opens the Time Off Types form, where you can restrict the number of register types that can be assigned to an employee to avoid employee Time Off register duplication. (You need to create only one of each time off type, such as vacation or illness.)

  • Time Off Accrual Schedules: Opens the Time Off Accrual Schedules form where you can create or update a Time Off register. (Time Off registers track the usable hours of Time Off that employees accrue over the course of the year.)

  • Time Off Pay: Opens the Time Off Pay form where you can create codes for all absences, paid and unpaid. (Paid absence codes are associated with a Time Off Pay Register. Unpaid absence codes are not.)

  • Time Off Auto Enroll Rules: Opens the Time Off Auto Enroll Rules form where you can specify plans that automatically enroll new employees.

Note: Note the following about these shortcuts:

  • Only the forms you can access will display as shortcut links. For example, if you do not have access to the Time Off Types form, the Time Off Types shortcut will not display. If you have no access or are denied access to these forms, the header and shortcuts do not display.

  • If you have Inquiry access to these forms, you can select these links and the appropriate forms will open, but you are unable to make any changes within the forms.

  1. Click Save.

Panels displaying below the Time Off Plan panel vary based on the value selected in the Accrual Method field.

  • Setting Up Flat Amount Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Months Worked Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Hours Worked (by Hours) Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Hours Worked (by Months) Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Hours Worked (by Position) Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Flat Amount by Hours Worked Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

  • Defining Unlimited Time Off Plans

  • Defining First Year Prorated Amounts

Defining First Year Prorated Amounts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_First_Year_Prorated_Amounts.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_First_Year_Prorated_Amounts.htm

Defining First Year Prorated Amounts

You can use the First Year Prorated Amounts panel in the Time Off Plans form to change the number of hours employees will receive in their first year of employment, which helps handle numerous time off changes. For example, if a client allows five hours of time off to accrue per pay period, they might only allow three hours to accrue per pay period for the employee’s first year, if they were hired from July to December.

To define first year prorated amounts:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. In Accrual Method, select Flat Amount. The First Year Prorated Amounts panel displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the Month and enter the Flat Accrual Amount and Max Per Year amount as required for the Flat Amount Time Off plan (see Defining Time Off Plans).

Note: Note the following:

  • The system uses the employee’s hire date to determine which month accrual to use.

  • If the employee is a rehire, the system uses the last hire date to calculate their prorated amount.

  • The prorated information is only used for flat accrual plans.

  • To save the information, you must enter both a Month and a Flat Accrual Amount. Otherwise, an error message displays, “In row 1, Both a Month and Flat Accrual Amount must be selected.”

  • You can only add numeric values for the Flat Accrual Amount. Otherwise, an error message displays, “A valid number is required.”

  • Once an employee reaches their Max Per Year time off hours they no longer accrue any more time off hours for that year.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up Flat Amount Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Flat_Amount.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Flat_Amount.htm

Setting Up Flat Amount Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

To set up a flat amount for Time Off plans, use the Flat Amount panel.

To set flat amounts:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. In Accrual Method, select Flat Amount. The Flat Amount panel displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Complete the following:
Field Description
Seniority Level For each seniority level: - Enter the After Months that employees must work to be eligible for each level of seniority of this time off. You must set the After Months for the first Seniority Level to 0 or else the system will not accrue time correctly. - Enter the Flat Accrual Amount to accrue at each level. - Enter the Max Per Year hours that determine when employee Time Off accrual stops when the total amount of accrued hours during a single plan year reaches the entered value. - Enter the Accrual Stop Balance limit (a fixed amount), which calculates this balance using the following equation: Employee’s Carryover (previous plan year) + Accurals during current plan year - Time Taken current plan year = Accrual Stop Balance Note: The calculated balance can never exceed the stop balance number but might not equal it. If the result of this equation equals the value entered, the employee no longer accrues time off until the result of this equation is below the entered value. Note: If you enter an Accrual Stop Balance that is greater than or equal to the Max Per Year, a warning message displays, “The Stop Balance will have no affect unless it is less than the Max Per Year.” To continue, click OK. In addition, if you set up a Time Off plan with an Accrual Stop Balance lower than the Max Per Year and you enroll an employee with an initial accrual amount greater than the stop balance, then that employee can accrue hours during the year if they use Time Off hours and their balance falls below the stop balance. - Enter the Maximum Carryover, which is the number of hours that carry over each year for that Seniority Level. Note: If you enter an amount in this field for a Seniority Level, it will override the Annual Carryover amount in the Time Off Plan. If you do not enter a Maximum Carryover for a Seniority Field level, the system uses the Annual Carryover amount.
  • Enter the After Months that employees must work to be eligible for each level of seniority of this time off. You must set the After Months for the first Seniority Level to 0 or else the system will not accrue time correctly.
  • Enter the Flat Accrual Amount to accrue at each level.
  • Enter the Max Per Year hours that determine when employee Time Off accrual stops when the total amount of accrued hours during a single plan year reaches the entered value.

  • Enter the Maximum Carryover, which is the number of hours that carry over each year for that Seniority Level. | Apply Current Hours Taken before applying Stop-Balance limits | Select to subtract the hours used before applying a stop-balance limit. If you do not select this option, the system disregards the hours already used. |
  1. Click Save.

Defining Flat Amount by Hours Worked Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Flat_by_Hours.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Flat_by_Hours.htm

Defining Flat Amount by Hours Worked Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

Define Flat Amount by Hours Worked as the Accrual Method for Time Off plans with the Flat Amount per Hours Worked panel.

To define flat amount by hours worked:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. In Accrual Method, select Flat Amount by Hours Worked to view the Flat Amount per Hours Worked panel. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • The Flat Amount per Hours Worked panel only displays when the Accrual Method is set to Flat Amount by Hours Worked. In addition, if you set the Accrual Method to anything other than Flat Amount by Hours Worked, the system clears any values entered in this panel when you click Save.

  • The Accrual Method must be set to Flat Amount by Hours Worked to view the Apply Carryover toward Maximum Accrual Per Year field in the Flat Amount per Hours Worked panel.

  1. Accrue a flat (a) hours for every (b) hours worked:
a. Enter the flat number of hours that employees accrue.
b. Enter the number of hours that employees must work to accrue that flat amount.
— —
  1. Enter the flat number of hours that employees must work to start accruing leave in the Begin accruing hours AFTER ___ hours worked field. (This field is associated with the Nebraska Healthy Families and Workplaces Act, in effect as of October 1, 2025.)

Note: Accruals differ depending on whether you enable If the actual hours worked are not an even multiple of the hours worked then accrue a fraction of the accrual hours.

  1. To indicate whether the system should accrue a fraction of time off hours if the actual hours worked are not an even multiple of the hours worked that you entered, enable If the actual hours worked are not an even multiple of the hours worked then accrue a fraction of the accrual hours.

  2. Complete the following:

Use this option — To do this

Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year — Enter the maximum number of hours accrued per year.

Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches — Enter the number of hours the system stops accruing hours.

Apply Carryover toward Maximum Accrual Per Year — Enable the Apply Carryover toward Maximum Accrual Per Year field to allow employees who work at qualified Colorado and Maine employers to carryover accrued leave. (There are yearly maximums on the accrued leave.) Note: Employees will stop accruing leave when the carryover hours plus the accrued hours reach the Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year value.

Apply Current Hours Taken before applying Stop-Balance limits — Subtract the hours used before applying a stop-balance limit. If you do not select this option, the system ignores the hours used.

Maximum hours for a detail time sheet entry — Enter the maximum number of hours that can be entered in the Hours Paid field on the summary or detail time sheet.

The following conditions apply to the values in the Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year and Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches fields:

If… — The system uses…

Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year is blank — the Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches value

Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches is blank — the Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year value

Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year and Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches have the same value — the Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year value

Maximum Amount Accrued Per Year value is greater than Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches value — the Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches value

  1. Click Save.

Defining Time Off Plans

========================================================================

Defining Hours Worked (by Hours) Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Hours_Worked_Hr.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Hours_Worked_Hr.htm

Defining Hours Worked (by Hours) Accrual Method for Time Off Plans

To define Hours Worked (by Hours) for Time Off plans, use the Pay Codes for Hourly Accrual and Hours Worked panels.

To define hours worked (by hours):

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. In Accrual Method, select Hours Worked (by Hours). The Pay Codes for Hourly Accrual panel displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In the Pay Codes for Hourly Accrual panel, enter each Pay Code on which to base the accrual, such as regular pay. You must specify at least one Pay Code, because if left blank, no paid time off accrues because the system cannot complete its calculations.

  2. Enter the Accrual Threshold for each Pay Period used by the client in the Pay Period Accrual Thresholds panel. This limits the number of hours that employees can accrue for the specified pay period. If there is no limit on time off accruals, you can leave this table blank. [Image: screenshot]

  3. In the Hours Worked panel, complete the following:

[Image: screenshot]

Field — Description

Seniority Level — For each seniority level: 1. Enter the After Hours that employees must work to be eligible for each level of seniority of this time off. You must set the After Hours for the first Seniority Level to 0 or else the system will not accrue time correctly. 2. In the Accrue Per Hours Worked field, enter the number of paid time off hours that employees enrolled in the Time Off plan will accrue for each hour either worked or paid (specified in the Hours Type drop-down). 3. Enter the Max Per Week hours that employees can accrue. 4. Enter the Max Per Year hours that employees can accrue. 5. Enter the Accrual Stop Balance, which is the total number of hours per month/year that employees can accrue. Accruals stop at this amount.

Hours Type — Select either Worked or Paid. If no value is set, the system assumes hours worked.

Apply Current Hours Taken before applying Stop-Balance limits — Select to subtract the hours used before applying a stop-balance limit. If you do not select this option, the system disregards the hours already used.

Enforce Yearly Maximum By Pay Period — Select to enforce the yearly maximum for this type of accrual on a pay period basis (yearly max divided by pay periods per year). If you select this option, the system ignores the yearly maximum, but not at the pay period level.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Unlimited Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Unlimited.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Unlimited.htm

Defining Unlimited Time Off Plans

Define Unlimited as the Calculation Basis for Time Off plans with the Pay Codes for Hourly Accrual, Pay Period Accrual Thresholds, and Flat Amount per Hours Worked panels. Any accrual information entered in those panels is ignored when Calculation Basis is Unlimited. Any fields related to carryover and accruals in the Time Off Plan panel are disabled because plans that offered unlimited time off do not accrue hours.

To define an unlimited Time Off plan:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Plans. The Time Off Plans form opens.

  3. In Accrual Method, select Unlimited. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Fill in fields as required for the unlimited Time Off plan (see Defining Time Off Plans). The system does not calculate hourly accruals for employees enrolled in unlimited Time Off plans, so completing fields in the Pay Codes for Hourly Accrual, Pay Period Accrual Threshold, and Flat Amount per Hours Worked panels is not required.

  2. Click Save.

Related Topics

Defining Time Off Plans

Understanding Unlimited Time Off Plans

========================================================================

Understanding Unlimited Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Unlimited_About.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Benefit_Plans_Unlimited_About.htm

Understanding Unlimited Time Off Plans

The Employee Time Off Register and Employee Time Off Register Inquiry forms contain fields that do not apply. They also do not include the Hours Calculated/Carry-over Details panel because that information does not apply to unlimited Time Off plans.

After the first payroll of the new year, the date fields in the registers might not update because there are no accruals. They update only after the employee uses time off hours in a posted payroll.

Note: Note the following:

  • The Employee Time Off Register Adjustment form does not allow users to make adjustments to unlimited time off plans.

  • During payroll initialization, there are no warnings about time off balances for employees enrolled in an unlimited Time Off plan.

  • Payroll vouchers for employees with unlimited Time Off plans show the hours used but not any calculated (accrued) hours.

  • The Employee Time Off Detail Report shows no information for carryover, accrued, or available time.

Related Topics

Defining Unlimited Time Off Plans

Defining Time Off Plans

========================================================================

Defining Time Off Types

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Classes.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Classes.htm

Defining Time Off Types

Restrict the number of register types that can be assigned to an employee to avoid employee Time Off register duplication with the Time Off Types form. You need to create only one of each time off type (such as vacation or illness).

To set a Time Off type:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Types. The Time Off Types form opens.

  3. Complete the required information in these fields:

Field — Description

Time Off Type Code — Enter a brief Time Off type code or select it by clicking the Time Off Type Code link. For example, SICK for sick leave.

Type Description — Enter a Time Off type description, such as Vacation or Illness.

Linked Accrual — Enable this field to mark the selected Time Off Type Code as a linked accrual (default is disabled).

Transfer To — Select a Time Off Type Code to transfer to another Time Off type. (This field only displays if Linked Accrual is enabled.) For example, if type A is linked/transferred to type B, type A does not display on the Transfer To list for other types to transfer to (effectively preventing anything from transferring to it).

  1. Click Save.

To remove a selected Time Off Type Code, click Delete. The following message displays, “Are you sure you want to delete this record?” Click Yes to continue.

Note: Note the following:

  • The system will transfer the prior year accrued hours from the accrual register to the balance register for employees enrolled in a Linked Accrual Time Off Register when the plan year is triggered.

  • If you set up a transfer to another Time Off type code more than once an error message displays, “Transfer to <xxx> is already in use.” (You cannot have two Time Off types transferred to the same Time Off type because this is a 1:1 relationship.)

  • If you enable Linked Accrual but do not select the Transfer To Time Off type an error message displays when you click Save, “Transfer To Time Off Type is required.”

  • The system tracks changes made to Time Off types in the audit log.

Related Topic

Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules

========================================================================

Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/PTO_Register_Types.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/PTO_Register_Types.htm

Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules

Use the Time Off Accrual Schedules form to create or update a Time Off accrual schedule. Time Off accrual schedules track the usable hours of Time Off that employees accrue over the course of the year. The events trigger Time Off accrual, and the strict definition of “year,” are defined on this form.

Note: Note: The Time Off accrual schedule indicates when employees accrue paid time off. The Time Off Plan indicates how much paid time off employees accrue.

To create a Time Off accrual schedule:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Accrual Schedules. The Time Off Accrual Schedules form opens.

  3. Complete the following:

Field Description
Schedule Code Enter a unique Schedule Code for this accrual. For example, V for vacation, S for sick, or P for personal time. (Required.) Note: For new Time Off Accrual Schedules, the Paid Absence option is enabled by default.
Description Enter an accrual description. (Required.)
Time Off Type Code Enter the Time Off Type Code that restricts the number of accrual schedules that can be assigned to an employee. (Required.) Paid absence benefits are typically provided on a yearly basis. The year-to-date period for tracking benefits is based on the anniversary of each employee’s hire date or some fiscal year basis. If you use anniversaries, the employee year ending dates will vary. If you use a fiscal year end basis, all employee accruals end on the same day.
Plan Year Based On Select the Plan Year Based On that this accrual tracks. (Required.) - Anniversary: - Fiscal:
  • Anniversary:
  • Fiscal: | Last Day of Fiscal Year | If the Plan Year Based On is Fiscal, enter the Last Day of Fiscal Year when each fiscal year ends. Note: The Accrual Frequency and On date fields work together to determine when an accrual occurs and through which date the accrual calculates. | | Accrual Frequency | Select the accrual calculation that triggers during payroll processing. (Required.) - Pay Period Accrual: Accruals calculate for employees with regular vouchers in the payroll. Calculations are from the last accrued through date through the end of the selected On date. - Anniversary Month: Accruals calculate when the On date crosses an employee’s anniversary day, and the last accrued through date is before that day. - Anniversary Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the beginning of their anniversary year for benefits accrued through the end of the current year. This method triggers when the On date crosses over the employee’s anniversary day. - End of Month: This method triggers when the pay period crosses into another month or ends on the last day of a month. Accruals calculate through the end of the month, or the employee’s anniversary day for the month, depending on the On date. - End of Quarter: This method triggers when the pay period crosses into a new quarter. Quarters are based on either the year-end or employee’s anniversary date, depending on the On date. Note: A warning message displays when you select End of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Plan Calculation supported by End Of Quarter Accrual Frequency. The Plan Year Based On must also be Fiscal Year ending on 6/30.” Click OK to continue. - End of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the end of the fiscal year. When the end of the fiscal year corresponds to the end of the calendar year, the accrual triggers when the pay period crosses into the new year or the period end date is the same as the end of the calendar year. Otherwise, the accrual triggers when the period end date crosses the year end date in the current year. - First of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual at the beginning of the fiscal year. The First of Year accrual frequency works exactly the same as the End of Year accrual frequency, except that the accrual level is based on the employee’s seniority level as of January 1st of the next year (instead of December 31st of the current year). - First of Following Month: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the previous month. - First of Current Month: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the current month. - First of Quarter: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a new quarter for employees enrolled in a Time Off Plan that has a calculation basis of Flat Amount and who are enrolled in a Time Off accrual schedule with the First of Quarter accrual frequency and the Pay Date accrual frequency date. For example, if the first pay date in Q4 occurs on 10/7/2024, Time Off accrual occurs during that payroll. (Quarters are typically calendar quarters: Q1 = January - March, Q2 = April - June, Q3 = July - September, and Q4 = October - December.) > Note: Note the following: - A warning message displays when you select First of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Time Off Plan Calculation basis supported for First Of Quarter Accrual Frequency.” Click OK to continue. - You can only select Pay Date as the On date if you select First of Quarter. - Holiday: Use the Scheduled Holidays panel to select one or more holidays when hours accrue. For more information, see Scheduling Holidays. - Prior Year Accrual: This method triggers the balance accrual to move the accrued Time Off hours from the accrual plan to the balance plan. The system initializes the On date when the pay period crosses over into the year’s end. The plan is triggered at year end from the accrual plan to the balance plan, which could be an anniversary date or a fiscal date, and uses the option set in the Year-End Basis field. (Either way, the transfer of hours happens during the pay period in which that year-end date occurs.) - Custom Basic Routine: Select this method when the client’s accrual methodology is unique and requires a custom routine to implement it. For more information, see Using Customized Accrual Subroutines. - Scheduled: Use the Scheduled Triggers panel to define certain intervals (in months) that trigger accruals. For more information, see Defining Intervals That Trigger an Accrual. > Note: Note: This option is available only when you set the Plan Year Based On to Anniversary. |
  • Pay Period Accrual: Accruals calculate for employees with regular vouchers in the payroll. Calculations are from the last accrued through date through the end of the selected On date.
  • Anniversary Month: Accruals calculate when the On date crosses an employee’s anniversary day, and the last accrued through date is before that day.
  • Anniversary Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the beginning of their anniversary year for benefits accrued through the end of the current year. This method triggers when the On date crosses over the employee’s anniversary day.
  • End of Month: This method triggers when the pay period crosses into another month or ends on the last day of a month. Accruals calculate through the end of the month, or the employee’s anniversary day for the month, depending on the On date.
  • End of Quarter: This method triggers when the pay period crosses into a new quarter. Quarters are based on either the year-end or employee’s anniversary date, depending on the On date.
  • End of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the end of the fiscal year. When the end of the fiscal year corresponds to the end of the calendar year, the accrual triggers when the pay period crosses into the new year or the period end date is the same as the end of the calendar year. Otherwise, the accrual triggers when the period end date crosses the year end date in the current year.
  • First of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual at the beginning of the fiscal year. The First of Year accrual frequency works exactly the same as the End of Year accrual frequency, except that the accrual level is based on the employee’s seniority level as of January 1st of the next year (instead of December 31st of the current year).
  • First of Following Month: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the previous month.
  • First of Current Month: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the current month.
  • First of Quarter: This method triggers when the pay period includes the first day of a new quarter for employees enrolled in a Time Off Plan that has a calculation basis of Flat Amount and who are enrolled in a Time Off accrual schedule with the First of Quarter accrual frequency and the Pay Date accrual frequency date. For example, if the first pay date in Q4 occurs on 10/7/2024, Time Off accrual occurs during that payroll. (Quarters are typically calendar quarters: Q1 = January - March, Q2 = April - June, Q3 = July - September, and Q4 = October - December.)
  • A warning message displays when you select First of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Time Off Plan Calculation basis supported for First Of Quarter Accrual Frequency.” Click OK to continue.
  • You can only select Pay Date as the On date if you select First of Quarter.
  • Holiday: Use the Scheduled Holidays panel to select one or more holidays when hours accrue. For more information, see Scheduling Holidays.
  • Prior Year Accrual: This method triggers the balance accrual to move the accrued Time Off hours from the accrual plan to the balance plan. The system initializes the On date when the pay period crosses over into the year’s end. The plan is triggered at year end from the accrual plan to the balance plan, which could be an anniversary date or a fiscal date, and uses the option set in the Year-End Basis field. (Either way, the transfer of hours happens during the pay period in which that year-end date occurs.)
  • Custom Basic Routine: Select this method when the client’s accrual methodology is unique and requires a custom routine to implement it. For more information, see Using Customized Accrual Subroutines.
  • Scheduled: Use the Scheduled Triggers panel to define certain intervals (in months) that trigger accruals. For more information, see Defining Intervals That Trigger an Accrual. | On | Select the date type through which the accrual calculation is triggered. The options vary based on the selected Accrual Frequency. | | Print on Pay Stub | Select if balances for this accrual schedule should print on employees’ payroll check stubs. | | Description for Pay Stub | Enter a name to describe the Time Off accrual schedule. This will appear on each employee’s payroll check stub and certain reports. | | Inactive Register | Select if this accrual schedule is no longer used. The system will not calculate accruals or update existing employee accruals. | | Paid Absence | Select to indicate that this Time Off accrual is for tracking paid absences. (This option is for informational purposes only.) | | Auto-enroll Thru Date is Day Before Start Date | Select to force the accrued through date to be the day before the start date when auto-enrolling employees in plans for this accrual schedule. This can help to ensure that auto-enrolled employees do not lose accrual for that day. | | Payroll Warning if More Hours Used Than Available | Select to display a warning during payroll initialization if an employee uses more hours than currently available. |
  1. Click Save.

Scheduling Holidays

This setting displays if you set the accrual schedule Accrual Frequency to Holiday (see above). Use the Scheduled Holidays panel to ensure that the Time Off accrual accrues holiday-based Time Off at the appropriate time (determined by a “look ahead” period).

To schedule a holiday:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Accrual Schedules.

  3. In Accrual Frequency, select Holiday. The Scheduled Holidays panel displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Complete the following:

Field — Description

Look Ahead Days — Enter the number of days to look ahead for holiday-based accrual. This acts as an offset. For example, if the pay period start and end dates are June 1 and June 15, and the Look Ahead Days is 7, the system checks for holidays between June 8 and June 22.

Holiday — Click Holiday and select one or more holidays from the list, then click Accept. The Holiday Name displays.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Intervals That Trigger an Accrual

To define intervals (in months) that trigger an accrual, use the Scheduled Triggers panel.

To define an interval:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Accrual Schedules.

  3. In Plan Year Based On, select Anniversary. In Accrual Frequency, select Scheduled. The Scheduled Triggers panel displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Complete the following:

Field — Description

Starting Monthly Intervals — Enter the number of months during an employee’s first year of employment when paid time off hours accrue. The last interval in the list should be 12.

Subsequent Interval (In Months) — Define the regular interval (in months) when hours accrue after an employee’s first year.

  1. Click Save.

Using Customized Accrual Subroutines

If the client uses an accrual method that differs from methods previously described, use a customized accrual subroutine.

Note: Note: Contact a systems professional to design a subroutine using the parameters described here.

To design a subroutine:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Accrual Schedules.

  3. In Accrual Frequency, select Custom Basic Routine. The Custom Basic Subroutines panel displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Trigger Subroutine, enter a custom accrual frequency subroutine to determine whether hours should accrue for each employee. The format is:

SUBROUTINE SUBR.NAME(ABSENCE.REGISTER.REC,EMPLOYEE.SSN,ACCRUAL.TRIGGERED)

The variable ACCRUAL.TRIGGERED should return either a 0 (zero) or 1 (one). If the value returned is 1, the system calculates an accrual.

  1. In Calculation Subroutine, enter a customized subroutine to replace the standard daily accrual calculation. The subroutine must have the following parameter list:

SUBROUTINE SUBR.NAME(COMPANY.NO,EMP.SSN,REG.TYPE,ABSENCE.REGISTER.REC, BENEFIT.PLAN.REC,VOUCHER.REC,HOURS.ACCRUED)

The hours accrued should be returned in the variable HOURS.ACCRUED. The calling program does not accept the return of a negative number. If the system returns a negative number, it is set to zero instead. Do not return a number with an embedded decimal point because it will be truncated.

  1. Click Save.

Rate Tables

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Rate_Tables.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Rate_Tables.htm

Rate Tables

Use the Rate Tables form to calculate client-sponsored benefit plan deductions and client match amounts. Once calculated, the system can report the total benefit premium amounts in box 12 (Code DD) of Form W-2.

Note: Note: You can use Rate Tables only in conjunction with Deduction Codes that have a Voluntary Deduction Type and an RT Calculation Method selected. See Maintaining Global Deduction Codes for more information.

To set up a rate table:

  1. Enter the unique Table ID.

  2. Select an Existing Effective Date for this rate table, or Enter New Effective Date.

  3. Enter a descriptive Table Name.

Note: Note: The Basis rate is either by Pay Period or by Month. Entering monthly rates is suggested unless the client company has only one pay cycle for all employees. When calculating during initialization, the rate found on the table is multiplied by 12 and then divided by 12, 24, 26, or 52, depending on the pay period (Monthly, Semi-Monthly, Bi-Weekly, or Weekly).

  1. Select the amount Basis for this rate.

  2. For each tier:

a. Enter the Tier level for the rate, for example, Family or Single.
b. Enter the employee Deduction Amount.
— —
c. Enter the Match Amount.
— —
  1. Click Save.

Regenerating Invoice Accounting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Regenerating-Invoice-Accounting.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Regenerating-Invoice-Accounting.htm

Regenerating Invoice Accounting

This process lets you correct account number errors that were set up in G/L Account Setup files by regenerating revenue accounting on invoices. You make corrections to the file that is subsequently imported into a financial accounting system. You can regenerate all invoices or select a date range for regeneration. You need access to G/L setup and the client must have already posted payroll.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To regenerate invoices:

  1. Click Back Office.
  2. From Operations|Action, click Regenerate Invoice Accounting.
  3. Select a regeneration option:
  • Invoices in this date range - Select a starting and ending date range for the invoices you want to regenerate.
  • All Invoices - This option is not recommended because it regenerates accounting for all invoices in the system and can take a long time to complete.
  • Cash Receipts Only - This option is not recommended because it regenerates every cash receipt in the system. You should only perform this action if instructed to do so by PrismHR Support. > Note: Note: The optional Clear Document File should be approached with caution. If you enable this option, then all entries within the External Invoice Posting screen are removed, not just the ones that fall within the selected date range. If you decide not to clear entries, this can result with duplicate entries for each pay date and employer ID within the selected range.
  1. Click Run.

Using Report Parameters Feature

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Report_Parameters.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Report_Parameters.htm

Using Report Parameters Feature

The Report Parameters feature allows you to specify run-time parameters for some of the reports that print for this client when a user processes the payroll. The reports in the list are specified in the Client Details Payroll tab.

Not all reports allow you to specify default parameters. The reports that do are listed below. The other reports have set parameters that you cannot customize for the client.

  • Setting ACH Direct Deposit Report Parameters

  • Defining Benefits Billing Report Parameters

  • Setting Calculate Checks Report Parameters

  • Setting Client Allocation Report Parameters

  • Setting Deduction Register Report Parameters

  • Setting Employee Check Acknowledgement Parameters

  • Setting Fax Back Report Parameters

  • Setting Gross-To-Net Register Report Parameters

  • Setting Payroll Allocation Report Parameters

  • Setting Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters

  • Payroll Cost Report

  • Setting Payroll Register Report Parameters

  • Setting Payroll No Earnings Report Parameters

  • Setting Workers’ Compensation Report Parameters

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: If a report is set at the global level, users can edit the default parameters from the main Client menu.

Using Report Print Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Report_Print_Options.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Report_Print_Options.htm

Using Report Print Options

Use the Report Print Options form to determine what prints on payroll reports.

To access this form:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select Report Print Options from the Actions menu.

  4. Complete these fields as required:

Field — Description

Suppress Deductions on Faxback — Prevents employee deductions from displaying in theFax Back Report.

Suppress ACH on Pay Register — Stops ACH account details from printing on the Payroll Register Report.

SSN Mask Override — Sets the format for how employee Social Security numbers display. Important! Updates to the SSN Mask Override setting determine how the system displays the SSN on the Personal tab on the Employee Details form and overrides the SSN Mask Option setting in the System Parameters form.

Batch Register Options — Specifies register options for the Payroll Register Report.

PDF Report Password — Sets a password for PDF report files. This overrides the password default set at the global level.

Print Service Report Logo Filepath — Specifies the complete path to the location on the mvQuery print server for report logo images. For example, C:.gif. See Setting Up Invoice Header Information for more information.

Name To Print [on] Invoices — Sets the name that appears on invoices.

  1. Click Accept.

Setting Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Check_Journal_Report.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Check_Journal_Report.htm

Setting Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters

Use the Payroll Check Journal Report Parameters form to determine what information prints on the Check Journal report and in what order.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

To set the Payroll Check Journal Report parameters:

  1. In the Sort Parameters panel, set the Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary Sort By categories.

  2. In the Other Parameters panel, select the appropriate settings to include in or exclude from the report:

Select this field: — To do the following:

Display Pay Codes Using Timesheet Detail Format — Uses the same detail as the time sheet and voucher

Include Expense Reimbursements — Includes expense reimbursement pay codes

Include Non-Cash Pay Codes — Includes non-cash as well as cash pay codes

Include ‘Employee Total Expense’ on Report — Includes the Employee Total Expense, which is the sum of all pay and contributions

Sort on Codes instead of Descriptions — Sorts by code rather than description

Suppress Employee Names on the Report — Excludes employee sort names from the report

Consolidate Report Totals by Pay Code Only — Displays the totals by pay code, rather than detail lines

Exclude Check Detail Information — Excludes detailed check information (check number, check date, and period start and end dates), allowing users to see more employees per page

Display Zero Hours/Units For Fringe Pay Codes — Displays zero hours or units for fringe pay codes

Split Hours and Units Columns — Enters a page break on the primary sort field

Generate Page Break — Enters a page break on the primary sort field

  1. Select the Report Type.

  2. Select the Report Format.

  3. Click Save.

Setting Client Allocation Report Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Client_Allocation_Report.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Client_Allocation_Report.htm

Setting Client Allocation Report Parameters

Use the Client Allocation Report Parameters form to specify the default information that the system should print on the Client Allocation Report for the client during payroll processing.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

To set the Client Allocation Report parameters:

  1. In the Report Parameters panel, determine how you want the report to display by completing the following fields:
Field Description
Allocation Format Select the Allocation Format. The number of columns included in the report based upon what you select displays. Allocation formats are managed in the Client Allocation Formats form. See Setting Client Allocation Formats.
Report Format Select the Report Format.
Report Type Select the Report Type. - Detail Report: Includes the detail information for each allocation level, sub-total, and report totals. - Summary Report: Includes allocation level sub-totals and report total.
  • Detail Report: Includes the detail information for each allocation level, sub-total, and report totals.
  • Summary Report: Includes allocation level sub-totals and report total. | Suppress EE Info in Report | Select Suppress EE Info in Report if you want to suppress employee information in the report. | | Suppress Employee ID | Select Suppress Employee ID to suppress the Employee ID column in the Client Allocation Report. | | Suppress Subtotals | Select Suppress Subtotals if you do not want the report to include allocation level sub-totals. |
  1. Select Other Parameters as appropriate.

Field — Description

Sort by Field Type Description — Select Sort by Field Type Description to sort by the allocation description associated with the Field Type.

Suppress Page Break on Primary Sort (Detail Report) — Select Suppress Page Break on Primary Sort (Detail Report) if you do not want the report to include a page break and sub-total for the Field Type.

Include Page Break on Primary Sort (Summary Report) — Select Include Page Break on Primary Sort (Summary Report) if you want the report to include a page break and sub-total for the Field Type.

Header Code — Select the Header Code. The Header Code displays under the report title. If your organization is not a PEO, the report title displays as Cost Allocation Report. All others display as Client Allocation Report.

Print Detail Line if Zero — Select Print Detail Line if Zero to print detail lines in the report even if the value is zero.

Suppress Miscellaneous Billing — Select Suppress Miscellaneous Billing to exclude miscellaneous billing from the report.

  1. Enter Header Options to determine what displays in the report’s header.
Field Description
Header Code Enter the Header Code. - Payroll Number: Displays in the header of the report. - Invoice Number: Displays in the header of the report. - Both: Displays both the Payroll Number (Batch Number) and Invoice Number in the header of the report.
  • Payroll Number: Displays in the header of the report.
  • Invoice Number: Displays in the header of the report.
  • Both: Displays both the Payroll Number (Batch Number) and Invoice Number in the header of the report. | Include Pay Group in Header | Select Include Pay Group in Header to include the pay group in the report’s header. |
  1. Enter the Output File Options if you want to create an output file of the report.
Field Description
Output File Select Output File to create an output file of the report.
Output File Params Select the File Output Params, which are the parameters to use in the output file.
Output File Totals Select Output File Totals if you want to include totals in the output file.

Note: Note: The following options only affect the output file (Excel) versions of the report. They do not affect the PDF versions of the report. You can save the parameters and retain the defaults, if you decide not to generate an Excel version of the report.

  • From the Field Type Column Display drop-down, select one of the following options:

  • Both Code and Description: This option includes a column in the output file for the Field Type Code and the Field Type Description.

  • Code Only: This option includes a column in the output file for the Field Type Code.

  • Description Only: This option includes a column in the output file for the Field Type Description.

  • Do Not Display: This option suppresses both the Field Type Code and Field Type Description in the output file.

  • From the Field Type Column Display Order drop-down, select one of the following options:

Note: Note: These options only affect the Detail version of the report; the Summary version does not have employee information.

  • Before EE Info: This option displays the Field Type columns in the Output File, before the columns with employee information. This is how the report ran prior to this additional option. This is the default.

  • After EE Info: This option displays the Field Type columns in the Output File, after the columns with employee information.

  1. Enter the Allocation and Sort Parameters in the table, in display order. The fields entered in this table control how the report is allocated and displayed.
Field Description
Field Type Select the Field Type. This field controls the sort; select at least one field type. There is no limit to the number of fields you can enter.
Field Labels You can enter custom Field Labels to go with the Field Type. For example, if allocating the Field Type DEPARTMENT, the label in the report displays DEPARTMENT unless the Field Labels is entered as Dept, then the report displays Dept.
Sort/Detail Select one of the following from the Sort/Detail drop-down: - Sort By: Includes the Field Type as part of the sort. - Detail Only: Displays details about the Field Type, but does not include the Field Type in the sort.
  • Sort By: Includes the Field Type as part of the sort.
  • Detail Only: Displays details about the Field Type, but does not include the Field Type in the sort. | Sub Total | Indicate whether to include a Sub Total for the field. |
  1. Click Save.

The global links at the top right allow you to manage the report parameters for clients that do not have their own defaults:

• Save Global: Saves a global parameters record; do this to define standard parameters for your organization that should apply to any clients that do not have their own parameters set for the report. Client-level parameters always override global-level parameters.

• Delete Global: Deletes the global parameters record; any client that does not have custom parameters uses the default parameters for the report.

Note: Note: To restrict access to these global settings, review the instructions on setting field security in the PrismHR System Administration Guide.

Setting Deduction Register Report Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Deduction_Register_Report.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Deduction_Register_Report.htm

Setting Deduction Register Report Parameters

Use the Deduction Register Report Parameters form to determine what information prints on the Deduction Register Report and in what order. This report lists all payroll deductions and the amount the employer contributes.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

To set the Deduction Register Report parameters:

  1. In the Sort Parameters panel, set the Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary Sort By categories.

  2. In the Report Parameters panel, complete the following fields:

Field Description
Detail Sort Select the Detail Sort to specify the way information displays and sorts on the report: - Employee Name: This is the default option. If used as the Detail Sort, the report groups and sub-totals all deductions by employee. - Deduction Code: Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by deduction code. - Deduction Description: Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by deduction description. - Deduction Code, Benefit Plan (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by benefit plan (as a sub-total within the deduction), then by deduction code. - Deduction Description, Benefit Plan Description (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by benefit plan description (as a sub-total within the deduction), then by deduction description. - Benefit Plan, Deduction Code (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by deduction code (as a sub-total within the benefit plan), then by benefit plan. - Benefit Plan Description, Deduction Description (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by deduction description (as a sub-total within the benefit plan), then by benefit plan description. - Deduction Code and Benefit Plan (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the deduction code and the benefit plan. - Deduction Description and Benefit Plan Description (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the deduction description and the benefit plan description. - Benefit Plan and Deduction Code (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sort and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the benefit plan and the deduction code. - Benefit Plan Description and Deduction Description (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sort and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the benefit plan description and the deduction description.
  • Employee Name: This is the default option. If used as the Detail Sort, the report groups and sub-totals all deductions by employee.
  • Deduction Code: Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by deduction code.
  • Deduction Description: Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by deduction description.
  • Deduction Code, Benefit Plan (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by benefit plan (as a sub-total within the deduction), then by deduction code.
  • Deduction Description, Benefit Plan Description (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by benefit plan description (as a sub-total within the deduction), then by deduction description.
  • Benefit Plan, Deduction Code (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by deduction code (as a sub-total within the benefit plan), then by benefit plan.
  • Benefit Plan Description, Deduction Description (Sub-Totaled Separately): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report first by deduction description (as a sub-total within the benefit plan), then by benefit plan description.
  • Deduction Code and Benefit Plan (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the deduction code and the benefit plan.
  • Deduction Description and Benefit Plan Description (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sorts and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the deduction description and the benefit plan description.
  • Benefit Plan and Deduction Code (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sort and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the benefit plan and the deduction code.
  • Benefit Plan Description and Deduction Description (Sub-Totaled Combined): Sort and sub-totals the details in the report by the combination of the benefit plan description and the deduction description. | Report Type | Select the Report Type: - Detail w/Break Totals: This option is the default. The report includes a break and sub-total for each of the sort options entered as well as a report total. - Detail Only: Displays only a report total; does not list sub-totals. - Summary Only: There is no detailed information, only sub-totals for each of the sort options entered, as well as a report total. |
  • Detail w/Break Totals: This option is the default. The report includes a break and sub-total for each of the sort options entered as well as a report total.
  • Detail Only: Displays only a report total; does not list sub-totals.
  • Summary Only: There is no detailed information, only sub-totals for each of the sort options entered, as well as a report total.
  1. Select the Other Parameters as appropriate.

Field — Description

Sort on Sort Parameters Descriptions — Sort on Sort Parameters Descriptions: Use the sort parameters descriptions instead of the codes for the options entered in Primary Sort, Secondary Sort, and Tertiary Sort.

Page Break on Primary Sort — Page Break on Primary Sort: Start a new page for a new Primary Sort.

Suppress Pay Group in Header — Suppress Pay Group in Header: Exclude the Pay Group, Pay Period Start, and End Dates in the report header.

Suppress Zero Deductions — Suppress Zero Deductions: Exclude zero deductions. The default is to show all deductions, even zero balance ones.

Output File — Output File: Generate a comma-delimited output file.

Include Return and Retain Amounts — Include Return and Retain Amounts to include the Retained Amount and Returned Amount columns on the report.

Setting Fax Back Report Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Fax_Back_Report.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/RptParams_Fax_Back_Report.htm

Setting Fax Back Report Parameters

Use the Fax Back Report Parameters form to determine what information prints on the report and in what order.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

To set parameters for the Fax Back report:

  1. Complete the following fields:
Field Description
Extra lines on breaks Enter the number of Extra lines on breaks to include on the report, if any.
Pay Rate Display Indicate the Pay Rate Display default for the report.
Sort By Specify how the report should sort: - Select the field to Sort By. These are entities used by the client. - From the Sort Field By drop-down, indicate whether the system should use the code or the description to sort the items.
  • Select the field to Sort By. These are entities used by the client.
  • From the Sort Field By drop-down, indicate whether the system should use the code or the description to sort the items. | Fax Back Header Notes | Enter Fax Back Header Notes that should print in the report’s header as needed. You can enter more than one line. You can also enter the substitution variables and to include the Remote Cutoff Date and Remote Cutoff Time from the payroll control record in the report. | | Fax Back Memo | Enter a Fax Back Memo that should print on the report, if any. You can enter more than one line. |
  1. Click Save.

To include employee deductions in the report, you must enter the Custom Feature CodeFAXBACKDEDN in System Parameters; see Maintaining System Parameters.

When this custom feature code is in place, you can suppress employee deductions for individual clients by selecting Suppress Deductions on Faxback in the Report Print Options form, accessed from the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

in Client Details; see Using Report Print Options.

The global links at the top right allow you to manage the report parameters for clients that do not have their own defaults:

• Save Global: Saves a global parameters record; do this to define standard parameters for your organization that should apply to any clients that do not have their own parameters set for the report. Client-level parameters always override global-level parameters.

• Delete Global: Deletes the global parameters record; any client that does not have custom parameters uses the default parameters for the report.

Note: Note: To restrict access to these global settings, review the instructions on setting field security in the PrismHR System Administration Guide.

Setting Payroll Allocation Report Parameters

Use the Payroll Allocation Report Parameters form to set the default options for the report that displays payroll costs that are distributed across multiple cost centers, such as departments, divisions, or locations.

Note: Note: These are the settings for the reports that the system generates during payroll processing as well as the defaults for users running the report. When running the on-demand version of the report, users can select other options at runtime (depending on their user role security settings).

To set the Payroll Allocation Report parameters:

  1. In the Sort Parameters panel, set the Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary Sort By categories.

  2. In the Report Parameters panel, complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Allocate Using — Select whether the report should Allocate Using gross pay, gross earnings, or hours paid.

Report Type — Select the Report Type: detail with break totals, detail only, or summary only format.

Exclude Contributions — Use the Exclude Contributions drop-down to indicate whether to include or exclude the contributions column.

  1. Select the Other Parameters as appropriate.

Field — Description

Sort On Allocation Codes Instead of Descriptions — Select Sort On Allocation Codes Instead of Descriptions to sort the report by the allocation codes; otherwise, it sorts by the description.

Page Break on Primary Sort — Select Page Break on Primary Sort to start a new page for the selected Primary Sort items.

Include Expense Reimbursement Pay Codes — Select Include Expense Reimbursement Pay Codes to include those pay codes in the report.

Include Non-Cash Pay Codes — Select Include Non-Cash Pay Codes to include those pay codes in the report.

Include Pay Rate with Pay Detail Information — Select Include Pay Rate with Pay Detail Information to display the pay rates with the pay details.

Suppress Client Bill Pending Allocations — Select Suppress Client Bill Pending Allocations to suppress any bill pending allocations and exclude them from the report.

Deductions Allocated to Employee Home Assignment — Select Deductions Allocated to Employee Home Assignment to prevent employee deduction allocations from displaying in the report.

Include Admin Fees (ASO Clients only) — Select Include Admin Fees (ASO Clients only) if this is an ASO client and you want to include the administrative fees in the report.

Include Sales Tax — Select Include Sales Tax to include sales tax amounts in the report.

Suppress Contribution Allocations — Select Suppress Contribution Allocations to suppress the allocation of contributions on the report.

Exclude Non-Billed Contributions — Select Exclude Non-Billed Contributions to exclude those amounts from the report.

Include Miscellaneous Charges — Select Include Miscellaneous Charges include those amounts in the report.

Include Administration Fees — Select Include Administration Fees to include those amounts in the report.

  1. Set the Output File Parameters by completing the following fields:
Field Description
Create Output File in column format Select Create Output File in column format to generate an output file. If you select this option, you can use the following options: - Select Group by Employee ID to group items in the output file by the employee IDs. - Select Include Descriptions for Codes to display the descriptions for the codes in the output file. - Select Include Additional Fields to include additional fields in the output file. - Select Include PTO Balances in Output data to include employee paid time off in the output file. - Select Include Pay Code column data only to include only the pay code column data in the output file. - Select Taxes Allocated to Employee Home Assignment to prevent employee tax allocations from displaying in the report.
  • Select Group by Employee ID to group items in the output file by the employee IDs.
  • Select Include Descriptions for Codes to display the descriptions for the codes in the output file.
  • Select Include Additional Fields to include additional fields in the output file.
  • Select Include PTO Balances in Output data to include employee paid time off in the output file.
  • Select Include Pay Code column data only to include only the pay code column data in the output file.
  • Select Taxes Allocated to Employee Home Assignment to prevent employee tax allocations from displaying in the report. | Include Additional Fields (1st Column) | Select Include Additional Fields (1st Column) to display net cash and net pay information in the report. |
  1. Click Save. When your organization processes payrolls for this client, the system generates the Payroll Allocation Report using these settings.
