Setting Up Employee Time Off

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Set_Up_Plans.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Set_Up_Plans.htm

Setting Up Employee Time Off

You can use the Set Up Plans form to create or update the employee time off accrual schedule, plan, pay, and portal configuration.

Note: The Set Up Plans form displays as part of step 2 in the Time Off Setup Workflow process, which is accessible when you select a time off type in the Time Off Type form and click

[Image: screenshot]

(see Setting Up Time Off Plans). (This form cannot be accessed in the search menu and cannot be added to a user role. See Using the Time Off Setup Workflow Process.)

There are four panels in the Set Up Plans form:

[Image: screenshot]

You can do the following in these panels:

  • Setting Accrual Schedule: Use the Accrual Schedule panel to set when employees accrue time off.

  • Setting Up Employee Accrual Time Off Plan: Use the Plans panel to set up the plan that determines how much time off employees will accrue.

  • Setting Pay Information: Use the Pay panel to set the pay information for the time off type, which includes setting the Pay Code and Accrual Schedule.

  • Setting Portal Configuration: Use the Portal Configuration (Optional) panel to indicate whether an employee can submit a time off request in Employee Portal (EP). (All settings in this panel are optional.)

Note: Note the following when working in the Set Up Plans form:

  • You cannot access the Set Up Plans form in the search menu and or add this form to a user role.

  • Clicking [Image: screenshot] in the Time Off Type form incorporates the time off type’s description in the header that displays at the top of the Set Up Plans form (that is, Set Up <Time_Off_Type_Description> Plans). For example, if you select the “PTO Accrual” type, the header displays as “Set Up PTO Accrual Plans.”

  • You can move through the panels without selecting any settings. However, you must complete all the required fields in the panels before moving on to step 3, the Confirmation form. (The [Image: screenshot] button is disabled until you complete this step.)

Navigating in the Set Up Plans Form

Use the buttons in the Set Up Plans form to do the following:

Click — Action

[Image: screenshot] — Opens a panel.

[Image: screenshot] — Collapses an open panel.

[Image: screenshot] — Saves the information in the current open panel, then closes that panel and returns to the panel above.

[Image: screenshot] — Saves the information in the current open panel, then closes that panel and advances to the next panel in the form. For example, if you click [Image: screenshot] in the Accrual Schedule panel, the Plans panel opens. (You can advance to the next panel even if all the fields in the open panel are not complete.)

[Image: screenshot] — Returns to the Time Off Type form.

[Image: screenshot] — Advances to the Confirmation form, which is step 3 in the Time Off Setup Workflow process. Note: You must complete all the required fields in the panels before moving on to step 3, the Confirmation form. (This button is disabled until you complete those fields.)

[Image: screenshot] — Saves your information and returns to the Time Off Setup Workflow form.

Setting Accrual Schedule

Use the Accrual Schedule panel to set when employees accrue time off. (This panel defaults to open.) For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following when working in the Accrual Schedule panel:

  • You cannot change data after the system populates it in this panel.

  • Select Create New Schedule to remove populated data and create a new accrual schedule.

  • If you create a new time off plan (see Setting Up Time Off Plans) and select an existing Schedule Code for the accrual schedule a warning displays, “<Code> is an existing Accrual Schedule and cannot be changed.”

To set the accrual schedule for a time off type:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Setup Workflow. The Time Off Setup Workflow form opens.

  3. Select the time off plan in the Batch ID column where you need to set the accrual schedule. The Time Off Type form displays.

  4. Select the Time Off Type from the options that display (the type description displays in highlighted text with the type name displaying underneath) and click [Image: screenshot] . The Set Up Plans form displays using the title of the time off type you selected. For example, Set Up PTO Accrual Plans.

  5. Complete the following:

Field Description
Schedule Code Enter a unique Schedule Code for this accrual schedule. For example, V for vacation, S for sick, or P for personal time. (Required.) Note: Click Create New Schedule to select a new accrual schedule for this time off type.
Description Enter a description for the accrual schedule. (Required.)
Plan Year Based On Select the plan year that this accrual schedule tracks, such as Fiscal. (Required.)
Last Day of Fiscal Year If the Plan Year Based On is Fiscal, enter the Last Day of Fiscal Year when each fiscal year ends. Note: The Accrual Frequency and on fields work together to determine when an accrual occurs and through which date the accrual calculates.
Accrual Frequency Select the accrual calculation that triggers during payroll processing. (Required.) - Pay Period Accrual: Accruals calculate for employees with regular vouchers in the payroll. Calculations are from the last accrued through date through the end of the selected on option. - Anniversary Month: Accruals calculate when the on option crosses an employee’s anniversary day, and the last accrued through date is before that day. - Anniversary Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the beginning of their anniversary year for benefits accrued through the end of the current year. The system triggers this accrual frequency when the on option crosses over the employee’s anniversary day. - End of Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period crosses into another month or ends on the last day of a month. Accruals calculate through the end of the month, or the employee’s anniversary day for the month, depending on the on option. - End of Quarter: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period crosses into a new quarter. Quarters are based on either the year-end or employee’s anniversary date, depending on the on option. Note: A warning message displays when you select End of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Plan Accrual Method supported by End of Quarter Accrual Frequency. The Year End Basis must also be Fiscal Year ending on 6/30.” Click OK to continue. - End of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the end of the fiscal year. When the end of the fiscal year corresponds to the end of the calendar year, the accrual triggers when the pay period crosses into the new year or the period end date is the same as the end of the calendar year. Otherwise, the accrual triggers when the period end date crosses the year end date in the current year. - First of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual at the beginning of the fiscal year. The First of Year trigger works exactly the same as the End of Year trigger, except that the accrual level is based on the employee’s seniority level as of January 1st of the next year (instead of December 31st of the current year). - First of Following Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the previous month. - First of Current Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the current month. - First of Quarter: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a new quarter for employees enrolled in a Plan that has a calculation basis of Flat Amount and who are enrolled in a time off accrual schedule with the First of Quarter accrual frequency and the Pay Date on option. For example, if the first pay date in Q4 occurs on 10/7/2024, time off accrual occurs during that payroll. (Quarters are typically calendar quarters: Q1 = January - March, Q2 = April - June, Q3 = July - September, and Q4 = October - December.) > Note: Note the following: - A warning message displays when you select First of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Plan Accrual Method supported for FQ Accrual Frequency.” Click OK to continue. - You can only select Pay Date as the on option if you select First of Quarter. - Holiday: Use the Scheduled Holidays panel to select one or more holidays when hours accrue. For more information, see Scheduling Holidays. - Prior Year Accrual: The system triggers this accrual frequency in the balance register to move the accrued time off from the accrual plan to the balance plan. The system initializes the on option when the pay period crosses over into the year’s end. The plan is triggered at year end from the accrual plan to the balance plan, which could be an anniversary date or a fiscal date, and uses the option set in the Plan Year Based On field. (Either way, the transfer of hours happens during the pay period in which that year-end date occurs.) - Custom Basic Routine: Select this accrual frequency when the client’s trigger methodology is unique and requires a custom routine to implement it. For more information, see Using Customized Accrual Subroutines. - Scheduled: Use the Scheduled Triggers panel to define certain intervals (in months) that trigger accruals. For more information, see Defining Intervals That Trigger an Accrual. > Note: Note: This option is available only when you set the Plan Year Based On to Anniversary.
  • Pay Period Accrual: Accruals calculate for employees with regular vouchers in the payroll. Calculations are from the last accrued through date through the end of the selected on option.
  • Anniversary Month: Accruals calculate when the on option crosses an employee’s anniversary day, and the last accrued through date is before that day.
  • Anniversary Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the beginning of their anniversary year for benefits accrued through the end of the current year. The system triggers this accrual frequency when the on option crosses over the employee’s anniversary day.
  • End of Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period crosses into another month or ends on the last day of a month. Accruals calculate through the end of the month, or the employee’s anniversary day for the month, depending on the on option.
  • End of Quarter: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period crosses into a new quarter. Quarters are based on either the year-end or employee’s anniversary date, depending on the on option.
  • End of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual for a paid time off benefit at the end of the fiscal year. When the end of the fiscal year corresponds to the end of the calendar year, the accrual triggers when the pay period crosses into the new year or the period end date is the same as the end of the calendar year. Otherwise, the accrual triggers when the period end date crosses the year end date in the current year.
  • First of Year: Employees receive a lump-sum accrual at the beginning of the fiscal year. The First of Year trigger works exactly the same as the End of Year trigger, except that the accrual level is based on the employee’s seniority level as of January 1st of the next year (instead of December 31st of the current year).
  • First of Following Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the previous month.
  • First of Current Month: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a month. Accruals calculate for the current month.
  • First of Quarter: The system triggers this accrual frequency when the pay period includes the first day of a new quarter for employees enrolled in a Plan that has a calculation basis of Flat Amount and who are enrolled in a time off accrual schedule with the First of Quarter accrual frequency and the Pay Date on option. For example, if the first pay date in Q4 occurs on 10/7/2024, time off accrual occurs during that payroll. (Quarters are typically calendar quarters: Q1 = January - March, Q2 = April - June, Q3 = July - September, and Q4 = October - December.)
  • A warning message displays when you select First of Quarter, “Warning: Flat amount is the only Plan Accrual Method supported for FQ Accrual Frequency.” Click OK to continue.
  • You can only select Pay Date as the on option if you select First of Quarter.
  • Holiday: Use the Scheduled Holidays panel to select one or more holidays when hours accrue. For more information, see Scheduling Holidays.
  • Prior Year Accrual: The system triggers this accrual frequency in the balance register to move the accrued time off from the accrual plan to the balance plan. The system initializes the on option when the pay period crosses over into the year’s end. The plan is triggered at year end from the accrual plan to the balance plan, which could be an anniversary date or a fiscal date, and uses the option set in the Plan Year Based On field. (Either way, the transfer of hours happens during the pay period in which that year-end date occurs.)
  • Custom Basic Routine: Select this accrual frequency when the client’s trigger methodology is unique and requires a custom routine to implement it. For more information, see Using Customized Accrual Subroutines.
  • Scheduled: Use the Scheduled Triggers panel to define certain intervals (in months) that trigger accruals. For more information, see Defining Intervals That Trigger an Accrual. | on | Select the date through which the accrual calculation is triggered. The options vary based on the selected Accrual Frequency. | | Print On Pay Stub | Select if balances for this accrual schedule should print on employees’ payroll check stubs. | | Description for Pay Stub | Enter a name to describe the time off accrual schedule. This will appear on each employee’s payroll check stub and certain reports. | | Auto-enroll Thru Date is Day Before Start Date | Select to force the accrued through date to be the day before the start date when auto-enrolling employees in plans for this accrual schedule. This can help to ensure that auto-enrolled employees do not lose accrual for that day. | | Payroll Warning if More Hours Used Than Available | Select to display a warning during payroll initialization if an employee uses more hours than currently available. | | Obsolete | Select if this accrual schedule is no longer used. The system will not calculate accruals or update existing employee registers. | | Paid Absence | Select to indicate that this Accrual Schedule is for tracking paid absences. (This option is for informational purposes only.) |
  1. Do one of the following:

  1. — Click [Image: screenshot] in the Accrual Schedule panel to open the Plans panel and complete those settings.

  2. — Click [Image: screenshot] to save the accrual schedule settings and return to the Time Off Setup Workflow form.

a. Enter the flat number of hours that employees accrue.
b. Enter the number of hours that employees must work to accrue that flat amount.

(There are yearly maximums on the accrued leave.) Note: Employees will stop accruing time off when the carryover hours plus the accrued hours reach the Maximum Amount Accrual Per Year value.

Stop Accrual if Balance Reaches — Enter the number of hours the system stops accruing hours.

Setting Up Yearly Group-Term Life Tax Rates

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Setting-Yearly-Group-Term-%20Life-Tax-Rates.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Setting-Yearly-Group-Term- Life-Tax-Rates.htm

Setting Up Yearly Group-Term Life Tax Rates

Set annual Group Term Life rates with the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To set rates:

  1. Click Back Office [Image: screenshot] .

  2. From System|Change, select System Parameters.

  3. From the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , select Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage.

  4. Click Tax Year to select the year to which coverage applies. The form fills with age ranges and monthly rate per thousand for these age ranges. If the year does not yet exist, enter the new year into the Tax Year field.

  5. Copy Rates - Once you enter a Tax Year (say 2023) on the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form, the Copy Rates option becomes visible. This allows you the convenience of copying existing rates from one tax year to another instead of manually completing the table. Only users with Full Access to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form will be able to see the Copy Rates option. You can also click the Tax Year hyperlink to select a tax year. If you manually enter a non-existing tax year then the Copy Rates option will not display. Click the Copy Rates hyperlink. A new selector named Copy Tax Rates opens as shown below.

  • On the Copy Tax Rates selector, the From field is already pre-populated with the tax year (2023) entered above. On the To field, select the tax year from the drop-down to which you want to copy the tax rates. You will see the current tax year and subsequent two tax years in the drop-down. Select 2024 if you want to copy the 2023 tax rates to 2024. Once you select a value for To field from the drop-down, the Copy button becomes selectable.

  • Click Copy. A warning message opens stating Warning! Overwrite existing tax rates for 2024?

  • Click Yes - The 2023 tax rates are copied for the 2024 tax year in the system. You will receive a confirmation message stating Tax rates successfully copied. Do you want to view the tax rates for 2024?

  • Click Yes - The 2023 tax rates are copied to 2024. The Copy Tax Rates selector closes and you return to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form. The tax year now reflects 2024 instead of 2023.

  • Click No - The 2023 tax rates are copied to 2024. The Copy Tax Rates selector closes and you return to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form. The tax year now reflects 2023.

  • Click No - The 2023 tax rates are not copied to 2024. You return to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form. The tax year reflects 2023.

  • Click Close - The 2023 tax rates are not copied to 2024. You return to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form. The tax year reflects 2023.

Note: Note:

If you pull up an existing tax year record (say 2023), make changes but do not save the record and click the Copy Rates hyperlink, you will receive a warning message stating There are unsaved changes that will not be copied. Do you wish to continue?

  • Click Yes - The last saved record for the From Tax Year selected will be used when coping rates to the To Tax Year. Unsaved changes on the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form will be ignored.

  • Click No - You return to the Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form with all the unsaved changes.

The Tax Rates for Taxable Life Coverage form now also has the following buttons:

Cancel - Click Cancel to clear the form at any time. All unsaved changes will be lost.

Close - This button is displayed when this form is opened in a modal. Click Close to close the form. All unsaved changes will be lost.

  1. Change From Age/To Age as required, or click + to add a new age range.

  2. Change the Monthly Rate per Thousand of Taxable Coverage if required, or create new rates for any new age ranges you created.

  3. Click Save to save the record.

  4. Click Delete to delete the record.

  5. Click Cancel to clear the form at any time. All unsaved changes will be lost.

  6. Click Close to close this form at any time

Recording External PTO Balances on Check Pay Stubs

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Setting_Up_External_PTO.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Setting_Up_External_PTO.htm

Recording External PTO Balances on Check Pay Stubs

To accommodate service providers whose clients use time and labor systems with their own built-in PTO capabilities that are maintained outside of PrismHR, the system provides a process to record external PTO balances on check pay stubs for regularly scheduled, manual, and special payrolls.

Important! This feature does not apply to checks created using the Manual Check Entry process or the On Demand Check creation process.

Enabling the External PTO Feature

You can enable the External PTO feature using the External PTO used field, which displays on the Other tab in the Client Details form. For example:

[Image: Other tab in the Client Details form screenshot]

The following message displays when you select the External PTO used field, “Selecting this option will ignore any PTO setup in Prism and should only be set when PTO is being tracked externally. Do you want to continue?” Selecting Yes keeps the External PTO used field enabled and ignores any PTO set up in PrismHR. (Select No to disable this field.)

When you enable the External PTO used field, the following PTO set up in PrismHR is ignored during the payroll calculation process, including:

  • PTO accruals

  • PTO requests, including leave requests

  • PTO set up in the following forms: PTO Benefit Plans, PTO Register Types, PTO Classes, PTO Absence Codes, Employee Time Off Register, and Planned Time Off Requests

In addition, any PTO set up in PrismHR does not display on payroll vouchers or on PTO reports.

Note: After you enable the External PTO used field and then save the form, if you try to disable this field the following message displays, “Warning: this setting should only be unchecked if PTO is no longer being tracked externally, and any PTO that is set up in Prism should be used. Are you sure you want to continue?” Selecting Yes disables the External PTO used field and reverts to using PTO set up in PrismHR. (Select No to keep the field enabled.)

Processing Payroll

When processing payroll with the External PTO feature enabled, the system retrieves the following data from an external PTO file that the client provides to display on the check pay stub:

  • Plan description

  • PTO balance

  • Accrued plan year (total plan year total; not per pay period)

  • Used plan year (PTO taken)

  • Carryover PTO

Note: The data that displays on the check pay stub matches the external PTO file by payroll number and employee ID. (If the employee does not have a check pay stub or is not in the payroll, the system ignores the data.)

During the payroll process, the system attaches each external PTO file to a specific payroll in PrismHR, which displays the most current PTO data on the check pay stub and maintains compliance with state requirements. (If the client does not submit an external PTO file for each payroll they process, the check pay stubs will not display any PTO balances.)

Note: Note the following:

  • The system can retrieve the external PTO file multiple times before the payroll posts.

  • The system reads all data sent in the external PTO file. However, it does not validate the data. (The data is the client’s responsibility.)

  • After the payroll finalizes, no additional PTO data can post to that payroll.

  • Each employee is allowed up to 20 different PTO plans listed on their check pay stub.

Reprinting External Employee PTO Information on Check Pay Stubs

When you reprint a check pay stub on the Reprint Payroll Checks form, either with or without selecting the Rebuild field, while using the External PTO feature, the check pay stub displays the external PTO file data that corresponds with the payroll number and employee ID.

During this process, the system retrieves the most recent file data that was submitted prior to the payroll being finalized and posted.

Note: Note the following:

  • If there is no external PTO file that matches the payroll number and employee ID, no data displays on the check stub.

  • If you reprint a check stub for inclusion in the check print file for an employee who did not originally have a check stub produced in the check file, the external PTO data displays on this check stub as well.

Post-Payroll Processing

Once you enable the External PTO used field, do not disable it after processing payroll.

After processing, the system locks the payroll and verifies if the client has finished submitting the current PTO file associated with the payroll’s number (applies to regularly scheduled, manual, and special payrolls). If the file is still uploading the system displays the following error message in the Process Payroll or SuperBatch forms, “External PTO data is currently being uploaded and must be completed before payroll finalization can continue.” (This applies to any user type finalizing payroll, including self-service worksite managers/worksite trusted advisors.)

Setting Company Shift Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Shifts.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Shifts.htm

Setting Company Shift Parameters

Set a company’s work shift parameters with the Shifts form.

To set shift parameters:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Shifts.

  3. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Shift Code — Enter a code for the shift.

Description — Enter a description of the shift.

Differential Method — Select a method to calculate shift differentials. If there is no differential, enter 0.

Differential Amount — Enter a percentage or fixed amount, based on the differential method selected.

Include in Pay Rate — Select to add this shift’s differential pay to the employee’s regular pay rate. Otherwise, it uses a separate pay code.

Regular Pay Code — Enter a code to pay the shift differential associated with regular hours.

Overtime Pay Code — Enter a code to pay the shift differential associated with overtime (premium) hours.

Overtime Pay Factor — Enter a pay factor (usually 1.5) to multiply the overtime shift premium amount when using a separate pay code.

Per Diem Percent — Enter the percent to allocate to non-taxable per diem when paid using the percentage method.

Calc O/T Differential on Straight Rate — Select to calculate the differential on overtime pay using the straight pay rate.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Shift Pay Rates

Set shift pay rates for the client with the Shift Pay Rates panel.

Note: Note: To define pay rates on the shift record, the Custom Features in Company Details need to include the SHIFTPAYRATES feature code. See Enabling Specialized Features.

  1. Complete these fields as required.
Field Description
Pay Code Enter each pay code to populate the pay description.
Pay Rate Enter the Pay Rate for the pay code. Enter a pay rode for the pay codes
  1. Click Save.

Importing Shift Code Data

You can access the Shift Codes import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import shift code data by clicking Import

[Image: screenshot]

.

The Shift Codes import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display when you access this template directly from the Shifts form.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by clicking

[Image: screenshot]

(Back Office), then select Data Import Tool on the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the Shift Codes import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing Shift Codes data:

  • Users whose user role is set to DENY for the Shifts form cannot import data using the Shift Codes import template and subsequently, the Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Shifts form, but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool cannot view the Import icon on the Shifts form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Shifts form can view the Shift Codes import template, but cannot import data.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

Defining State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/State_Tax_Bill_Rates.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/State_Tax_Bill_Rates.htm

Defining State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA)

Define a client’s SUTA billing rates with the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form.

Note: Note the following:

  • SUTA is based on an employee’s home location and is specified by the location code in the Employee Details. Entering hours for a different state does not trigger the SUTA for that state.

  • You cannot have multiple SUTA states in a single payroll. If an employee worked in multiple states for a single payroll and you need to calculate SUTA for each state, you must run a separate payroll to process hours for each state the employee worked in. For each of these payrolls, you must change the employee’s home location in Employee Details before processing.

Setting a Location for State Tax Bill Rates

Set a location to which a state tax bill rate applies with the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) panel.

To set location:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA). [Image: State Tax Bill Rates SUTA form for Massachusetts]

  3. Fill in the required information in these fields.

Field — Description

State Code — Enter a two-character state code, or select it from the drop-down. The Client State ID displays.

Location Code (Optional) — Enter or select the Location Code where the billing rate applies. Leave this field blank if the billing rate applies to all client locations in the state.

Effective Date — Enter a date when the bill rate goes into effect, or select an existing date to edit.

  1. Click Save.

Setting SUTA Bill Rate Information

Set SUTA bill rate information with he SUTA Bill Rates panel. If the client has multiple employers, you can establish rates for each employer.

To set SUTA bill rate information:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) to view the SUTA Bill Rates panel. [Image: SUTA Bill Rates grid]

  3. Fill in the required information in these fields.

Field — Description

SUTA Bill Rate — Enter at least one SUTA Bill Rate for the state that applies to the client.

Employer — If appropriate, select the Employer to which this SUTA bill rate applies. To specify a different billing rate for different employers, enter that information on different rows. Otherwise, do not select an employer.

Overbill Credit Amt (Optional) — Enter the Overbill Credit Amt back rate only if it is different from the SUTA bill rate.

  1. Click Save.

Establishing Officer and State Bill Rates

Set officer and state bill rates with the Other Billing Rates panel.

  2. From Client|Change, select State Tax Rates (SUTA) to view Other Billing Setup.

  3. Fill in the required information in these fields.

Field Description
Officer SUTA Rate Enter the Officer SUTA Rate, if it is different for officers.
SDI Rate Enter the employer’s SDI Rate if the employer pays disability insurance in this state.
Miscellaneous override rates Enter the miscellaneous override rates for employer-paid taxes. If you leave any of these fields blank, the system uses the default rate. - Enter the State Misc1 Rate (Tax ID 26). - Enter the State Misc2 Rate (Tax ID 28). - Enter the State Misc3 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 22-41 (Massachusetts) and 48-41 (Washington). - Enter the State Misc4 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 24-54 (Minnesota).
  • Enter the State Misc1 Rate (Tax ID 26).
  • Enter the State Misc2 Rate (Tax ID 28).
  • Enter the State Misc3 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 22-41 (Massachusetts) and 48-41 (Washington).
  • Enter the State Misc4 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 24-54 (Minnesota). | Location Rate Obsolete Date | If this rate is for a specific location that is closing, and the code is no longer used in calculations, enter the last active pay date in the Location Rate Obsolete Date field. | | Overbill CR Bill Code (Optional) | Enter the Overbill CR Bill Code to use for credit backs for SUTA overlimit credits instead of the default. | | Wage Base Override | Enter the Wage Base Override if you want to use a different amount than that specified in the Vertex guide. For example, Michigan has two tiers based on experience. | | Lock Down Employer | Select Lock Down Employer to “lock down” the employees working in this state to one of the employer IDs entered in the SUTA Bill Rates panel. This lock goes into effect during payroll calculation and new hire/rehire. Use the Lockdown Employer panel. |
  1. Click Save.

Selecting Relevant Employers

Choose the relevant employers from the Lockdown Employer panel if Lock Down Employer is selected.

To select relevant employers:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select State Tax Rates (SUTA) to view Other Billing Setup.

  3. Select Lock Down Employer to view the Lockdown Employer panel. [Image: Lockdown Employer grid]

  4. Select Yes from the Lock New Hire drop-down to lock new or rehired personnel working in this state to the employer.

  5. Click Save.

Importing SUTA Billing Rates Data

You can access the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import SUTA billing rates data by clicking Import

[Image: import icon]

.

The State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display when you access this template directly from the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by clicking

[Image: hammer and wrench icon]

(Back Office), then select Data Import Tool on the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) data:

  • Importing only creates/adds new entries and does not edit or update existing state tax bill rate entries.

  • If you try to import a record with the exact same state code and effective date combination as an existing record the following message displays, “A record already exists for Client <Client ID> for <state code> with an effective date of <effective date>. Updates to existing records should be done on the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form.”

  • You can import at the operations level for single clients or for multiple clients at once.

  • Users whose user role is set to DENY for the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form cannot import data using the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) import template and subsequently, the Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users whose user role is set to FULL or INQUIRY for the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form, but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool cannot view the Import icon on the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form can view the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) import template, but cannot import data.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

Establishing State Tax Rates (SUTA)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/State_Tax_Rates.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/State_Tax_Rates.htm

Establishing State Tax Rates (SUTA)

Set a client’s state SUTA information (instead of using the PEO’s state SUTA rate) with the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form.

To establish SUTA rates:

  1. Select Client.

From Client|Change, select State Tax Rates (SUTA).

[Image: State Tax Rates (SUTA) for Massachusetts]

Fill in the required fields.

Field — Description

State Code — Enter a two-character state code, or select it from the drop-down. The Client State ID displays.

Effective Date — Enter a date when the bill rate goes into effect, or select an existing date to edit.

  1. Click Save.

Note: Note the following:

  • SUTA is based on an employee’s home location and is specified by the location code in the Employee Master/Details. Entering hours for a different state does not trigger the SUTA for that state.

  • You cannot have multiple SUTA states in a single payroll. If an employee worked in multiple states for a single payroll and you need to calculate SUTA for each state, you must run a separate payroll to process hours for each state the employee worked in. For each of these payrolls, you must change the employee’s home location in Master/Details before processing.

Establishing Officer and State Bill Rates

Establish officer and state bill rates with the Rate Breakdown panel.

To set these rates:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select State Tax Rates (SUTA) to view the Rate Breakdown panel.

  3. Fill in the required fields.

Field Description
SUTA Rate Enter the client’s SUTA Rate for this state.
Officer SUTA Rate Enter the Officer SUTA Rate if it differs from the regular SUTA rate.
SDI Rate Enter an employer’s SDI Rate if the employer pays disability insurance in this state.
Miscellaneous override rates Enter the miscellaneous override rates for employer-paid taxes (for example, you can increase the tax rate paid by the employer). Note: If these fields are left blank, the system uses the default rate. Set these fields as follows: - Enter the State Misc1 Rate (Tax ID 26). - Enter the State Misc2 Rate only to set employer tax rates for Vermont Childcare Employee Tax Rate (Tax Code 48-28). If no tax rate is entered for the employer, but a tax rate is entered for the employee using the State Misc 2 Employee Rate field below, you will see the full amount for the employer calculated at 0.44%. The employee tax rate below does not offset the employer tax rate here. So, you must enter the tax rates for both the employer and the employee in this form if using overrides. - Enter the State Misc3 Rate to set employer tax rates for paid family medical leave for tax codes: - 22-41 (Massachusetts) - 48-41 (Washington) - 51-41 (Wyoming) - 08-41 (Delaware) - 20-41 (Maine) - Enter the State Misc4 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 24-54 (Minnesota).
  • Enter the State Misc1 Rate (Tax ID 26).
  • Enter the State Misc2 Rate only to set employer tax rates for Vermont Childcare Employee Tax Rate (Tax Code 48-28). If no tax rate is entered for the employer, but a tax rate is entered for the employee using the State Misc 2 Employee Rate field below, you will see the full amount for the employer calculated at 0.44%. The employee tax rate below does not offset the employer tax rate here. So, you must enter the tax rates for both the employer and the employee in this form if using overrides.
  • Enter the State Misc3 Rate to set employer tax rates for paid family medical leave for tax codes:
  • 22-41 (Massachusetts)
  • 48-41 (Washington)
  • 51-41 (Wyoming)
  • 08-41 (Delaware)
  • 20-41 (Maine)
  • Enter the State Misc4 Rate to set employer tax rates for tax codes 24-54 (Minnesota). | Wage Base Override | Enter the Wage Base Override to use a different amount than what is specified in the Vertex guide. For example, Michigan has two tiers based on experience. | | State Misc 2 Employee Rate | Enter the State Misc 2 Employee Rate only to set employee tax rates for Vermont Childcare Employee Tax Rate (Tax Code 48-27). The employer tax rate above does not offset the employee tax rate here. So, you must enter the tax rates for both the employer and the employee in this form if using overrides. | | State Misc 3 Employee Rate | Enter the State Misc 3 Employee Rate to set employee tax rates for paid family medical leave for tax codes: - 22-40 (Massachusetts) - 48-40 (Washington) - 08-40 (Delaware) - 20-40 (Maine) For example, you can decrease the tax rate paid by employees. |
  • 22-40 (Massachusetts)
  • 48-40 (Washington)
  • 08-40 (Delaware)
  • 20-40 (Maine) | Note: If you adjust State Misc2 Rate or State Misc 2 Employee Rate and State Misc3 Rate or State Misc 3 Employee Rate, you must use both the corresponding fields. Do not assume one field will adjust automatically when a change is made to the other field. | | | PFML Employer Pickup | This field will display only if the field named PFML ER Pickup Fringe Pay Code on the Systems Parametrs form as been defined. This field is optional. Enter the amount the employer is contributing in excess of their mandatory contribution amount here. You can only enter numeric values with decimals. For example, if the State PFML rate is 0.8 with a 50% split between Employee and Employer, and the employer contributes the full 0.8% with the employee contributing 0%, you will enter 0.4 in this field. During payroll calculation, PrismHR uses your configured PFML Employer Pickup percentage for the state as defined on the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form and PFML ER Pickup Fringe Pay Code as defined on the System Parameters form to calculate the employer paid PFML amount and automatically posts it as a taxable non-cash fringe income to the employee’s payroll voucher under Other Payments, provided both values are valid. If either value is missing or invalid, no taxable fringe income is posted. The employer paid employee PFML amount is calculated as: (YTD net taxable) multiplied by (PFML ER Pick up Rate) minus (YTD PFML Fringe Pay) > Note: Note: The calculation does not include the PFML wages on the current voucher. |
  1. Click Save.

Importing SUTA Rates Data

You can access the State Tax Rates (SUTA) import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import SUTA rates data by clicking Import

[Image: screenshot]

.

The State Tax Rates (SUTA) import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display when you access this template directly from the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by clicking

[Image: screenshot]

(Back Office), then select Data Import Tool on the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the State Tax Rates (SUTA) import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing State Tax Rates (SUTA) data:

  • Importing only creates/adds new entries and does not edit or update existing state tax rate entries.

  • If you try to import a record with the same state code and effective date combination as an existing record the following message displays, “A record already exists for Client <Client ID> for <state code> with an effective date of <effective date>. Updates to existing records should be done on the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form.”

  • You can import at the operations level for single clients or for multiple clients at once.

For example:

  • If the SUTA PEO/Client Based field is set to SUTA is PEO Based on the State Rules form the following message displays, “Warning: <state code> is not a client reporting state. There should not be a client reporting entry required for Client <Client ID>. Row <row number>.”

  • If there is no state code set on the Tax tab in the State Tax Setup panel on the Client Details form the following message displays, “Warning: The State Information for <Client ID>/<State Code> is not on Client Details State Tax Setup section. Row <row number>.”

  • If the SUTA PEO/Client Based field is set to SUTA is PEO Based on the State Rules form AND you select the Officer SUTA Client Based field the following message displays, “Warning: Client Reporting is required for Officers only in <state code> for Client <Client ID>. Row <row number>.”

Note: These warnings are informational only and when you select Import, the system imports the data unless there are other failure messages. In addition, the warning messages display in the Confirmation Report after the import finishes with the “Success” import status.

  • Two failure errors occur in conjunction with the Wage Base Override column:

  • If the amount is less than zero (0) the following error displays, “You can’t set an override wage base less than zero - Client <Client ID> Row <row number>.”

  • If the amount is equal to zero (0) the following error displays, “You can’t set an override wage base of zero - Client <Client ID> Row <row number>.”

  • Users whose user role is set to DENY for the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form cannot import data using the State Tax Rates (SUTA) import template and subsequently, the Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users whose user role is set to FULL or INQUIRY for the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form, but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool cannot view the Import icon on the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form can view the State Tax Rates (SUTA) import template, but cannot import data.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

Setting Up Time Off Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Time_Off_Setup_Workflow.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Time_Off_Setup_Workflow.htm

Setting Up Time Off Plans

You can use the Time Off Setup Workflow form to continue setting up in-progress time off plans or create new time off plans. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Important! You cannot update existing plans using the Time Off Setup Workflow process. Instead, you must edit existing plans in the legacy Time Off Work Center. (Click

[Image: screenshot]

in the header area of the Time Off Setup Workflow form to access the legacy Time Off Work Center.)

Note: Note the following:

  • The Time Off Setup Workflow form is only available for service providers. For more information about the Time Off Setup Workflow process, see Using the Time Off Setup Workflow Process.

  • You can access the Time Off Setup Workflow form either by entering the form name in the Search menu or by selecting Time Off Setup Workflow in the Plan Setup column, which you can access on the Work Centers Quick Access panel after selecting Time Off Plan Setup and Enrollment.

Using the Time Off Setup Workflow Form

The Time Off Setup Workflow form contains two panels:

  • Client Time Off Setups in Progress: This panel lists the in-progress time off plans in numerical order, which you can Filter by User. The following information displays in this panel:

Column — Description

Batch ID — Displays the batch number that was assigned to the time off plan. (Clicking the number in the Batch ID column displays the Time Off Type form. You can edit the steps of the workflow and save any changes.)

Name — Displays the name of the time off plan.

Status — Displays the last step the user completed for the time off plan. Note: Click the link in the Status column to proceed directly to the time off plan’s step in the Time Off Setup Workflow process.

Time Off Type — Displays the time off plan type that was selected in the batch.

Last Modified Date — Displays the date, in MM/DD/YYYY format, when the time off plan was last modified.

Last Modified User — Displays the user ID of the last user who modified the time off plan.

Actions — Select Delete ( [Image: screenshot] ) to delete an in-progress time off plan.

  • Past Setups: This panel displays time off plans created in the selected time period, so you can search for existing plans. (The date range defaults to a range of 30 days, where the To date (end date) displays as the current date.)

Setting Up a Time Off Plan

You can create a new time off plan by selecting New Setup in the Client Time Off Setups in Progress panel. The Add Time Off Setup Batch form displays, where you can name the batch.

To set up a time off plan:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Setup Workflow. The Time Off Setup Workflow form opens.

  3. Complete the following as appropriate:

Panel Description
Client Time Off Setups in Progress (required) Select New Setup to create a new time off plan. The Add Time Off Setup Batch form displays where you can name the batch. (After naming the batch, that name displays back in the Time Off Setup Workflow form.) > Note: Note the following when using the Add Time Off Setup Batch form: - The Batch ID is automatically named “NEW” after clicking Save. - Each client starts a Batch ID at 1000, and every new batch they create increments from there. - You must create a batch Name before clicking Save.
  • The Batch ID is automatically named “NEW” after clicking Save.
  • Each client starts a Batch ID at 1000, and every new batch they create increments from there.
  • You must create a batch Name before clicking Save. | Past Setups (optional) | Search for time off plans by entering or selecting the time period when the plan was created. The batches that meet the date criteria display, where you can view the batch ID, batch name, when the batch completed, and the user who processed the batch. |
  1. Select the plan you just created. The Time Off Type form displays.

To proceed in setting up a time off plan, follow the instructions in Setting Time Off Plan Type.

Setting Time Off Plan Type

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Time_Off_Type.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Time_Off_Type.htm

Setting Time Off Plan Type

You can use the Time Off Type form to set the type for the time off plan. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: The Time Off Type form displays as step 1 in the Time Off Setup Workflow process, which is accessible from the Time Off Setup Workflow form (see Setting Up Time Off Plans). (This form and its accompanying Add Time Off Type form cannot be accessed in the search menu and cannot be added to a user role. See Using the Time Off Setup Workflow Process.)

Understanding the Side Stepper

The side stepper displays your position in the Time Off Setup Workflow process. As you move through the process, each completed step displays in green. For example, the step shown in green below, Time Off Type, confirms that the time off type was selected (step 1), so you need to complete step 2, Set Up Plans, the next step in the process, which is highlighted in black:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Steps that display in gray are not complete.

Setting the Time Off Type

You can use the Time Off Type form to set the type for a time off plan by either selecting a standard time off type in the list that displays (the type description displays in highlighted text with the type name displaying underneath) or creating a new type.

To set the type for a time off plan:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Setup Workflow. The Time Off Setup Workflow form opens.

  3. Select the time off plan in the Batch ID column where you need to set the type. The Time Off Type form displays.

  4. Select the Time Off Type from the options that display (the type description displays in highlighted text with the type name displaying underneath) or select Add Type to add a new type. For example, you can create a new “Personal Holiday” time off type in the Add Time Off Type form that displays. (Both fields in the Add Time Off Type form are required.)

Note: Note the following:

  • You can only select one time off type.

  • You cannot duplicate existing time off types. Therefore, if you enter an existing Time Off Type Code the current description displays. If you update the current description, the system processes the updated description after you process the entire workflow.

  1. Do one of the following:

  1. — Click [Image: screenshot] to advance to the Set Up Plans form, which is step 2 in the Time Off Setup Workflow process (see Setting Up Employee Time Off). (The [Image: screenshot] button is disabled if you did not select a time off type.) The system enables the Time Off Type step on the left side by displaying the step number in green, confirming that this step is complete.

  2. — Click [Image: screenshot] to save the time off type and return to the Time Off Setup Workflow form.

Note: You can edit existing time off types by clicking the pencil icon (

[Image: screenshot]

), which opens the Add Time Off Type form where you can update the Type Description.

Updating Group Term Life

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Update-Group-Term-Life.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Update-Group-Term-Life.htm

Updating Group Term Life

Update group term life taxable amounts for the plan year in the employee benefit enrollment records for life insurance with the Update Client Group Term Life form.

Note: Note: A Group Term Life (GTL) plan must be set up at the system and client level before performing an update. Employees must also be enrolled in a GTL plan under Benefits Enrollment so that system enrollment records exist.

To update group term life:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Action, select Update Client Group Term Life.

  3. Select a Client or enter a client ID.

  4. Select a plan year.

  5. Click Update. A report form displays with the date, time, and number of records processed.

Entering User-Defined Fields

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/User-Defined-Fields.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/User-Defined-Fields.htm

Entering User-Defined Fields

You can create global and client user-defined fields using the Global User Defined Fields and Client User Defined Fields forms. (Any user-defined fields configured at the system level are accessible from the Actions menu of the local form where they were created.)

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Configuring User-Defined Fields - Global Level

To access this form and create global user-defined fields:

  1. Click Back Office [Image: screenshot] .

  2. From System|Change, select Global User Defined Fields.The Global User Defined Fields form displays.

  3. Select a form name from the Page Titlewhere you want to set up fields. For example, selecting Employee Dependents (DEPENDENTS) displays the following fields:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click + to create new fields, then complete the following information for each field:
Field Description
Field Name Name of the field to be added. Fields can not contain any spaces.
Field Description Description of this field.
Field Type Type of field to be added. Options include Alphanumeric, Date, and Numeric.
Mandatory
  1. Click Save.

Configuring User-Defined Fields - Client Level

Use the Client User Defined Fields form to set user-defined fields on a client level.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form and set user-defined fields for a client:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Action, select Client User Defined Fields. The Client User Defined Fields form displays.

  3. Select a form name from the Page Title where you want to set up fields. For example, selecting Employee Details (EMPLOYEE.MASTER) displays the following fields:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click + to create new fields, then complete the following information for each field:
Field Description
Field Name Name of the field to be added. Fields can not contain any spaces.
Field Description Description of this field.
Field Type Type of field to be added. Options include Alphanumeric, Date, and Numeric.
Mandatory
  1. Click Save. Any fields created only display on the selected Page Title for the client where they were set up.

Note: Note the following:

  • If a client has both global user-defined fields and client level user-defined fields, all fields will display together on the User Fields screen in the Actions menu for each screen (set with Page Title) they were configured on.

  • If you attempt to delete a user-defined field that contains data, the system warns you that deleting this record will also delete any data associated with this field.

  • You can also clone user-defined fields from an existing client with the Clone Client feature. see Cloning a Client for more information.

  • The option of deleting user-defined fields or amending the order of user-defined fields is no longer available. If a new field is created, it must be the last record in the multi-value grid.

Importing User-Defined Fields

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/User_Defined_Fields_Import.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/User_Defined_Fields_Import.htm

Importing User-Defined Fields

You can use the User Defined Fields Import form to import global and client user-defined fields that are related to specific forms/files. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Client Access Group and Data Entity security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, click the Back Office menu. Under Operations|Action, click User Defined Fields Import.

Note: This form is available for service providers, worksite managers, and worksite trusted advisors who have user-role access to the User Defined Fields Import form. Also, if your user role does not have access to the form/file listed in the import file, the import fails.

To import global and user-defined fields:

  1. Expand the Generate Import Spreadsheet panel. In the Select One Form field, select a form whose fields you want to import. (The form you select determines the column names that display in the spreadsheet.)

Note: You cannot select any forms where you do not have user-role access.

  1. Enter or select the Client ID or the Employer ID. (The field that displays depends on which form you select.)

  2. Select Download Spreadsheet. The system creates a spreadsheet that displays in a separate window, which is suitable for editing.

  3. Update the downloaded spreadsheet.

  4. In the Import Data section, enter an Import Name. (This is a required field; you cannot import a file without this information.)

  5. Select Upload File. Click Add Files and select the file to upload, then click Start Upload. (This file must be in .csv format and can only contain one form.)

Important! You must upload the most-recent version of the import file before performing an import so the field column headers match the field’s current type and required status. (If a field is duplicated, the system uploads the last value.)

Note: The system only imports rows for the first form listed in the file. (Subsequent rows that contain a different form name are ignored.)

  1. Click Close. A separate Downloads window displays the name of the .csv file that the system created. (Click Open file to view the downloaded file.)

  2. Click Import. Processing steps describe the information saved and include a date and time stamp. You can view this as a report or download in PDF or XLS format by clicking Report.

Note: Note the following that occurs during the import process:

  • The system validates the file information to ensure that the user can access the Client ID, Employer ID, Employee ID. If they cannot access this information because of CAG or Data Entity Security restrictions, the system fails the entire row in the import file.

  • The system fails the entire row if the user does not have access to the Department, Division, Shift, or Location.

  • The system adds two columns to the beginning of the import file, STATUS and ERRORS. If at least one field fails, “FAIL” displays in the STATUS column. Otherwise, “SUCCESS” displays. All errors display in the ERRORS column.

  1. Click Close.

  2. Click Transaction Report to display a link to a downloadable report that contains the updated user-defined fields transactions. (This report indicates in the STATUS column if the fields were populated successfully.)

Note: Note the following:

  • You can only select active clients in the Client ID field.

  • If you enter an inactive client or a client that you are not allowed to access the following error message displays, “Invalid Client ID.”

  • The imported file includes the global and client- or employer-specific fields for the selected form and client or employer.

  • To clear the form, click Cancel.

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/User_Fields.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/User_Fields.htm

Entering Information for Custom Fields

If there are custom fields defined by your organization, you can enter information in the User-Defined Fields form.

  1. Enter the Field Values as needed.

If there is a red indicator in the Mandatory column, you cannot save the record without entering that value.

  1. Click Accept when you have entered the values.

  2. Click Save. If you do not click Save, your changes are not saved in the User-Defined Fields form.

Adjusting W/C Billing Modifiers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/WC_Billing_Modifiers.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/WC_Billing_Modifiers.htm

Adjusting W/C Billing Modifiers

You can adjust worker’s compensation rates to bill the client with the W/C Billing Modifiers form. The amount billed is based on a percentage of the gross rate. The system keeps a history of discount and modifier data that you can view by selecting the date.

Importing a Workers’ Compensation Template File

Clicking the Import button

[Image: import icon]

displays the Data Import Tool form, which allows you to import a W/C Billing Modifiers template file that contains the following fields (this file must be in .txt format):

  • Company ID (Required)

  • State Code (Required)

  • Effective Date (Required)

  • Location

  • Use Override Rates? (Required)

  • Discount/Debit

  • Modifier

  • W/C Class Code

  • Billing Override Rate

  • Bill on Wages or Hours

  • Accrual Override Rate

Note: Note: The Import button displays on the W/C Billing Modifiers form for all user roles except the “Deny” and “Inquiry-only” roles. Imports can be performed for multiple clients at the operations level. For more information about the Data Import Tool form, refer to the Payroll Administration Guide.

To adjust rates, do the following:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select W/C Billing Modifiers. [Image: W/C Billing Modifiers form]

  3. Fill in the required information in these fields.

Field Description
State Code Enter a two-character state code, or select it from the drop-down.
Location Code If the rate applies to a specific location for this client, enter the Location Code or select an existing location billing modifier record.
Effective Date Enter a new Effective Date or select an existing effective date.
Use Override Rates Select Use Override Rates to calculate the billing amount using the override rates. Otherwise, the system uses the Discount/Debit and Modifier values.
Pay Levels For each of the client’s Pay Levels established in the billing rules for the client, enter the rates for the selected state: - Enter the employer’s Discount/Debit rate. - Enter the employer’s Modifier rate.
  • Enter the employer’s Discount/Debit rate.
  • Enter the employer’s Modifier rate. | Location Obsolete Date | If this billing modifier is for a location that is closing, enter the Location Obsolete Date. This is the last pay date to use this billing modifier. |
  1. Click Save.

Using Override Rates Option

You can charge the client a different rate per workers’ compensation classification if you select Use Override Rates.

To enter billing rates in the Overrides panel, do the following (make sure you define a billing rate for each of the classifications used by the client):

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select W/C Billing Modifiers and select the Use Override Rates checkbox. [Image: W/C billing modifiers override settings]

  3. Enter the required information in these fields for each W/C classification code used by the client:

Field — Description

W/C Class Code — Enter or select each W/C classification code. If you select the W/C Class Code field, you can search for a class code using the WC Class Code or Description field in the Select WC Class Codes list. Note: After you enter or select the codes and save them, the system alphabetizes the list.

Billing Rate — Enter the Billing Rate. This is the rate per hundred dollars in wages or per hour worked (depending on the state) used to calculate the bill amount to the client for this W/C classification.

Bill on Wages or Hours — Select whether to Bill on Wages or Hours. The standard is wages unless the state specifies that the rate is based on hours.

Accrual Rate — Enter the Accrual Rate. This is the rate per hundred dollars in wages or per hour worked (depending on the state) used to calculate the accrual amount to the client for this W/C classification.

  1. Click Save.

Defining Work Groups

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/Company/Change/Work_Groups.htm Path: PD/Company/Change/Work_Groups.htm

Defining Work Groups

Set up work groups for employees with the Work Groups form. You can group employees, restrict user access, and so forth.

To define work groups:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Work Groups.

  3. Enter a work group name and description.

  4. Click Save.

Obsoleting a Work Group

To make an existing work group obsolete:

  1. Select Obsolete.

  2. Click Save. The obsolete work group displays Yes in the Obsolete Code column on the Work Group with User Security list, which you can access by selecting Work Group.

Note: After a work group is made obsolete, the following occurs:

  • You cannot enter or select the work group in the Work Group field on the Work tab in the Employee Details form.

  • You cannot enter or select the work group in the Work Group field on the New Hire form.

  • You cannot enter or select the work group in the Work Group field on the Rehire form.

  • You cannot enter or select the work group in the Work Group field on the Payroll Check Journal Report form.

  • You cannot enter or select the work group in the Work Group field on the Time Off Auto-Enrollment Rules Maintenance form.

  • You cannot import the work group using the Employee Fields Import form.

Reviewing Employees Assigned to Selected Work Group

You can review all employees assigned to the selected work group for the client by selecting Employees Assigned To on the Actions menu. The Employees Assigned to Work Group form displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Only the employees you have access to display.
